The file tier is already implemented, so the delta is one additional scope level, not a new mechanism. Retiring the carve-outs touches `src/parser/mod.rs:652` + `:692` (statics) and `:282-290`/`:1030` (fields). ⚠ **Enforcement is the bulk of the work, not the default flip:** field and equip-method visibility checks DO NOT EXIST today (`StructField.visibility`'s only consumer in the tree is `src/formatter/mod.rs:613`). ⭐ **Worth doing independently of visibility:** retire the two loader path-hacks standing in for real package resolution — `is_project_root_namespace` (hardcoded `compiler` namespace, `src/loader.rs:89`) and the hardcoded `segments == ["std","iter"]` case (`src/loader.rs:190`). ✅ **Manifest filename settled: it stays `gorget.toml` (D44, SHIPPED; the `manifest.toml` rename was reverted 2026-08-09 — see the D44 amendment).** D43's decoupling made a generic name acceptable but never better, and that does not justify renaming a file already in use.

**⚠ ROUND XXXIX PROCESS LESSONS (fresh, worth the read):**
  1. **A `#[test]` that mutates shared working-tree state is a `#[test]` in shape only.** R39's A1 fix un-`#[ignore]`d `sh_bootstrap_stage2_double_free_after_fmt_sweep` as a live regression gate; the test mutates ~2,754 `.gg` files via `gg fmt --in-place` and did not restore. `#[serial(gg_build)]` only serializes against other gg_build-tagged tests; parallel readers (tensor_*, network) race the mutation window and fail non-deterministically. Post-run save/restore is necessary-not-sufficient. Right pattern: scratch-tree isolation. Filed MED for R40+.
  2. **`gg fmt` regressions on metaprogramming stdlib modules silently corrupt semantics.** `gg fmt` on `lib/xtd/tensor.gg` produces a source `gg build` can't compile (`MetaOpInfix not substituted before GIR lowering`). Likely affects `xtd.dataframe`, `xtd.ecs`, `xtd.compress` too. First fmt bug of this class — the fmt-output round-trip guarantee spans SEMANTICS, not just syntax. Filed MED.
  3. **Convergence STRICT 2× waiver is a genuine choice at close, not a formality.** R39 hit net +27 despite stale-scan + fold discipline; the deficit is 47 closures. Owner granted the waiver on merit (honest inflow from Core #11/12 durable-repro obligation + fmt-verdict fold), NOT precedent per rule. R40 opens back at STRICT 2×.
  4. **Multi-round fmt-verdict fold works when a single reviewer would fragment.** R39's fmt track landed 7+ distinct fixes across snags #2/#3/#15b/c/e + collit escape + block-header + fn-header + top-level blank + golden test + close-time #3-close-tail — each with its own commit, RED-verified fixture, and Core #4 chokepoint where applicable. Batching by verdict-report rather than by ad-hoc opportunity kept the fold coherent.
  5. **Sweep parallel-load flakes can look like ~37 failures.** R39 close's first LLVM sweep at autoscaled threads showed 37 failures (tensor + httpserver + p2p + network + self-host); every single one passed in isolation. Root cause was the sweep-test mutation race (lesson #1). Same shape as R35's `c_emit_comparison` false-red. **When failure count is impossibly large, isolate before root-causing.**

**⚠ ROUND XXXVIII PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **Mid-round rescope on higher-severity discovery is legitimate.** R38 opened as A1 (SH stage-2 memory-safety), executor Stop Condition #4 surfaced Rust-gg build non-determinism as a foundational Core #8 defect that also EXPLAINED why A1 diagnosis was fragile. Owner-ratified rescope; A1 slips to R39 with reliable ground. The gauntlet work invested in A1 (scout + 3-pass brief-review) is R39's opener, not waste.
  2. **Phase-4 N=3 wrapper-run is the proof pattern for "does A subsume B?"** Post-fix, run the wrapper-B test N=3 times. All GREEN → A subsumes B. All RED with identical signature → B is separate (proof-strength with determinism enforced). MIXED → fix incomplete (stop condition). Track A used this pattern to prove A1 is not a downstream of SSA non-det.
  3. **Same-round discovery filing (Task Continuity discipline) — the LEFT-nested `??` SIGSEGV was surfaced by Track C's output-review + independently verified reproduces on pre-R38 base.** Filed durably as `known_gaps/default_op_left_nested_chain_segv.gg` + `#[ignore]`d wrapper. Discipline gap closure took ~5 min at orchestrator level; committed as separate follow-up to Track C for clean bookkeeping.
  4. **Convergence-first process fix (owner 2026-08-06) worked for the 5th round in a row.** Ran BEFORE the ~90-min sweeps; caught net +2 immediately post-integration; stale-scan surfaced 4 closures in ~45min; convergence flipped to −2 BEFORE burning sweep time.

**⚠ ROUND XXXVII PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **Scout `--check` is not `--apply`.** R37's scout used `scripts/fmt_sweep_smoke.sh --check --skip-bootstrap` to preview the sweep, counting diffs but never reformatting-and-recompiling. Both the empty-body Core #10 defect AND the SH stage-2 double-free manifest ONLY under `--apply`. Standing rule: full-corpus fmt scouts MUST use `--apply` in a scratch tree, not `--check`.
  2. **Partial-scope landing with durable repro for the blocker IS a legitimate close.** R37 shipped everything it could + Core #10 chip + all guards + doc write-through, while the deferred Phase 2 sweep has a procedural durable repro (`#[ignore]`d wrapper that fails RED when un-ignored). Not "hidden defer"; fully filed.
  3. **Discovery-time misdiagnosis is why output-review verifies claims, not just diffs.** Executor's "Vector[T] move-and-return garbage" report was WRONG; actual defect was already-filed `print(<composite>)` raw-address entry OWING a durable repro since 2026-07-25. Output-review pass 2 independently rebuilt both shapes to confirm the misdiagnosis.

**⚠ ROUND XXXVI PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **9-pass gauntlet is the accept/reject-surface norm.** CLAUDE.md predicts ~9 for this class; R36 hit exactly. Arm enumeration grew 5 → 13 → 20 across the 9 passes because each Pratt cross-check surfaced sibling-drift misses. This IS convergence for wide-surface class fixes — not gauntlet failure.
  2. **Discovery-time defects during execution are Core #8 same-round obligations.** Executor found 4 more bugs while wiring the fix (Pow chain-flattener, IIFE, at_line_start, Await bp). Fold inline; don't file as follow-ups.
  3. **Formatter fixes have net-positive parity impact.** MATCH +8, ADJ +6 vs pre-round (R36); R37 added another +3/+2. Deterministic emission means some fixtures whose Rust+SH lanes agreed by accident now agree by construction. Fmt fixes are a rare double-win.
  4. **`scripts/fmt_sweep_smoke.sh` is the safety net for future fmt rounds.** BUT use `--apply` for full-corpus scouts, not `--check` (R37 lesson).

**⚠ ROUND XXXV PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **Convergence-first + owner-directed stale-scan combined to prevent a 5th waiver.** Convergence-first (R34 process fix, R35 first use) caught the 0:3 pre-audit state before ~90 min sweep waste. Stale-scan (60 min budget) surfaced 6 real closes from ~569 items across R18/R29/R30/R32 fixes never retired from the ledger. **Stale-scan is a systematic tool worth running at every round-close where convergence needs a boost** — every long-running project accretes un-retired filings that a scan can convert to real closes.
  2. **Sweep flakes CAN look like real regressions.** First C-sweep showed `c_emit_comparison FAILED (Matched 654 < floor 1283)` — looked like a huge regression. Isolated test showed `Matched 1486` (comfortably above floor). Root: parallel-load contention with `c_emit_comparison`'s internal `parallel_map_fixtures` worker pool. Re-run cleared. **When a sweep flake looks impossibly large (2x+ drop), isolate the test before spending time root-causing.**
  3. **Ratified plans can decay between ratification and execution.** Wave-plan C3 (composed D27+D22+D28 fmt sweep) was ratified when D22 was expected to land alongside D27. D22 didn't land (nobody scouted it); R33's D28 landing was CODE-not-fmt-sweep. Scout caught the compose premise was hollow before executor burn. **Ratified plans need Core #5 re-verification just like premises do.**
  4. **Executor rescope-on-red is honest and correct behavior.** When R35 executor hit bootstrap regression from formatter defect amplification, they RESCOPED to chip+docs (aborted the sweep) rather than shipping known-red. Chain of small fold-back iterations rather than one-shot success is a legit path when discovery happens mid-execution — 4 fold-backs total this round, each surfaced a new blocker the prior didn't see.

**⚠ ROUND XXXIV PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **Convergence-first process fix.** Runs BEFORE sweeps to catch fail early.
  2. **Removal rounds are reliable convergence generators.** Deletes machinery + fixtures citing it.
  3. **Ratchet-tighten-backs are systematic closes worth searching at removal rounds.**

**⚠ ROUND XXXIII PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **The Core #8 "shipped known defect" trap will fire — output-reviews must interrogate every FILED follow-up for "does this ship a live known defect?".** D26 F1 executor filed shift-fallible Route B as a MED follow-up "for a later round" while their code check-lane accepted `Result[int, ArithError] r = a <<! b` and their lowerer silently SIGSEGV'd it. Combined output-review caught it. Option B check-reject fix was ~15 LOC and satisfies Core #10 same-round.
  2. **Executor-authored claims about semantics are inherited premises subject to Core #15e Q7.** D28 executor's commit body had a factually-wrong parse-tree claim; output-review caught it; the R1 fold's new commit body explicitly corrected the prior one rather than amending.
  3. **Ratchet raise (parity ceiling) is a DOCUMENTED owner-ask** — R34 followed the same shape (137→142) for the same reason (SH lane-lag).

**⚠ ROUND XXXII PROCESS LESSONS (still valid):**
  1. **Class-fix rounds SURFACE adjacent latent defects** — project the filing rate, not just the closes. The ledger converts hidden debt into counted debt.
  2. **Round-close FULL BATTERY caught 14 collateral failures that per-track targeted gates missed.** Run the full battery EARLY (though CONVERGENCE-FIRST per XXXIII lesson #1 — battery after).
  3. **Owner-decided reverts are LEGITIMATE round-close work, not failure.** Each documented + follow-up filed = Core-invariant discipline.

**Commands, not numbers** (regenerate everything before quoting):
  - convergence: `scripts/convergence.sh <prev_kg> <prev_todo> <filed>` — the ARBITER; quote its verdict verbatim in the DONE entry.
  - parity: `GG_RUNTIME_DIFF=1 GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 cargo test --test integration --release self_host_runtime_diff -- --nocapture`
  - round-close battery: full C sweep then LLVM sweep **sequentially**, plus `--lib`, `--test lints`, `--test security`, `--test spec_conformance`, `-p ggdef` — ⚠ ALL its targets, never `--lib`-only: the seven `spec/ggdef/tests/*.rs` incl. `gen_idempotent` are the only fence-visible instruments (recipe fixed 2026-08-11; Core-13 break-one-fence red-check rides each round-close battery) — and the bootstrap.
  - ⚠ **MIND THE PROBE:** invoke binaries DIRECTLY and read `$?`; a pipeline (`| tail`) reports the pipe's status, not the probe's.
  - ⚠ **ROUND-CLOSE FULL BATTERY BEFORE DONE.md** (Round XXXII lesson #2): even with clean per-track output-reviews, run the full battery before writing DONE.md — targeted gates miss whole-graph collateral.

## Operating invariants (load-bearing — process/reference context, not filed work)

**NORTH STAR = RUNTIME PARITY: self-host-compiled binary produces SAME output as Rust gg.** RE-MEASURE (never trust a dated number — `*_comparison`/`runtime_diff` are diagnostic-always-pass). **Round-close procedure (the three gotchas):**
```
# 1. FORCE-REBUILD the cached self-host driver (THE load-bearing step)
rm tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/driver{,.c}
# 2. Regen — GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 only; leave GG_TEST_TIMEOUT_SECS at default
#    (setting test timeout to 600 changes zero counts but stalls hang-class fixtures ~20×)
GG_RUNTIME_DIFF=1 GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 \
  cargo test --test integration --release self_host_runtime_diff -- --nocapture
```
Read the printed `PARITY = MATCH/(...)` line and the adjudication split (ADJ-MATCH · UNADJ · BOTH-WRONG). Diagnostic-always-pass — only the printed count means anything. Floors `RUNTIME_DIFF_MATCH_FLOOR` + `GGDEF_ADJUDICATED_FLOOR` ratchet **up only**; if MATCH/ADJ rose, reseed the const in `tests/integration.rs` same commit (never lower).

**OPERATING INVARIANTS (load-bearing):**
- **Branch reality check (2026-07-07):** the gorget-1/2/3 worktree paths in git metadata are macOS-host paths that do NOT exist in this Linux container; in `/workspace/gorget` the working branch IS `main` (environment: "Main branch: main") and the 2026-07-06/07 session landed ~40 merges/commits on it with the owner present throughout. The older "never advance main, land on gorget-1" rule applied to the host environment — in THIS container, land on `main` via reviewed worktree merges as this session did. If an environment change reintroduces gorget-1, re-verify before assuming either way.
- **Agent-worktree base:** every delegated agent preamble opens with `git merge --ff-only gorget-1` (worktrees branch from main, lag without it). ALWAYS pass `isolation:"worktree"` PARAMETER + pwd-check preamble + `git add <exact files>` only.
- **Per-track loop (NO pack reviews — owner 2026-07-21):** scout (verify premises + MEASURE end-to-end: compile AND run AND diff stdout, never source-read) → brief → ≥3 fresh SEQUENTIAL brief-reviews **of that brief only** (new clean-context agent each pass; N tracks ⇒ N×≥3 brief-review agents; passes across tracks may parallelize, never pack N briefs into one agent) → **one** executor (worktree) per track → **one** fresh output-review per track → integrate + COMBINED gate. A measured-green tight prototype may collapse to output-review→integrate.
- **Gate battery** (re-run, don't trust): `cargo test --lib` (~1085/0 debug; the 2 former `--release` `should_panic`-over-`debug_assert` reds in `src/lir/validate.rs` are now `#[cfg_attr(not(debug_assertions), ignore)]`d — fixed) + `--test lints` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` (`GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600` — load-bearing canary, double-frees on wrong drop model) + full integration + `self_host_runtime` (snapshot lock-in) + `*_comparison`/`runtime_diff` diagnostics. **⛳ OWNER-REQUIRED (2026-06-20): run the FULL `cargo test --test integration` at every round's close to confirm green — not just the targeted/self-host gates** (`GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS=600 cargo test --test integration -- --test-threads=4 2>&1 | tee /tmp/integration-$RANDOM.log`; debug = the clean baseline). **⛳ OWNER-REQUIRED (2026-07-23 post-mortem): the local battery must MATCH CI's test-job target set — ALSO run `cargo test -p ggdef` + `--test spec_conformance` + `--test security` (three SEPARATE targets `--test integration` never touches; three CI-red causes — ggdef gate-drift, `.filter().map()` byte-trunc MISCOMPILE, LLVM-missing conformance build-fail — hid here red for a week while rounds closed on integration-green). This makes local-green SUFFICIENT (it now covers every target CI runs, so no code defect hides in an unrun sibling) → local-green IS the sign-off; autonomous continuous local rounds do NOT wait for CI. Residual: a pure CI-*config* failure (missing-`llc` job, Linux-only floor skipped locally) is invisible to local runs but is NOT a per-round gate — separate CI-hygiene (periodic glance), never a round-close blocker.** [[run-full-integration-each-round]] CoW changes add ASan + eager/pre-change Step-0 baseline (ASan is BLIND to wrong-output AND view-UAF — stdout fixtures are primary). Integration: partition (heavy self-host serial, rest `--test-threads=4`) OR whole suite `--test-threads=1` (peak RSS ~1.2GB/self-compile, not OOM — the constraint is 10 CPUs). `cargo test` takes ONE positional filter; extra name filters go after `--`.

## CoW / ownership / materialization

### High
- **🆕🐛 [MED — LLVM-LANE ONLY, C builds clean; found 2026-08-17 by the Track-F brief-review pass 3, orchestrator-reproduced] The LLVM backend never emits the SDL runtime blob, so any `xtd.sdl` call fails at LINK.** Six-line repro (no struct anywhere): `from xtd.sdl import sdl_init, sdl_quit, SDL_INIT_VIDEO` + `sdl_init(SDL_INIT_VIDEO); sdl_quit()` → `gg build` rc 0, `gg build --backend=llvm` rc 1 with ``undefined reference to `gorget_sdl_init'`` / ``gorget_sdl_quit'``. ⚠ **DISTINCT from gorget-arena snag #1 — do NOT merge them.** Snag #1 is a missing C TYPEDEF for a struct in an FFI-only module and fails at shim COMPILE (`unknown type name 'GorgetSDLWindow'`); this repro mentions **no struct at all**, so the shim compiles and the failure is at LINK — the runtime blob was never emitted. Snag #1's stated fix (extend the shim's type-collection walk) emits no function bodies and therefore cannot close this. Conditional `SDL_RUNTIME` emission lives in the *C* codegen and has no LLVM counterpart. **In the wild:** this is why `examples/sdl_hello.gg` fails on the LLVM lane (15 undefined `gorget_sdl_*`) while building clean on C; its two sibling LLVM-failing examples `gfx_demo`/`breakout` fail the OTHER way (shim compile) and those two ARE snag #1. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/llvm_sdl_runtime_blob_not_emitted.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_llvm_sdl_runtime_blob` asserting the LLVM build SUCCEEDS, RED-verified 2026-08-17.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — A SHIPPED STDLIB FUNCTION THAT CAN NEVER BUILD; found 2026-08-17 by the Track-F brief-review pass 2, orchestrator-reproduced] `std.io.stdin_eof()` binds the runtime symbol `gorget_stdin_eof`, which EXISTS NOWHERE IN THE TREE.** `grep -rn 'gorget_stdin_eof'` over the whole repo returns exactly ONE hit — the declaration itself, `lib/std/io.gg:92` (`extern bool stdin_eof() = "gorget_stdin_eof"`). Every block-mate around it (`gorget_term_cols`, `gorget_term_rows`, `gorget_input`, `gorget_readline`, `gorget_getchar`) is implemented in `src/backend/c/runtime/runtime_io.c`, so this is ONE missing runtime function, not a missing subsystem. Probe: `bool e = stdin_eof()` → `gg check` "OK: no semantic errors" (rc 0), `gg build` rc 1 with `error: implicit declaration of function 'gorget_stdin_eof'`. **TWO defects per Core #8:** (1) the runtime symbol is missing — stdlib surface that cannot be used; (2) a C-compile failure of GENERATED code reached the user as a raw `.c:` error, which `docs/language-design.md:120` forbids (that half is the already-filed check-clean-then-C-error class; this is a live instance). ⚠ **Consequence in the wild:** this is what actually blocks `examples/agentic_loop.gg` from building once its typed-error rot is repaired — so agentic_loop is a stdlib hole with an example sitting on top of it, NOT example rot, and the examples-gate track cannot repair it. ⚠ **Fix by IMPLEMENTING `gorget_stdin_eof` in `runtime_io.c`. Do NOT delete the declaration** — that removes documented stdlib surface to make a red go away. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/stdlib_stdin_eof_no_runtime_symbol.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_stdlib_stdin_eof_builds` asserting the build SUCCEEDS, RED-verified 2026-08-17.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — TWO FACES, ONE ROOT, both `gg check`-clean; found 2026-08-17 by the Track-E brief-review pass 2, orchestrator-reproduced] `Dict[K, Option[<heap>]].values()` MIS-TYPES THE PAYLOAD as `int64_t`.** Iterating a Dict's values where the value type is `Option[<heap type>]` loses the payload's type — the slot carries the Option's scalar representation instead. What that produces depends only on the readback: **FACE 1, `unwrap()` → SILENT WRONG ANSWER** (`gg check` OK, `gg build` 0, runs and prints **`0`** where the payload is the String `"aa"`, no diagnostic anywhere); **FACE 2, `match v: case Some(s)` → BROKEN C REACHES THE USER** (`gg check` OK, `gg build` exit 1 with `error: incompatible types when assigning to type 'Str' from type 'int64_t'` — a C-compile failure of generated code is by definition an ICE and must never surface as a raw `.c:` error, per `docs/language-design.md:120`). The `int64_t` in face 2's message is what identifies the shared root, so fix it once at the `values()` payload typing, not twice at the readbacks. By Core #8 face 1 is the worse of the two: a loud failure beats a silent wrong answer. ⚠ **DISTINCT from the `unwrap()`-on-a-borrowed-payload DOUBLE FREE filed above** — there the discriminator is `unwrap()` (a `match` readback is rc 0) and the program CRASHES; here BOTH readbacks are broken and neither crashes, so this is a typing defect in `values()`, not the payload-ownership defect. Payload must be heap-forced (a literal is a false negative). Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/dict_values_option_payload_mistyped.gg` carrying BOTH faces + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_dict_values_option_payload` asserting the INTENDED `aa\naa`, RED-verified 2026-08-17. Own scout → brief → gauntlet.
- **🆕🚨💥 [CRITICAL — MEMORY SAFETY, BOTH BACKENDS, from a plain READ of safe syntax; found 2026-08-17 by the Track-E brief-review, orchestrator-reproduced; ⚠ AXIS CORRECTED by pass 2 — the first filing was measurably too narrow] `unwrap()` on an `Option` payload BORROWED out of a collection frees what the collection still owns — DOUBLE FREE.** Repro: `Vector[Option[String]] d = [Some(mk("a","a"))]` / `Option[String] o = d.get(0).unwrap()` / `print(o.unwrap())` → `gg check` prints **"OK: no semantic errors"** (rc 0, no diagnostic), `gg build` exits 0 on C **and** `--backend=llvm`, both run SIGABRT (rc 134). ⚠ **NO ASSIGNMENT AND NO LOOP ARE INVOLVED — this is a READ**, so it is a **DISTINCT MECHANISM** from the `for x in coll: x = v` element-rebind double free filed below, not the same bug in another costume; the for-loop spelling crashes identically, which shows the loop is incidental rather than causal. **This REFUTES the "one missing typed fact at one write site / two halves of ONE mis-lowered assignment" framing** that the for-in scout arrived at — that framing may still describe the String-element rebind cells, but it is not the whole family. **⚠ THE DISCRIMINATOR IS `unwrap()`, NOT the container and NOT the element type** (the first filing said "a `Vector[Option[heap]]` element" and was wrong): a **`match o: case Some(s)` readback of the very same value is rc 0**, binding without unwrapping is rc 0, and a **STRUCT element whose Option is a FIELD** (`struct Holder: Option[String] f` in a `Vector[Holder]`, read-only `h.f.unwrap()`) crashes identically — so it localises to unwrap's payload handling, not to the collection read or the Option type. False negatives that must never be used as fixtures: scalar payloads (`Option[int]`), container-free Options, and **LITERAL payloads (`[Some("lit")]`) which run CLEAN** because a static literal is never freed — heap-force everything. INTENDED: the read prints its payload and exits 0 — there is nothing to reject, every construct is safe documented Gorget, so "reject it" is not an available answer. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/security/attack_100_unwrap_borrowed_payload_double_free.gg` (TWO costumes: Option-typed element AND struct-element-with-Option-field) + `#[ignore]`d `security_unwrap_borrowed_payload_double_free` (tests/security.rs) asserting the INTENDED output, RED-verified 2026-08-17. Own scout → brief → gauntlet; ASan on both backends is the instrument (ggdef is structurally blind to memory invalidation, Core #13).
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — Core #10 SILENT DATA LOSS BY THE FORMATTER; found 2026-08-17 by the Phase-4c brief-review pass 2, orchestrator-reproduced] `gg fmt` DELETES an attribute line the author wrote, exit 0, no diagnostic.** Measured at HEAD on a five-costume file: **5 attribute lines in, 1 out.** Only the one above a FUNCTION survives; the lines above `public static int S = 7`, `type Alias = int`, `newtype Meters(int)` and `const int C = 3` are gone from the output. With `gg fmt --in-place` that is source lost from disk. Mechanism: those parser arms DISCARD attributes, so the attribute never reaches the AST, and the formatter prints from the AST — the deletion is not a formatter bug per se but the visible face of the dropping-position class. `assert_fmt_preserves_check_verdict` CANNOT see it (the file is accepted before and after, since the dropped attribute was never validated) — Core #15e Q2, a guard green over the class it would need to catch. INTENDED: an unknown attribute is REJECTED so `gg fmt` refuses to format (as it already does for a parse error — "N parse error(s) found; refusing to format", exit 1); a known attribute in these positions is HONOURED and round-trips, since attributes attach to DECLARATIONS and every construct above introduces a name (ratified 2026-08-17, `docs/define-gorget/decisions.md`). Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_silently_deletes_attribute_line.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `fmt_does_not_delete_attribute_lines`, RED-verified 2026-08-17 (`left: 1, right: 5`). ⚡ Closed by the `@fmt(skip)` position class-fix (Phase 4c) — graduate the fixture out of `known_gaps/` that round; this entry exists so the defect survives a 4c rescope.
- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — Core #8 SOUNDNESS: an UNDEFINED TYPE NAME is silently accepted in 9 of 10 type positions; found 2026-08-17 by the example-rot scout, orchestrator-verified] The resolver CAN reject undefined types — it just is not called at 9 of the 10 write sites.** Measured at HEAD: `Zorblatt s = "hello"` (a LOCAL binding) is correctly REJECTED, while `struct Holder: Zorblatt field` and `int takes(Zorblatt x)` both report **"OK: no semantic errors"**. The accepting positions are function params, return types, struct fields, tuple elements, every generic argument, and `Callable` types. Not `str`-specific — an arbitrary `Zorblatt` is accepted just as readily. Consequence: garbage types reach the backend, where the program either emits broken C (`gg run` on an undefined struct-field type → `C compiler exited with: exit status: 1`, a raw C error leaking to the user, which `docs/language-design.md:120` already forbids) or, per the scout, BUILDS AND RUNS SUCCESSFULLY in some cells (a `Zorblatt` struct field printing `7`; `Vector[str].push("a")` printing `1`). **The single correctly-rejecting cell proves the capability exists**, so this is a missing call at 9 sites — a Core #4 chokepoint fix, not 9 independent repairs. ⚠ This is the ROOT CAUSE of `examples/md2html.gg`'s five-month invisible breakage (it uses the removed `str` keyword and nothing complained). Accept/reject change ⇒ Core #9: all three lanes + cross-lane NEG conformance fixtures. Needs its own scout → brief → gauntlet. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/undefined_type_name_accepted.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_undefined_type_name_rejected`, RED-verified 2026-08-17.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — NAME-MATCHING CLASS, CLAUDE.md "No name matching"; scoped 2026-08-17] The C runtime-chunk selector routes 759 `lib/` extern symbols through dozens of ad-hoc substring predicates, and there are TWO of them that disagree.** `src/backend/c_lir/emit_types.rs` uses `starts_with(...)` chains; the drifted sibling at `src/main.rs:1499` uses `.contains(...)` over the same symbols. Two parallel lists kept in sync by hand is the exact symptom the rule names, and it has already produced one live miscompile (the `gorget_exec_` trailing-underscore gate, since fixed — `exec` alone emitted broken C for ~6 months because a predicate missed by one character). Reference-grade shape: a typed provider field on the `extern` declaration naming its runtime chunk, set at the source, read via an accessor — one source of truth, no substring tests, and a new extern cannot silently route nowhere. ⚠ Do NOT convert one chunk at a time: a partial conversion leaves two routing mechanisms disagreeing, which is worse than the status quo. Own scout → brief → gauntlet; the arm-count/exhaustiveness guard is part of the fix (what stops extern N+1?).
- **🆕🧹 [MED — SHIPPED EXAMPLES ROT SILENTLY, no gate; found 2026-08-17] Example entry points break and nothing catches them — the GATE is the work.** Census when taken: 22/29 green · 4 rot · 1 build-broken · 2 intentional-fail. Every rot case is a MIGRATION THAT SKIPPED `examples/`: snake + input_test (~6 months, `exec` moved `std.os`→`std.process` in `4dd5f9399`, which updated the reference and two fixtures but not the examples — those two are repaired, and their repair is exactly the evidence that nothing gates this), toml_config + agentic_loop (~4 months, the typed-error migration), md2html (~5 months, the `str` keyword removal — invisible because of the undefined-type-name hole filed above). Re-run the census before scoping; it is a dated figure. `run_example` ALREADY EXISTS (locate by symbol, `grep -n 'fn run_example' tests/integration.rs` — full build+run+diff) but is OPT-IN and wired for only 15 of 29; CI references `examples/` nowhere. **Owner narrowed the scope 2026-08-17: gate that examples BUILD; RUNNING them is deferred** — so the shape is ONE rule, `every discovered entry point must `gg build` cleanly except an explicit `EXPECT_BUILD_FAIL` allowlist pinned to its error codes`, with discovery from **`git ls-files examples/`** (NOT from disk — a bare filesystem walk trips over build artifacts, since `run_example` builds in place and only cleans up after its asserts). ⚠ Name it `EXPECT_BUILD_FAIL`, never `EXPECT_CHECK_FAIL`: it must gate on `gg build`, NOT `gg check`, or it misses md2html (which passes check and dies at C compile), and a tier named for `check` re-plants the exact confusion the gate exists to retire. ⚠ **Four hazards measured by the brief gauntlet, each of which would have shipped a gate that looks green while covering nothing:** (a) discovery keyed on `examples/*.gg` + `examples/*/main.gg` SILENTLY SKIPS a `main.gg` at any other depth — discovery must be TOTAL-OR-LOUD, failing by name on an unrecognised shape; (b) `gg build` has NO output flag and writes `examples/<name>` / `<name>.c`, the exact paths `run_example` execs, so an in-place gate races the wired tests under the autoscaling thread count (measured 333 exec failures in 2130 runs, `rc=126`) — builds must be hermetic (temp dir), and `--test-threads=1` would only HIDE it; (c) **the gate is TWO-LANE** — CI runs the integration suite under `GG_BACKEND=llvm`, and 3 examples build on C but fail under LLVM, so the expectation table is keyed by **(example, lane)**; `skip_under_llvm()` is NOT the mechanism for this (all four of its call sites are the "test reads emitted `.c`" case); (d) **discovery that SHRINKS or EMPTIES is green** — `git ls-files` outside a repo exits 128 with empty stdout, so an unchecked `.output()` yields zero entry points and a passing gate; copy `tests/lints.rs`'s `tracked_files()` (expect-on-spawn + `status.success()` + a count FLOOR). **Known limitation to state in the gate's own doc:** an UNIMPORTED library module beside a package `main.gg` rots invisibly — nothing compiles it, and no build gate can close that. The deferred RUN half remains real work. **Worth filing separately:** `gg build` can only write next to its source, which is why (b) exists at all.
- **🆕📐 [HIGH — CAMPAIGN, owner-ratified 2026-08-17 (ledger); "fix first, promote second"] Make `for x in xs` the ACTUAL default idiom, not just the documented one — sequenced tracks, several BLOCKED.** Measured by the idiom scout: `for x in xs` is not merely free but FASTER than `.get(i).unwrap()` — Vector[int] 1.75× (C) / 9.5× (LLVM), Dict 2.7× / 4.0×, **String 33× (O(n) vs O(n²), the repeated UTF-8 re-walk)**, Vector[String]/Set/range identical-or-better; it ties `xs[i]` exactly, clone counts 0, peak RSS identical. **The one loser is `zip` — 9.9× SLOWER (C) / 5.4× (LLVM)** because `Vector.zip` is EAGER (`typecheck.rs:8226` materializes a `Vector[(T,U)]`) and the lazy form does not link. Census (regenerated, supersedes the orchestrator's first pass which was wrong on all four counts): **616** `.get().unwrap()` · **261** `.len()` while-loops (624 total loops) · **ZERO** files using for-in outside `Iterator`'s own trait defaults · **86.2% of the primary population is pretend-index** (225/261: 140 for-in, 44 zip, 30 enumerate) · only **287 of 616** unwraps die with the loops (165 are presence assertions, so a "616-site" yield claim overstates by 2.1×) · `dataframe.gg` is 96/225 loops and 182/287 unwraps and holds **36 of the 44 zip rows in its hot loops** — rewriting those today is a 5-10× regression. New type hole nobody had found: **24 string-BYTE cursors** (`for c in s` yields codepoints; `s.bytes()` does not exist). ⛔ **BLOCKED BY** the CRITICAL `for x in &coll` double-free (filed same day) — the idiom cannot be taught while its mutable form corrupts the heap — and by the type holes, or the lint fires where no legal spelling exists. **Ratified shape:** repo lint (`tests/lints.rs` ratchet, env-gate → burn down → fatal, the census as its initial allowlist) + the book; NO warn-by-default compiler warning yet (ledger 2026-08-17). **NAMED NON-TARGETS, in the lint's own doc, not left to reviewer judgement:** real cursors — a byte-walking parser that backs up, the self-host token cursor — are not failed for-ins; forcing them would be a lie AND slower. Track order: A0 double-free → A (the 5 link/ICE/type defects + the receiver-axis guard fix) → B (the `Dict.iter()` leak) → C (Core #10 class-fix) → C2 (type holes) → D (lint) → E (rewrite, 4 waves, zip wave BLOCKED on lazy zip) → F (compiler warning, deferred). ⚠ Track E must be filed ONE BULLET PER WAVE or landed waves are invisible to the convergence metric.
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — LEAK/OOM; found 2026-08-17 by the idiom scout] `Dict.iter()` clones the map ~4× per element and leaks it** — 983 MB on a **1000-entry** dict, OOM-killed at 15.4 GB at benchmark scale. ⚠ The shipped fixture that supposedly guards this asserts "walking the bucket array **in place**" in its own comment and is STDOUT-BLIND, so it has never been able to see the clone (Core #15e Q2 — a guard green over the class it was built to retire). Fix direction: the iterator protocol must borrow the bucket array, and `security_safe_no_leak` should be pointed at the iterator protocol.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Core #10 SILENT DROP; found 2026-08-17 by the idiom scout] A for-loop over a NON-ITERABLE type is silently discarded — no error, no warning, the body simply does not run.** Five bare `return`s in `lower_for_iterable`. ⚠ The Core #10 silent-fallthrough ratchet NAMES that file but only detects `_ =>` arms, so a bare `return` walks straight past it — teach the ratchet to see bare returns as part of the fix (that widening is the class-fix; the five arms are the instances).
- **🆕🐛 [MED — the Book teaches two functions that DO NOT EXIST; found 2026-08-17] `zip(a, b)` and `enumerate(c)` appear in `docs/book/` as free functions.** The real spellings are methods (`a.zip(b)`, `c.enumerate()`). Every reader who copies those loop examples writes code that does not compile. Fix with the idiom campaign's book track — but this half is a plain correction and needs no campaign to justify it. (`range()` was the third fiction; it is gone — integer sequences are `..` / `..=` only.)
- **🆕🚨💥 [CRITICAL — MEMORY SAFETY, BOTH BACKENDS, from SAFE SPEC-DOCUMENTED SYNTAX; found 2026-08-17 by the for-in idiom scout, orchestrator-reproduced] `for x in &coll` + assigning to the loop binding DOUBLE FREES.** Six-line repro: `Vector[String] d = ["aa", "bb"]` / `for s in &d: s = "zz"` / read `d` → `gg check` prints **"OK: no semantic errors"** (rc 0, no diagnostic of any kind) and the program dies `free(): double free detected in tcache 2`, **SIGABRT**. Reproduced on the C backend and on LLVM. The element type is the discriminator: a heap-owning element (String) corrupts the heap, a scalar element does not. **This is not misuse of an escape hatch** — there is no `unsafe`, no ownership operator, and `docs/language-reference.md:1374` teaches `for x in &coll` as THE in-place mutation form, so the language's own documented idiom is memory-unsafe on its documented element types. ⚠ DISTINCT from the filed `&`-write-through gap (§9.1's status note, where the compiler silently DROPS the write for a by-value element type): that one loses data, this one corrupts the allocator. INTENDED: the assignment writes through as specified, or the program is REJECTED at check time with a teaching diagnostic — crashing is neither. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/security/attack_99_amp_for_in_element_assign_double_free.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `security_amp_for_in_element_assign_double_free` (tests/security.rs) asserting the SPECIFIED output, RED-verified 2026-08-17. ⛔ **This BLOCKS promoting `for x in xs` as the default idiom** (the campaign the scout was measuring): the idiom cannot be taught while its mutable form is unsafe.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — A2 SWEEP HAZARD, no executable guard; found 2026-08-17 by the Phase-4b confirming pass] 21 fixtures carry a `MUST EXCLUDE from any bulk `gg fmt` sweep` header that NOTHING ENFORCES.** Thirteen predate R42. The header is the sweep's own s4-a exclusion signal (`grep -rl 'MUST EXCLUDE' tests/fixtures`), so it IS machine-readable — but no guard asserts that a file carrying it was actually excluded, and no guard asserts that a fixture whose subject IS fmt output carries it. Both directions fail SILENTLY: sweeping a marked file bakes the defect it pins into the canonical form, and an unmarked fmt-subject fixture is swept without anyone noticing (measured this round: `fmt_golden_sample_input.gg` swept leaves its test GREEN-vacuous). Fix: a `tests/lints.rs` guard pairing the header set against the sweep's include list, plus the inverse check that every fixture wired into a formatter-API assertion carries the header. Do it BEFORE the A2 sweep regenerates — the sweep is a one-way flattening.
- **🆕🧹 [LOW — coverage gap, found 2026-08-17 by the Phase-4b confirming pass] `tests/fixtures/fmt_magic_comma/` has no directory census, so a future unwired fixture there goes unnoticed.** `fmt_suite_layout/` has one (`FMT_SUITE_LAYOUT_FIXTURES` + its lint); the new magic-comma directory does not. Both current fixtures ARE wired, so there is no live gap — this is the next-sibling hole (Core #4: what stops fixture N+1 from being unwired?). Add the census when the directory next grows.
- **🆕⚡ [HIGH — BLOCKS THE A2 SWEEP; ratified 2026-08-16 (define-gorget ledger); R42 Phase 4c] Implement `@fmt(skip)` on items — the formatter escape hatch, family CLOSED at one member.** `@fmt(skip)` above an item makes `gg fmt` emit that item's source BYTE-FOR-BYTE. Chosen over a `# fmt: skip` magic comment because attributes are already validated against an allowlist and unknown ones are rejected (`E_UnknownDirective`; the allowlist is at `src/semantic/mod.rs:219` inside `validate_attributes` at `:204-231` — `:165-200` is the SEPARATE `directive` mechanism, do not cite it for this), so a typo FAILS THE BUILD instead of silently doing nothing. ⚠ **That rationale is FALSE TODAY and fixing it is part of the track: `gg fmt` runs NO semantic validation** (`src/main.rs:3540` calls the parse-only `format_source_result`), so the SWEEP eats the file long before any build fails — the check must run on the path `gg fmt` actually takes — the silent-no-op class this round ruled against three times; rustfmt (`#[rustfmt::skip]`) is the precedent, while Black/Prettier/clang-format use comments only because their languages lack a validated annotation at those positions.

  **Why it blocks A2:** the bulk sweep is a ONE-WAY flattening of hand-built structure. Measured targets: `compiler/data/resources.gg` (a top-level `public static Vector[ResourceEntry] RESOURCES = [...]` data table, 3130 lines / 353 blanks → 1106/12 under the PRE-4b canon, and **1468/341 measured at a post-4b HEAD** — preserve-and-cap SAVED the paragraphing, so entry-field packing, not paragraph loss, is what still justifies a mark) and `lib/xtd/math3d.gg` (4×4 matrices inside methods such as `Mat4 identity():` whose entire body is the matrix; 840 → 786 lines, **16 hunks** post-4b — not the 29 measured before it — across 28 distinct (block, method) cells; never quote the hunk count as a method count — and the magic trailing comma does NOT rescue them: without a trailing comma the matrix collapses to one 94-column line, WITH one it explodes to 16 lines, one element per line, so the grid dies either way, exactly as it does in Black). The marker must exist and the affected items must be marked BEFORE the sweep is regenerated.

  **Scope.** Both known cases are ITEM-granular, so the grammar is NOT extended to expressions. Attributes today sit on function/struct/enum/trait defs (`docs/language-reference.md:654/731/758/832`); `static`/`const` items need the grammar row added, and **equip-method attribute support is REQUIRED, not a "verify" footnote** — every `math3d.gg` marker sits on a method, so a top-level-only implementation covers 0% of that target, and it carries TWO self-host changes (the `parse_equip_item` port AND the REJECT mirrors in `parse_module`'s item loop). ⚠ `type alias` and `newtype` have **no `attributes` AST field at all** and must gain one; they are in the HONOUR set, so wiring the reject guard onto them would ship wrong code. The skipped span is re-emitted verbatim from source, which makes idempotence automatic; the verbatim-emission path already exists from the R41 verbatim-first string work, and Track A's typed comment table makes marker-to-node attachment reliable. ⚠ The skip RE-INDENTS (min-over-non-blank-lines) — a raw byte copy emits files that do not re-parse. Accept/reject change ⇒ Core #9: all three lanes (Rust gg, ggdef, self-host parser mirrors), pinned by **per-lane driver tests** — NOT a `spectests/` fixture, since `spec_conformance.rs` reads only `spectests/run` and `spectests/parse-error/` is empty with no harness, so a fixture there could never go RED. Per Core #11 shipped WITH the feature.

  **The family is closed at ONE member, enforced not merely stated.** `@fmt(explode)`/`@fmt(pack)` are refused as a category — style options rather than escapes, AND redundant, since the magic trailing comma ratified the same day already expresses explode-vs-pack in the code itself (one-canonical-way, the rule that retired `pow()` for `**`). `@fmt(width=...)` and per-file configuration are refused for the gofmt reason. An unratified `@fmt(align)` is rejected by the existing directive allowlist until deliberately added. A REGION form (`@fmt(off)`/paired markers) is DEFERRED, not chosen: the next-item form covers both known cases and unbalanced region markers fail silently.
- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — SILENT WRONG-CODE, BOTH LANES, `gg check` clean; ratified 2026-08-16 (define-gorget ledger), surfaced by the R42 A2 style review] A `case` arm naming something that does not exist silently becomes a CATCH-ALL that kills every arm below it — implement the ratified two-rule REJECT.** Measured at HEAD, one probe, `enum Day{Mon,Sat}`, scrutinee `Mon()` returned from a function (nothing const-folds), `gg check` → "OK: no semantic errors" for every cell:

  | pattern | name is a variant of the scrutinee? | Rust gg | ggdef | verdict |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `case Sat:` | yes | `else-arm` ✓ | `sat-arm` ✗ | DIVERGENCE A — ggdef BINDS instead of matching |
  | `case Sat():` | yes | `else-arm` ✓ | `else-arm` ✓ | agree |
  | `case Satx:` | no (typo) | `typo-arm` | `typo-arm` | agree — both silently catch-all |
  | `case Satx():` | no (typo) | `typo-arm` (catch-all) | `else-arm` (never matches) | DIVERGENCE B |

  With the typo'd arm FIRST, Rust gg prints `typo-arm/typo-arm` — two live arms dead — while ggdef prints `mon-arm/sat-arm`: one accepted program, two different behaviours ⇒ Core #8, ≥2 bugs. Not limited to typos: `case Red:` where `Red` is a variant of a DIFFERENT enum than the scrutinee is taken by BOTH engines. Root: `src/semantic/typecheck.rs:5833-5840` deliberately treats a `Pattern::Binding` whose name is not in the scrutinee's variant set as a variable binding ("It's a variable binding — acts as a catch-all"), and the qualified spelling (`case Day.Sat:`) is a fixed point of the whole axis on both engines.

  **RATIFIED FIX — two rules, both compile ERRORS (ledger 2026-08-16):** **(1) UNREACHABLE-ARM** — any arm following a catch-all (bare binding, `_`, or `else`) is an error (Rust's `unreachable_patterns`, promoted from lint to error); this catches the typo'd variant, the typo'd CONSTANT, and works on non-enum scrutinees. **(2) BARE NAME ON AN ENUM SCRUTINEE MUST RESOLVE** — where the scrutinee's type is an enum, a bare identifier in the arm's TOP-LEVEL pattern must resolve to a variant or a `const`/`static`, else an error whose diagnostic points at `else:`; this closes (1)'s blind spot, a typo in the LAST arm where nothing below becomes unreachable.

  **Bindings are NOT removed and cannot be** — `Pattern::Binding` is how destructuring names payloads (`case Custom(r, g, b):`), how guards name the scrutinee (`case x if is_even(x):`), and how `is` introduces names into the following `then` block (`docs/language-reference.md:1930`). All SIX bare-binding sites in the corpus match on `int` and are unaffected by rule (2): `match_guard_complex.gg:30`, `test_match_advanced.gg:121`, `const_match_pattern.gg:41`, `match_patterns.gg:17`, `fmt_suite_layout/orphan_close_arm_containers.gg:59,71`.

  **PRECEDENT — this project was already bitten by this exact class:** before 2026-05-13 a bare `case CONST_NAME:` always shadowed the constant with a fresh binding, "so the FIRST `case` arm always matched (every input went down it)", found during self-host `format_gir.gg` work (see `tests/fixtures/const_match_pattern.gg`'s header). Fixed for constants only; the variant flavour stayed live.

  **OPEN for the implementing scout:** whether rule (2) extends to NESTED pattern positions — `case Some(v):` where the payload type is itself an enum would reject a legitimate destructuring bind under a naive reading, while `case Some(None):` must keep working. Decide with the scout, not in the brief. Accept/reject change ⇒ Core #9: all three lanes + cross-lane NEG conformance fixtures, its own round. Durable repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/case_unresolved_name_is_silent_catchall.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_case_unresolved_name_rejected`.
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — VISIBLE-FLOW HOLE, filed 2026-08-10; found by the Fable error-model review, orchestrator-verified] A bare `Result`-typed value in a `T` position silently unwraps-or-early-returns with NO mark — surviving pre-D29 value-plane auto-propagation.** Measured: `int fails() throws String` / `Result[int,String] r = fails()` / `int consume(int x)` — the call `consume(r)` passes `gg check` clean, and at runtime **the callee never runs** and the error propagates (probe printed only the outer catch's `-1`). ⚠ **PROBE PREMISE (re-measured 2026-08-10 post-rebase on a fresh build): the call must sit in a PROPAGATING context** — `should_auto_propagate` gates on enclosing-has-throws-or-returns-Result, so the same shape in a non-throws `main` rejects correctly with `E_TypeMismatch`; a scout re-verifying in bare `main` samples the wrong cell and would wrongly read this claim as stale (that exact near-miss happened during the post-rebase sweep). D29 ratified that every fallible USE carries a mark; this is a second, unmarked propagation channel that D29's subject (fallible CALLS) does not cover — a Core #15e Q4 category gap, not a widening of the existing rule. ⚠ **Two ratified doctrines in direct conflict:** `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_autoprop_indirect_bare_arg_skips_call.gg` and `sound_autoprop_method_arg_rejected.gg` record adjacent shapes with headers describing the free-call behaviour as INTENDED (the free-call path sets `expected_type` to the callee's declared param type, so `Result`→non-`Result` fires by design). Re-litigating those headers is part of the fix. **Blocks A34** (the annotated causal chain): its premise is that the mandatory mark makes the compiler aware of every propagation hop, which is false while this path exists — so killing it is a PREREQUISITE, not a parallel track. Proposed disposition (Fable R1, HIGH confidence): a bare `Result`-typed value in a `T` position becomes `E_TypeMismatch`; propagation happens at `!`-marked calls only. Owner call — this is an accept/reject change and needs all lanes per Core #9. ⚡ **RULED (D45 pin 6, owner 2026-08-10): the KILL is RATIFIED** — exactly the proposed disposition. Fixture dispositions are OPPOSITE (pass-1 review correction): `method_arg_rejected`'s "INTENDED: both accept" is OVERTURNED → flips to a NEGATIVE (both call kinds reject); `indirect_bare_arg_skips_call` is VINDICATED — its callees take `Result[int,int] &x`, so its INTENDED ("each callee runs") is what the kill mandates → graduates as a passing POSITIVE. Rides round E0 (D45) (see the D45 block in the define-gorget section).
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — check-clean MISCOMPILE, filed 2026-08-10; found by the Fable review, orchestrator-verified] `catch` on a VOID fallible call miscompiles — `gg check` clean, `gg build` fails with a raw C error leaking to the user.** Probe: `void act() throws String: throw "boom"` + `act()! catch (e): print("recovered")` → `gg check` reports "OK: no semantic errors"; `gg build` exits 1 with `vc1.c:3701:11: error: void value not ignored as it ought to be`. Two defects in one: (a) the void success-type cell of `catch` is not handled in lowering; (b) **a C-compile failure of generated code is by definition an ICE and must never surface as a raw `.c:` error** — `language-design.md:120` already promises "errors should point to source, not generated C". Fix (a); for (b) trap C-compiler failure and render an ICE with the *Gorget* span, exit 101. Class-guard (Core #6): have the integration harness grep build stderr for C-compiler ERROR lines (`\.c:` co-occurring with `error:`) on every fixture and fail on sight — NOT bare `\.c:`, which is red-on-arrival everywhere (pass-5 measured: a pre-existing `-Wmissing-field-initializers` leak prints 3 `.c:` WARNING lines on a two-line hello-world; that leak is filed on the A38 track). Axis-complete fixtures per Core #12: success-type × {void, scalar, resource} × {inline, block} × {`catch`, `rethrow`}. ⚡ Rides round E0 (D45). Note D45 pin 4 retires `rethrow` at E3 — this fix still nets BOTH forms while they exist (the rethrow cells become the tombstone-diagnostic cells at E3).
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — exit-taxonomy violation, filed 2026-08-10; found by the Fable review, orchestrator-verified] The C runtime calls `exit(1)` 59 times, bypassing the error model and emitting the STATIC-REJECTION exit class for RUNTIME failures.** Count: `find src/backend/c -name '*.c' -not -name sqlite3.c -exec grep -c 'exit(1)' {} +` → 59 (regenerate before acting). `language-reference.md` §10.10 pins exit 1 = "the program was rejected before it ran — stdout stays exactly empty" and states the classes MUST be distinct "so a runtime crash can never masquerade as a correct static rejection". A missing file makes `read_file` `fprintf`+`exit(1)` from the runtime, which is exactly that masquerade — and errno-style hidden fatal failure is a named anti-goal (`language-design.md:81`). Compounding: `lib/std/*.gg` declares **0** `throws` (`read_file` is typed infallible, `lib/std/fs.gg:6`), so the stdlib has no error channel to route these into — the fix is coupled to the D17 stdlib-throws sweep. Each site becomes either a thrown `IoError` or a `trap` (exit 101); add a lint ratchet forbidding new `exit(1)` in the runtime. ⚠ This also breaks the machine-consumer contract: 59 places an agentic harness mis-classifies "my program failed" as "my program didn't compile". ⚡ Rides round E1 (D45), coupled to the D17 sweep as filed; thrown-error cases exit **102** under D45 pin 7's taxonomy.
- **🆕📖 [MED — the book documents a FICTIONAL stdlib, filed 2026-08-10; orchestrator-verified] `docs/book/10-errors.md`'s flagship example does not compile.** The chapter teaches `String content = read_file(path)!`, but `lib/std/fs.gg:6` declares `read_file` INFALLIBLE, so the real shape is rejected: `error[E_MissingFallibleMark]: this `!` does not mark a fallible call`. The chapter's error-handling narrative is written against a stdlib that does not exist. Rides the D17 stdlib-throws sweep (fix the stdlib, then the book becomes true); until then the examples are teaching an idiom that cannot be run. Also demote `throws String` from the chapter's default idiom to one "prototyping only" paragraph — it is taught 9× and is the anti-goal at `language-design.md:81`. ⚡ Rides round E1 (D45) as the chapter's FIRST pass — against the surface AS SHIPPED AT E1: real stdlib, `catch (e): throw …` instead of `rethrow` (legal at HEAD), no wrapper-enum/`From` pattern (pin 3 deletes it), `throws String` demoted + the multiplicity section (pin 11) + the `stringly-error` lint makes the demotion mechanical (pin 13). The set-alias/subtraction teaching is the SECOND pass at E2/E3 when the syntax exists — teaching syntax that cannot run is this entry's own defect class (pass-2 review catch).
- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — ICE, filed 2026-08-10 during the error-model design discussion] `throw <catch-error-binding>` inside a `match` ARM panics the Tier 2a consume-site validator at IR lowering (exit 101).** `thread 'gg-main' panicked at src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:2114: Tier 2a consume-site violation … fn @handle bb2 i3 — AssignIntoOwnedSlot(dst: CE) — owned-but-live source consumed without preceding clone`. The catch binding is consumed by the `throw` without the preceding clone the validator demands — an ownership-tag gap on the catch err-binding when the consume happens inside a nested match arm. **The `match` arm is the discriminator, bisected via `gg build`:** bare `throw e` in the catch block → OK · `throw e` inside an `if` → OK · `match e` with no `throw` → OK · `throw e` inside a `match` arm → **ICE**. Enum arity is not the trigger (reproduces at one variant and two). ⚠ **`gg check` reports "OK: no semantic errors"** — the panic is in IR lowering, so check-only gates are blind to it; reproduce with `gg build`. **Why it matters beyond the crash:** this is the natural idiom for PARTIAL error handling (recover from some variants, re-throw the rest). The working workaround is Result-capture + nested match (`Result[T,E] r = f()` then `case Error(CE.NotFound(_)):` — verified correct, prints 8080), so the capability exists and this is an ergonomics gap wearing an ICE. **Discriminated from the filed Tier 2a siblings:** the `s.debug()` panic is a different site (`mod.rs:2084`) with a builtin-method trigger; the Dict/Set `put`/`insert` consume-classification item is a check-time REJECT, not an ICE; the self-host `lower_catch_expr` err-binding item is an SH-lane verify-by-running task, whereas this is a Rust-gg lowering panic. Durable repro + `#[ignore]`d test asserting the INTENDED output (`8080`): `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/catch_binding_throw_in_match_arm_ice.gg` / `integration.rs::catch_binding_throw_in_match_arm_ice`. (b) ⭐ *Why symlinks at all:* imports resolve SAME-DIRECTORY for bare names (`from parser import parse_source`) and downward-dotted for `std.*`, via `resolve_import_path(base, segments)` (`src/loader.rs:76`) — there is no sibling/parent path, so a driver cannot import a module from another directory and the symlinks fake co-location. So the asymmetry is Rust=embed-authoritative (hack) vs SH=disk-authoritative (reference-grade); succession makes SH's model the target, so fixing the Rust side is oracle-hygiene, not new design. Reference-grade end state: stdlib is an ordinary package resolved through the real loader relative to the compiler install (Rust sysroot / Go GOROOT / Zig lib-next-to-binary), disk authoritative, embed at most a hermetic-distribution fallback — the SAME shared-library-in-a-manifest shape (b) needs. Own architectural round (loader/package resolution), NOT a formatter-theme fold; (a)/(b)/(c) are one campaign. Owner asked whether embed is reference-grade (2026-08-10); answer = no.

The remaining wrinkle is only that `Vector` gets direct `.enumerate()` sugar while Set must spell `.iter().enumerate()`. Defensible on the typing, and the fix-it makes it discoverable — **once the fix-it compiles** (see the panic entry above; fix that FIRST, this is cosmetic by comparison). Decide then whether to add the Set sugar or document the asymmetry.

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — LEAK, both backends, filed 2026-07-25 by the Track-B1 output-review] Re-assigning THROUGH a `&`-param never drops the param slot's OLD pointee.** `void f(Vector[int] &v): v = build(2)` leaks the caller's original buffer (64 bytes, LSan-confirmed). Write-through VALUES are already correct (4 then 2) — only the drop of the old value is missing. **PRE-EXISTING and independent of the return boundary** (identical before and after Track B1); it was MASKED in the combined costume `local = v; v = build(2); return local`, where the bind's double-free cancelled the missing drop — so fixing the bind correctly UNMASKED it. (`security_known_unsafe` is the wrong harness: it runs `detect_leaks=0`, so a pure leak cannot trip it.) Fix at the write-through re-assign: drop the old pointee before storing the new one. Leak-freedom is non-negotiable. 🆕 **THE `String` CELL OF THE SAME DEFECT IS ALSO LIVE (measured 2026-07-27 during the Family-1 round; same root, second type — file as one, fix as one).** `void app_str(String &x): x = x + "!"` called in an 8-iteration loop on a struct's `String` field leaks **70 bytes in 7 objects** via `gorget_str_cat` ← `app_str` ← `main`, plus 6 bytes for the initial heap seed (76 B / 8 allocs total), LSan-confirmed, exit 99. **Attribution is tight:** the identical concatenation on a plain LOCAL (`String x = …; x = x + "!"` in the same loop) is **CLEAN**, so it is specifically the assign-through-a-`&`-parameter that fails to release the old buffer — not string concatenation. **PRE-EXISTING and NOT Family-1's:** it reproduces byte-identically at the pre-Family-1 commit on a plain (non-Box) `String` field, a cell that fix does not touch. ⚠ **It is what stopped the Family-1 box-projection leak fixture from using a heap-seeded String field** — `security/sound_amp_deref_box_field_leak.gg` seeds its field with a LITERAL and assigns a computed string exactly once, so it pins the whole-struct-clone leak Family-1 fixed without also tripping THIS one; its header records that. Method: delete alone, run the targeted subset, report which fixtures depend on it. It is a read-site reconstruction of what a writer dropped (Core #1 / wrong-layer heuristic).

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — MEMORY-UNSAFE, both backends] An `equip` method whose body is an EXPRESSION-BODY return of its own OWNING `!` param USE-AFTER-FREES.** The statement-return path move-zeroes a returned `!` param (`owning_param_returned` → `move_zero_and_mark`); the FOUR expression-body return sites in `functions.rs` do not, so the callee's exit `drop_if_alive *p` frees the buffer the returned value aliases. Emitted GIR: `_2 = copy _1.*` / `[Mv] _0 = copy _2` / `drop_if_alive _1.*` / `return copy _0`. Live shape in-tree: `equip Row with FromRow: Row from_row(Row !row): row` (`lib/xtd/db.gg`). Durable repro + `#[ignore]`d test asserting the INTENDED output: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/equip_exprbody_owning_param_return_uaf.gg` / `integration.rs::equip_exprbody_owning_param_return_uaf`. Fix at the class, not per-site: the four expression-body returns need the same owning-param transfer the statement return already performs (a shared helper, or hoist the move-zero to the one place both forms converge). **Burning this down is the precondition for promoting the `AssignIntoReturnSlot` consume-site class to FATAL** — it is the ONE violation across the 1676-fixture corpus (measured; regenerate with `GG_VALIDATE_CONSUME_SITES=<log>` + `gg build --emit-gir` over `tests/fixtures/*.gg`).

- **🆕🧹 [MED — Layering, typed metadata] The `&`/`!`-param auto-deref site reads the WRONG typed bit for "is this an owning param".** `exprs/mod.rs`'s `let is_move_param = ctx.is_owned_local(builder, local_id)` is documented as "`!` params (owned): use Move to transfer ownership", but a `!` param's `ownership` is `Borrowed { Param(self), Unique }` — identical to a `&` param — so `is_owned_local` is FALSE and that branch never fires for the shape its comment names. The single typed bit that distinguishes them is `is_owning_param` (`set_owning_param`'s own doc says so). Measured live: adding a `&`-param-only ownership tag to the else-branch immediately mis-tagged `!` params in the self-host (`Lexer__lex_emit`, `Parser__mk_expr/mk_type/mk_pat`, `parser___decompose_with_stmt`) and produced 10 consume-site violations until `is_owning_param` was excluded explicitly. Switching the predicate to `is_owning_param` is the write-through, but it CHANGES the emitted shape for every `!`-param read (Move + `set_owned` instead of Copy), so it needs its own measurement pass. Grep the other `is_owned_local`-as-a-proxy-for-owning-param sites at the same time.

- **🆕📐 [MED — charter] A `String &`-param bind still costs ONE clone where a hand-writer pays ZERO.** The other resource types bind a `&`-param as a zero-cost CoW Ptr alias; String cannot, because a Copy-mode store of a String read through a pointer is `gorget_string_copy_cow` at BOTH backends (an eager deep copy), not the shallow `memcpy` every other aggregate gets. The frontend now tags that temp honestly (drop-registered + `FreshOwned`, which is what removed the double-clone at the return boundary), but the eager copy itself is a backend-layer decision the GIR cannot express: `AssignMode::Copy` means "shallow alias" for `GorgetArray` and "deep clone" for `GorgetString`. Reference-grade fix: make the GIR say which it means (the LIR already spells the shallow form as `SlotStore { is_move: true }`, and `insts.rs`'s own comment calls that "a SHALLOW byte-copy (an alias / borrow)"), then let the boundary chokepoints materialize uniformly. Related: the alias-at-bind leaves the now-dead auto-deref temp emitted (a wasted 64-byte struct `memcpy` per bind — no allocation, but it should be DCE'd or not emitted).

- **🆕🛡 [MED — Core #6 burn-down] Promote `ConsumeSiteClass::AssignIntoReturnSlot` from the non-fatal `assign_warnings` partition to `fatal_warnings`.** The class exists and fires (4 unit tests in `src/ir/validate.rs` pin it firing on borrowed AND untracked sources, and staying silent on the sound owned-and-dead transfer and on the borrow-contract return). It is routed non-fatally because a new class is fatal the instant it lands and the corpus count is not yet zero. Run the burn-down with `GG_RETURN_SLOT_GUARD=fatal`; when the count reaches zero, delete the partition + the env gate and let it join the other six classes. Re-measure the count before acting on it.

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — SOUNDNESS, MEMORY-UNSAFE, both backends, adversarial audit 2026-07-22, parent-reproduced+ASan-confirmed] The D10 "one exclusive writer" rule — the load-bearing invariant the whole lazy-CoW model rests on — is NOT structurally enforced.** `check_call_aliasing` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs:1472`) is a SYNTACTIC place-overlap check spread across 3 call-shaped arms (`check_expr.rs:316` Call / `:438` MethodCall / `:1304` DotShorthand), with costume holes that produce heap-use-after-free. Confirmed holes (ASan `gg build --sanitize`, C backend; both backends share the semantic pass): **#1 (broadest) get-chain element VIEW as a call arg** — `.get()/.first()/.last().unwrap()` are invisible participants (no MethodCall arm in `find_root_def_id_with_path` helpers.rs:567) → `f(v.get(0).unwrap(), &v)` accepts; resource element → UAF in `gorget_string_copy_cow`. **#2 method RECEIVER not a participant** (`check_call_aliasing(args)` only; receiver walked separately) → `v.grow_from(&v)` accepts → UAF. **#3 module-level `Static` roots excluded** by the `DefKind::Variable`-only filter (helpers.rs:1494; globals are Static, resolve.rs:714) → `f(G,&G)` accepts+UAF while byte-identical LOCAL `f(L,&L)` REJECTS (clean isolated proof; parent-repro'd c10c-vs-c10d). **#4 for-loop body mutation via a `&`-arg free call** (`for x in v: grow(&v)`) opaque to the iterable guard (check_expr.rs:576) → UAF; direct `v.push` in body rejects. **#5 match-scrutinee element binding across a mutating arm** (`case Some(x)` over `v.first()`, then `v.push`) not recorded as a CoW-borrow source → UAF (for-loop has a guard, match has none). **#6 MED KNOWN-DEFERRED** `v[i] += mutate(&v)` should reject per D10(b) addendum-2 (RV-C track), doesn't. Structural half HOLDS: stored `&`-bindings (`Type &name=...`) rejected at parse (LocalBorrowBindSigil). ROOT CAUSE: check is spelled over SYNTAX not SEMANTICS (sees `v[i]` not `v.get(i)`, args not receiver, Variable not Static) + scattered across 3 arms, not one chokepoint. **⚖ RULED (owner 2026-07-22, "reference-grade, no question"): (b) STRUCTURAL.** Rebuild as ONE semantic place-overlap chokepoint that EVERY call routes through (free-fn / method incl. RECEIVER / operator-overload / index-store `__set`/`__put` / closure / constructor), with a complete participant model + a SEMANTIC place-resolver that descends `.get()/.first()/.last().unwrap()` view-chains (reuse `find_get_chain_taint_root` helpers.rs:512 / `find_collection_source_with_path` :602), the receiver (implicit sigil: `&self`⇒writer, bare `self`⇒reader), and `Static` roots — Core #4 centralize-at-producer, retire the 3 syntactic arms. Land WITH a Core #6 guard forcing every future call-lowering shape through the single check (arm-count / unavoidable-by-construction) so costume #7 can't reopen it. Patching-the-syntactic-check is REJECTED. Also fold the deferred #6 (`v[i] += mutate(&v)`, D10(b) addendum-2 / RV-C) into the same chokepoint. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews→gauntlet ON OWNER GO; the scout designs the chokepoint grounded in the D10 ledger + layering-discipline doc (devbook/24); neg fixture per hole; ASan-gate; both lanes; the audit's for/match separate nets (iterable guard, match) get subsumed or kept-and-covered. Own scout→brief→gauntlet after the ruling; neg fixture per hole (get/first/last/dict × arg-order; receiver; Static; for; match; String elements); ASan-gate; both lanes. **DURABLE REPROS (committed 2026-07-25, one per costume, all RED-verified at HEAD — each passes `gg check` then ASan-aborts with a heap-use-after-free; all `#[ignore]`d asserting the INTENDED `E_BorrowConflict` reject, in `tests/fixtures/security/` + `security.rs`):** `sound_excl_getchain_local` (#1) · `sound_excl_getchain_static` (#3) · `sound_excl_getchain_fieldpath` (#1 field-path) · `sound_excl_getchain_alias_provenance` (bind alias) · `sound_excl_receiver_writer` (#2) · `sound_excl_forbody_amp_writer` (#4 — ⚠ needs ≥2 elements; the 1-element shape is a FALSE NEGATIVE) · `sound_excl_nested_writer_{call,closure,index_store,ctor,tuple}` (writer nested ONE CALL DEEP, per call shape) · `sound_excl_overload_alias_twin` (⚠ built on `Acc b = a; a + b`; `a + a` runs CLEAN and is false confidence). Hole #5 has the OPPOSITE polarity — see the match-scrutinee MATERIALIZE entry below. LIVE over-rejection guards that must stay green: `sound_ctor_mover_sibling_accept` (ADDENDUM 3's mover exemption), `sound_ctor_mover_first_reject` (must stay `E_UseAfterMove`, the LIVENESS axis), `sound_getchain_sever_accept` (intra-function sever). ⚠ **ggdef is NOT the oracle for this class** — measured 2026-07-25, it ACCEPTS every in-subset fixture above: it is a value-semantics interpreter, so realloc-invalidation is structurally unobservable there. ggdef owes the same reject as part of this landing (Core #9). Session-scratch probes /tmp/excl_probes/ superseded by the committed fixtures.

- **🐛💥 [HIGH — MEMORY-SAFETY, both backends, R1 — box-fix output-review 2026-07-24, parent-reproduced] Moving a `Box[T]` via `!bx` into a consuming `!`-param fn BAD-FREES a stack address.** `void consume(Box[int] !b): print("consumed")` + `Box[int] bx = Box(42); consume(!bx)` is ACCEPTED by `gg check` but ASan-aborts `attempting free on address which was not malloc()-ed` (C) / SIGABRT (LLVM) on ALL `Box[T]` (int/String/struct, verified). The `!`-param Box CONSUMPTION ABI passes the box param as `&slot` (the caller's stack address) and the callee `free`s it → freeing a stack address (miscompile of an accepted program). Pre-existing + ORTHOGONAL to the 2026-07-24 scope-exit-drop fix (that touches the LOCAL's drop, not the `!`-param ABI). More severe than the two box siblings below (a memory-safety abort, not a leak/compile-break). **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/security/box_move_param_bad_free.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d `security_safe` test `box_move_param_bad_free_safe`; expected `consumed\ndone`). Fix the `!`-param Box consumption ABI (free the boxed heap alloc, not the slot address); ASan-gate both backends. Own scout→gauntlet.
- **🐛 [HIGH — pre-existing C-emit name collision, BOTH backends, found 2026-07-24 fixing the Box[resource-struct] leak] `Box__<inner>__drop` is DEFINED TWICE for `Vector[Box[R]]` and nested `Box[Box[R]]` when R needs a recursive drop.** `helpers.rs:313` emits `static inline void Box__R__drop(Box__R self){GORGET_FREE(...)}` (the box's trivial free-helper) while `emit_box_drop_wrappers` (`emit_types.rs:1623`) emits the recursive `void Box__R__drop(void* slot)` wrapper — both are referenced the moment a Box is a collection ELEMENT or a nested-box payload → C `redefinition of 'Box__R__drop'` / LLVM runtime-compile failure. This is a COMPILE BREAK, not a leak (the program never builds). `Option[Box[R]]` and struct-field `Option[Box[R]]` are FINE (build + LSan-clean — those paths don't emit the free-helper). This collision is ALSO what blocks fixing the nested-`Box[Box[R]]` LEAK: routing the outer box's drop through the inner `Box__R__drop` wrapper (the natural fix) trips exactly this redefinition. Fix = one owner of the `Box__R__drop` symbol — give the trivial free-helper a distinct name (mirror the `__gorget_box_free_R` scheme) OR suppress it when the recursive wrapper is emitted. Own scout→gauntlet; ASan + build-both-backends gate; add `Vector[Box[resource-struct]]` + `Box[Box[resource-struct]]` fixtures that must BUILD and be LSan-clean. **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/box_element_drop_symbol_collision.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d integration test `box_element_drop_symbol_collision`, expected `done`). ⚠ CORRECTION (verified 2026-07-24): only the collection-ELEMENT case (`Vector[Box[R]]`) compile-breaks; nested `Box[Box[R]]` actually BUILDS (it LEAKS instead — file that separately, do not lump).

The full drop lives in the UNIFIED `__gorget_dtor_R` (`user fn + field frees`, `type_drop_fns[R].drop_fn_name` for user-drop/collision types). Repro: `struct Money{String label}; equip Money with Drop: void drop(!self): print("x")`; `Box[Money] bx = Box(Money(heaped()))` → LSan 22-byte leak (the user `drop` DOES run — "x" prints — but `label` is never freed). Fix the CLASS at both sites: target `type_drop_fns[R].drop_fn_name` (= `__gorget_dtor_R` for user-drop/collision, `R__drop` for plain Recursive) — the same unified full-drop a plain `Money` local uses. Touches `box_inner_drop_fn` (WIDE — the wrapper is shared by Vector/Option/field Box contexts) → own scout→gauntlet, ASan-gate every wrapper context. Distinct from the Recursive-struct/enum leak fixed 2026-07-24 (that was a MISSING match arm; this is a WRONG-fn-selection shared with the wrapper). **known_gaps repro: `tests/fixtures/security/box_user_drop_struct_field_leak.gg`** (`#[ignore]`d `security_safe_no_leak` test `box_user_drop_struct_field_no_leak`, expected `done\ndropping`; verified 2026-07-24 — user `drop` runs, `label` leaks 22B).
