- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — SILENT CALL LOSS, `gg check` clean, BOTH backends; found 2026-07-27 by the Family-1 output review, reproduced by three parties] An INDIRECT call (closure-variable call OR IIFE) silently SKIPS when any argument is `Result`-typed — even though the callee's parameter is itself a `Result`.** `maybe_auto_propagate`'s FIRST gate is `ctx.func_state.expected_type`. The free-call path and the method-call path each set it to the callee's declared parameter type before lowering the argument, so a `Result` bound for a `Result` parameter is correctly left alone. **Neither indirect path sets it**, so `should_auto_propagate` is consulted against whatever ambient value was left over, fires, unwraps the `Result`, sees an `Error`, and PROPAGATES — the callee body never runs and the enclosing fn returns the error. Nothing was there to unwrap: the parameter is declared `Result[int,int] &x`. ⚠⚠ **TWO INDEPENDENT MASKS — EITHER ONE ALONE HIDES IT, and between them they made THREE people measure three different things in one round, each reading their own program correctly.** Measured at base, one program, all four cells (`1` = called, `-1` = skipped): `auto` + `Error(5)` → **SKIPPED, the only exposing cell** · `auto` + `Ok(7)` → called (masked by PAYLOAD: the unwrap succeeds and the call proceeds, so a correct and a broken compiler are indistinguishable) · `Callable[void(&Result[int,int])] f = …` + `Error(5)` → called (masked by ANNOTATION: the annotated VarDecl leaves `expected_type` in a state that blocks the unwrap) · annotated + `Ok` → called (masked twice). **So any probe must be BOTH `auto`-annotated AND `Error`-seeded**; adjacency of the constructor and ambient-state pollution were separately ruled out (construction moved into a helper fn plus an intervening statement — still skips). **PARTIALLY CLOSED by the Family-1 chokepoint, for the PROJECTION argument form only** — a resolvable `&h.r` returns early before `maybe_auto_propagate`, so both indirect kinds now reach the callee (RED-verified base→post; live pin `cow_amp_projection_indirect_call_arg.gg`). **STILL BROKEN for the BARE-IDENTIFIER form** (`&hr`) at both call kinds, because `try_resolve_place` has no `Expr::Identifier` arm BY DESIGN. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_autoprop_indirect_bare_arg_skips_call.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_autoprop_indirect_bare_arg_skips_call` (both siblings in one fixture), asserting the INTENDED call. ⚠ **DO NOT close it by giving the producer an `Identifier` arm** — that changes what `&x` means at every `&`-formation site to paper over a defect in the CALL paths. **Reference-grade fix: make the closure-variable and IIFE call sites set `expected_type` to the callee's declared parameter type, exactly as their free-call and method-call siblings do** — one site, fixes bare AND projection, both call kinds (Core #4: the class, not the costume). ⚡ **FIX SHAPE SUPERSEDED (D45 pin 6, owner 2026-08-10): do NOT set `expected_type` — the value-plane auto-prop firing is killed outright at E0** (check-side `E_TypeMismatch` on bare `Result` in a `T` position; lowering peel re-gated on `!`-mark provenance written at the mark). Under the re-gate an unmarked indirect call never peels, so the callee runs — this entry's defect dissolves and its fixture GRADUATES as a passing positive (full ruling on the visible-flow entry, CoW/High). Rides round E0 (D45).

- **🆕🛡 [HIGH-attention LESSON, no open defect — the arm-set lints have a PERMANENT blind spot; recorded 2026-07-27 after `&g.a` survived three fix rounds] The place-resolver guards compare SYNTACTIC `Expr::` arm sets; part of the producer's object domain is TYPE-DRIVEN and is structurally invisible to them.** `top_level_expr_arm_variants` (`tests/lints.rs`) extracts `Expr::<Variant>` names from arm-indentation lines. But `try_resolve_field_place` resolves a `Guard[T]` auto-deref (`g.f`, where the field lives on the guarded value and `lookup_field` on the `Guard__…` wrapper misses) via a **type-level branch**, not an `Expr::` arm. Both `amp_formation_and_assign_cover_the_same_place_forms` and `place_type_only_covers_the_producer_forms` were GREEN while `&g.a` silently dropped the callee's write and `g.a = v` worked — Family-1's own served-at-one-face signature — and an earlier version of the object limb's comment named `g.f` as a form it made "visible". It never could. **CONSEQUENCE FOR REVIEWERS AND FOR ANYONE ADDING A RESOLVER BRANCH: a green lint here means only that the two SYNTACTIC arm sets agree.** A type-driven branch (guard wrappers, and any future auto-deref/newtype/smart-pointer projection) owes a MEASURED write-through probe, because no lint in that file will ask for one. ⚠ **Same blind spot, still-open instance:** `&t.0.fd` (struct field under a tuple) drops the write — filed separately as the resolver arm-MATRIX gap — and neither lint can see that cross-resolver object asymmetry either, for exactly this reason. Fixing the extractor is not obviously worth it (it would have to model the resolvers' type logic); the durable mitigation is the axis-complete write-through fixture, `cow_amp_projection_base_shapes.gg`, whose rows 7 and 8 exist because the lints could not demand them.

- **🆕🧹 [MED — LATENT SILENT-DISAGREEMENT SOURCE, no live defect known; found 2026-07-27 while root-causing the Family-1 auto-propagate family] `resolve_tuple_field_type` returns `I64_TYPE` on a MISS, not `None` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/type_reg.rs`).** A caller that wants "the type, or nothing" gets a plausible WRONG answer instead of an unknown, and no fail-safe downstream can distinguish the two. Concretely dangerous for any predicate whose `None` branch is the safe one: a bogus `I64_TYPE` reads as "not a propagating `Result`", which is exactly the answer that licenses skipping `maybe_auto_propagate`. **Avoided rather than fixed for now:** `place_expr_type_only`'s `TupleFieldAccess` arm does the struct-field lookup INLINE and returns `None` on a miss, precisely so that `None` keeps meaning UNKNOWN there — see its comment. The fallback stays because `resolve_tuple_field_type`'s other callers (`patterns.rs`, the tuple-field read arm) want a type unconditionally. **Reference-grade shape: split it** — a `try_resolve_tuple_field_type -> Option<TypeId>` core plus a thin `resolve_tuple_field_type` that applies the `I64_TYPE` default for callers that genuinely want it — so no future caller silently inherits a wrong type. Audit the existing callers when doing so; the fallback's own comment records a past mis-typing bug it was added to paper over, which suggests at least one of them would rather have the `Option`. ⚠ **NAME THE WHOLE FAMILY, not this one function (Core #15e Q3):** `infer_collection_element_type` has the IDENTICAL shape — its own doc says "or `I64_TYPE` if unknown" — and `place_expr_type_only`'s `Index` arm calls it one line below the `TupleFieldAccess` arm that was hardened against exactly this. That arm is reachable today only via `Deque` (separately broken upstream, its own filing), so no live defect is claimed — but the two are one class and should be split together, along with any other `-> TypeId`-with-a-default helper feeding a predicate whose `None` branch is the safe one.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg check` ACCEPTS, C backend then FAILS TO BUILD; PRE-EXISTING, re-confirmed 2026-07-27] `&(*b).0` — a TUPLE FIELD through a `Box` DEREF — does not compile.** `Box[(Result[int,int], int)] b = Box((Error(mk()), 1))` + `take(&(*b).0)` → `gg check` clean, then the C build fails with `unknown type name 'Tuple__Result__int64_t__int64_t__int64_t'` (the mangled tuple type is referenced but never emitted) plus `void value not ignored as it ought to be`. Measured identical at base `6cf79126` and after the Family-1 round, so it is neither introduced nor fixed here. ⚠ **IT BRIEFLY BECAME A SILENT WRONG ANSWER** during the round — at tip `a65bbe97` it BUILT and printed `in take` / `1` / `called`, swallowing the `Error` — because the auto-propagate pre-check's `None` case was inverted. The fail-safe (`None => false` in `lower_call_arg`'s `safe_to_skip_auto_propagate`) restored the loud failure; that loud→silent→loud excursion is why this is filed rather than left as folklore (Core #8: a loud build failure is strictly better than a silent wrong answer, and trading one for the other is a regression even when the shape was already broken). **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_amp_box_tuple_field_cc_fail.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_amp_box_tuple_field_cc_fail`, asserting the INTENDED propagation. Likely sibling of the filed `(*b).0` READ-arm `llc` failure (B5) — check whether one emission fix closes both. ⚡ **DO NOT CLOSE via pin 6 (S3 scout, 2026-08-10):** the `Tuple__Result__…` C-emit gap is reachable by a program pin 6 still ACCEPTS (measured: plain `&(*b).1` int-field borrow, no `Result` in any `T` position — `gg check` OK, C build fails `unknown type name`). At T-E0a's landing: flip this fixture to a NEG for its pin-6 face, commit the REPLACEMENT repro for the surviving C-emit gap, and REPHRASE this entry (convergence-neutral rephrase, not a close — closing it ships a known defect, Core #8).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — ACCEPT/REJECT ASYMMETRY between two argument positions, `gg check`-visible; found 2026-07-27 auditing the Family-1 auto-propagate interaction; PRE-EXISTING, identical at base `6cf79126` and after] Result→T auto-propagation is admitted at a FREE-call argument but REJECTED at a METHOD-call argument.** Same expression, same declared parameter type: on a `(Result[int,int], int)` tuple, `take_f(&t.0)` with `void take_f(int &x)` is `gg check`-CLEAN and runs, while `r.take_m(&t.0)` with the identical `void take_m(&self, int &x)` fails `error[E_TypeMismatch]: type mismatch: expected 'int', found 'Result[int, int]'`. **CHECKED AGAINST THE DESIGN RECORD BEFORE FILING (Core #15e Q1 — an accept/reject asymmetry may be two ratified positions rather than a bug): it is a gap against INTENT.** `DONE.md` 2026-05-15 (Snag #49) records auto-propagation centralized producer-side at the `lower_expr` exit and **"gated to `Call` / `MethodCall` expressions ONLY"** — method calls named explicitly in scope — and Snag #43's note generalizes it to "every value-consuming site … that crosses a Result-to-T boundary in a `throws E` context". ⚠ **The asymmetry is on the CHECK side, not in lowering:** the free-call arm admits the unwrap when typing the argument, the method-call arm does not, so it never reaches lowering — where `methods.rs` DOES set `expected_type` and would have handled it. **Fix belongs in the typechecker, making the two argument positions agree**; do not teach the lowering to compensate for a check-side refusal. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_autoprop_method_arg_rejected.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_autoprop_method_arg_rejected` (both positions in one program), asserting the INTENDED symmetric acceptance. ⚡ **RULING REVERSED (D45 pin 6, owner 2026-08-10): the positions agree by BOTH REJECTING.** Bare `Result` in a `T` position is a type error at every call kind — the method-call REJECT is the ratified behavior; the free-call ACCEPT is the bug. This fixture's "INTENDED symmetric acceptance" header is OVERTURNED; it flips to a NEGATIVE (full ruling on the visible-flow entry, CoW/High). Rides round E0 (D45).

CLOSE the double-eval while there: the SH compound arm READs via `lower_expr(target_expr)` AND writes via `emit_field_write_from_local(*base_box,…)` = double-eval of the base `.get()`; the write-through-`Ptr(elem)` fix (base evaluated ONCE) closes it. Fold both into this campaign's SH-parity work.**

- **🆕🐛 [D6 — TRACK 2: refcount PARAM / boundary ownership model, filed by the stage-1b wrong-code TRACK-1 executor 2026-07-19. Needs its OWN scout+prototype — has an unresolved DESIGN tension, do NOT band-aid.] The Track-1 fix made refcount handles (Shared/Weak/Channel) clone-if-live at CONSUMING positions (ctor/literal/push/put — landed, both consuming clones verified ASan-clean). The PARAM/boundary side is untouched and has a sigil-aware design gap:** `needs_param_drop` (`src/ir/types.rs:573`) keys on TYPE only (`Trivial && drop_strategy!=None && clone_fn.is_some()`) and CANNOT distinguish bare `Shared s` (borrow — register_param must NOT drop; the return-boundary must incref) from `Shared !s` (owned — must drop). Picking either side without a SIGIL-AWARE redesign yields a refcount double-count OR the plain-call UAF. **THREE repros (all MEASURED @HEAD 596a3e16 = PRE-EXISTING, my Track-1 change did NOT alter them):** (a) **plain-call param UAF** — `void takes(Shared[int] x): print(x.strong_count())` then `takes(s); s.strong_count()` → heap-use-after-free in `gorget_shared_strong_count` (the bare param is a borrow but `needs_param_drop(Shared)=true` so `takes` decrefs it at scope exit → `s` freed under the caller). (b) **passthrough-return** — `Shared[int] passthrough(Shared[int] s): return s`, caller keeps `s` → UAF in `gorget_shared_drop` (the return boundary — `ensure_owned_at_boundary` Case 2, `context.rs`, UNWIDENED for refcount — does not incref). (c) **Shared-captured-by-closure** — a `Callable` capturing a live Shared routes EXCLUSIVELY through `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`closures.rs`), same boundary fn; its baseline was ASan-clean in the Track-1 matrix but is the same family. Plus the **`!Shared` param** enumeration: `is_mut_ref_param`/`is_ref_param` are both false for a non-resource Move type, so a `!Shared` param reaches NEITHER param path today — the model map must cover it. **needs_param_drop UNIFICATION note:** it is the sibling of the Track-1 consuming accessor `is_refcount_clone_type` but is NOT interchangeable (the third `drop_strategy!=None` clause excludes Channel, which is `DropStrategy::None`); the sigil redesign is where the two converge. Fix = a sigil-aware param ownership model (borrow vs owned `!`) driving register_param drop + the return/capture boundary incref, coherent across all three families. Own scout→brief→gauntlet; Track 1 deliberately did NOT touch `needs_param_drop` or `ensure_owned_at_boundary`.

### Medium

- **🆕 ROUND-8 FOLLOW-UP (filed 2026-06-28, from the get_or ownership executor `ac9980be`):**
  - **🐛 TEMPORARY resource default to `Dict.get_or`/`get_or_put` leaks on a HIT (pre-existing, CoW move-elision vs borrow-wrapper interaction).** `dh.get_or("k", Holder(x))` — passing a TEMP resource default (not a named local) — leaks on the HIT path (the existing value is returned, the temp default is unused). Confirmed PRE-EXISTING on the baseline driver (String temp default, 9-byte leak), INDEPENDENT of the `e81233ac` clone fix (which clones for the borrowing wrapper). Root: the move-eligible temp is marked moved-into-the-call (CoW last-use), but the get_or wrapper takes the default BY BORROW + only clones it on miss → on a hit the temp is never consumed/dropped. The `e81233ac` fixtures use NAMED-LOCAL defaults (the brief's shape), so this is unexercised by them. Fix needs the call-site move/borrow decision reconciled with the borrowing-wrapper ABI (drop the temp at the call, or pass-by-value-and-let-wrapper-drop). Check both compilers. Own scout.

Fix = `"get_or" or "get_or_put"`. But R1 alone turns the CC-FAIL into an executing double-free (R2).
    - **R2 — the REAL write-site (Core #1) + the WIDER Core #8 bug:** NOT `gorget_map_put` move-vs-clone (the C1 review's "Rust leaks" claim is STALE — Rust is CLEAN for String: `copy_cow` into the result slot + a separate map clone, `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:2318`). The self-host bug = the wrapper RETURNS the caller's BORROWED `__d` (`lir_codegen.gg:915` get_or_put / `:919` get_or); the self-host passes the default by BORROW (fn-call default-borrow, no consuming clone unlike Rust's inline LIR) → frees both the original default AND the returned result → DOUBLE-FREE (ASan-confirmed for String, get_or + get_or_put). **The WIDER Core #8 finding:** for NON-String resource values (`Dict[String, Vector[int]]`) **Rust gg ITSELF double-frees on BOTH C AND LLVM** (its special-case only covers `val_is_str` `insts.rs:3171`; a non-String aggregate gets a shallow memcpy, not a deep clone) — AND so does self-host. Latent (no corpus fixture exercises the owned-live-default resource shape under ASan), but the textbook "both backends agree on the wrong answer."
    - **Reference-grade fix (DEEP, both-compiler):** clone EACH owned output of the wrapper, leaving the borrowed `__d` to the caller, using a TYPED resource-clone (not String-only). Self-host: `dict_get_or_wrapper_for` (`lir_codegen.gg:915/919`) via the existing `lc_collection_clone_fn` (`lir_codegen.gg:1653`, resolves any value type → `*_clone_inplace`), gated on resource-ness (trivials keep move-and-return-same → int fixture stays green).   - **🐛 bare `Ok(..)`/`Error(..)` unconstrained combinator chains = Core #8 BOTH-COMPILER miscompile + a LANGUAGE-DESIGN fork (MIRAGE for parity; PHASE-2-aligned). SCOUTED `a32e021d` 2026-06-28.** ⚡ **THE FORK IS DECIDED — NOT AN OWNER CALL (pass-7 catch, 2026-08-10): D6 (ratified 2026-07-05) ruled REJECT with annotation fix-it, resolving error-model §9 Q17 for spec-v1.** What remains below is IMPLEMENTATION of the ratified rejection (both compilers + negative fixtures), not a reject-vs-default decision; read the fork prose below as historical derivation only. A truly-bare `Ok(5).map(c).unwrap_or(0)` with NO sibling `Result[T,E]` in scope MISCOMPILES IN BOTH compilers: Rust gg link-fails (C) / returns garbage (throws) / prints wrong (interpreter), `gg check` ACCEPTS it; self-host CC-FAILs (`GorgetArray = int`, the Some-twin). Rust only compiles it correctly when a sibling Result constrains the unbound `E` via its UNIFICATION ENGINE (`typecheck.rs:4472-4490` fresh_type_var; `ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:1533/1588` `resolve_option_result_variant` returns None when neither expected_type nor throws-result is a Result → falls through to a plain i64 `@Ok` = the miscompile). **MIRAGE for parity (+0):** only `test_result_chaining.gg` is blocked corpus-wide, and it sits BEHIND a 2nd independent blocker (the Result-producing closure-return-type bug, separately filed below / `result_map`). Everything else in the Result cluster already MATCHES (typed `Result[T,E]` locals supply `expected_type`). **The real finding = a language-design decision (own scout→brief targeting the RUST TYPECHECK FIRST, then mirror self-host):** what should fully-unconstrained `Ok(5).map(...)` DO — (a) REJECT with `gg check` "cannot infer error type for `Ok`; annotate the Result" (the Rust-grade, Core-#8-correct outcome + negative fixture), (b) default the unobserved type param consistently in BOTH typecheck+lowerers (a silent guess — diverges if E later unifies to a resource), or (c) status quo (silent-miscompile/CC-FAIL — NOT acceptable). The self-host arm CANNOT be designed in isolation (mirror whatever Rust is fixed to do); do NOT ship a self-host default-E guess Rust doesn't make ("redesign around a compiler gap" in reverse). Enforcement-flavored (needs the unbound-E unification verdict, NOT a simple FP-enumerable single-node check) → **PHASE-2-aligned (the deferred general enforcement pass) OR an explicit owner reject-vs-default call.** Self-host write site for the eventual mirror: `lower_expr.gg:6420-6431` (the `Some` arm to mirror), reads `ctx.expected_type` (carries `Result__T__E` when present) + would need `fn_is_throws` (`lower.gg:418`) widened to carry the throws Result T/E. **⭐ FOLDED INTO THE ERROR-MODEL DESIGN (owner 2026-06-28): the reject-vs-default-vs-existential decision is now `the error-model design notes (git history)` §9 Q17 — decide it when the error model is revisited, NOT as a standalone fix.** Cross-language survey DONE (in §9 Q17): camp (a) REJECT = Rust/Haskell/Swift-explicit-Result (recommended, fits Gorget's monomorphized `Result[T,E]`); (b) DEFAULT-to-universal-existential = Swift `any Error`/Go `error`/Zig `anyerror` (needs an existential Gorget lacks — a deliberate feature, NOT a bug fix; nobody defaults `E` to a concrete `Unit`/`Never`); (c) INFER-from-body = Zig error sets (different error model). Enforcement-flavored → PHASE-2-aligned; Rust-typecheck-first then mirror self-host.

Post-fix: `int a = None()` REJECTS, `take_int(None())` REJECTS, `Some(3).map((int x): None())` REJECTS, `Result[int,int].map((int x): None())` REJECTS, `None(1)` REJECTS with `E_WrongArgCount`. Residual class members: `.filter` (Option) and `.unwrap_or_else` (Option, int dest) still silently accept a bare-`None()` closure body. Root: the closure-taking-method fallback at `src/semantic/typecheck.rs:8229-8244` returns `receiver_type` / `elem_type()` without invoking `extract_fn_return_type` against the closure signature — a distinct typechecker gap in the closure-return-type wiring for these two methods, UNRELATED to the bare-`None()` callee class. - **🚨💥 [CRITICAL — SEVERITY CORRECTED 2026-08-17 from MED: this is a DOUBLE FREE, not a leak. Filed by the stage-1b TRACK-1 executor 2026-07-19; re-measured by the Track-E brief-review pass 3, orchestrator-reproduced] `Set[String]` uses the byte-based `gorget_ordered_set_new` variant, not `_new_str`** (`lower_set_literal_from_array`, `collections.rs:223` never selects the str-keyed ctor). So a String-keyed set has `key_drop=NULL` + no `key_materialize` → it cannot own a HEAP String key and dedups by BYTES not content. ⚠ **THE ENTRY PREVIOUSLY SAID "leaks it" AND "benign only for static-literal elements". BOTH UNDERSTATE IT.** With HEAP elements, READ-ONLY iteration twice DOUBLE FREES: `gg check` "OK: no semantic errors", builds rc 0 on C **and** `--backend=llvm`, both run **SIGABRT (rc 134)**. No assignment anywhere, so this is a THIRD memory-safety mechanism distinct from the for-in element rebind and from `unwrap()`-on-a-borrowed-payload. ⚠⚠ **THE CELL IS ACCIDENTALLY CORRECT AT ONE LOOP** — measured: literal elements + two loops → rc 0 (false negative); heap elements + **ONE** loop → rc 0; heap elements + **TWO** loops → rc 134. That is why it was twice reported as not crashing, and why any fixture here needs BOTH heap elements AND two reads (Core #15e Q6). Durable repro `tests/fixtures/security/attack_101_set_string_heap_elem_double_free.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `security_set_string_heap_elem_double_free`, RED-verified 2026-08-17. Severity now follows the crash; the leak and the dedup difference are real, are the same root (one missing `_str` ctor selection), and are fixed by the same change. This BLOCKS the Defect-A set-literal clone-if-live fix: routing a live String element through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` produces a heap clone that the byte-variant set then leaks — so the set picker fix was REVERTED (the live-source `Set[String] s = {x, ..}` shape stays a pre-existing "shallow copy of resource" validator panic). Fix (own track, has set-dedup SEMANTIC implications — byte→content): detect a String/Hashable-resource element type in the set literal and select `gorget_set_new_str`/`gorget_ordered_set_new_str`, THEN re-apply the clone-if-live picker (thread the element AST expr into `insert_elem`, route through `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` — the diff is in `/tmp/sharedmove_exec_M5.patch`) and add a live-source `Set[String]` fixture. Tuple literals were audited and already clone-if-live correctly (no work).

- **[MED — under-materialize; CoW 2G step-4 gap, deferred by design] A user `&self`-mutator receiver in a loop is not caught by the pre-header prescan.** The prescan is an UNTYPED AST walk; it can't resolve that a user method NAME (`b.compact()`, first param MutPtr) mutates its receiver, so the loop-carried materialize doesn't fire → per-iteration throwaway (the in-body materialize DOES fire via the typed `fn_sigs` lookup in `needs_mut`, `exprs/methods.rs:~1846`, but is reverted each iteration). The `MUTATING_METHODS` fallback catches user methods named like a builtin mutator (push/pop/…); arbitrarily-named ones (compact/bump) stay uncovered. Fix = a conservative name-based over-approx (any known `&self`-mutator method NAME marks its receiver mutated) built from the STABLE `fn_param_ownerships` pre-pass — DEFERRED: clone-balloon risk program-wide (the memory gate must re-confirm) + misses generic-instance sigs registered DURING lowering (the ALREADY-FILED R38 generic-equip residual, ~line 353 — cite, don't re-file). - **[HIGH — self-host latent FOOTGUN; from T-B] `apply_collect_target_rewrites` (`lower.gg:2665`) RESETS equip-method `self` param `.ownership` to bare (0) in `m.items`** — the AST reconstruction silently drops the `&self`/`!self` ownership axis on the reconstructed param, so `mi_meth.params.get(0).ownership` is UNRELIABLE for the self-convention (T-B works around it by reading the typed `fn_borrow_params[key][0]` populated by the earlier pre-pass). Any future pass that trusts `m.items` equip-method param ownership will silently mis-classify self. Fix = preserve the param ownership through the reconstruction (or re-derive it from the typed maps at the reconstruction site). Latent beyond T-B's scope.
- **[MED — self-host, Rust; T-B residual, Core #8] GENERIC-equip `&self` mutator on a bare-value-param named receiver still WRITES THROUGH.** `compute_method_mutates_self` classifies NON-generic equips only (`type_params.len()==0`), so `Cell__assign` (a generic-equip method) is absent from `method_mutates_self`; the named-receiver gate returns false → self-host `Y/Y` vs Rust `Y/A`. DEGRADE-TO-BASE (no NEW miscompile — the pre-R38 self-host wrote through for ALL named user-receiver mutators; T-B closed the non-generic sub-case; `cow_materialize_projected_root` is itself double-gated on a bare-value root so a false read-only cannot corrupt). Executable breadcrumb: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/generic_equip_mutator_named_recv.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `cow_named_recv_generic_equip_gap` integration test (asserts the intended Y/A; kept in a subdir so it stays OUT of the runtime_diff corpus). Fix = classify generic-equip methods too, mirroring the `fn_sigs` generic-instances pre-pass, then RE-MEASURE RSS (more-mutating → re-bomb risk — the double-gate limits it to bare-param roots, but confirm).
- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual, Core #8] name-collision on an UNRESOLVABLE projection stays read-only (both the SCAN and the GATE tail).** The user→builtin order closes the name-collision for a RESOLVABLE receiver (named, or a projection whose root local + field/element types resolve — verified: `v[i].get()` / `s.v[i].get()` materialize). Two unresolvable tails remain, both DEGRADE-TO-BASE (write-through, no bomb): (a) SCAN — in `mutinf_scan_expr`, a self-projection whose type resolves to −1 at scan time (`self.field.get()` where `self.field`→−1) falls to `builtin(get)=Some(false)`; (b) GATE — in `mutinf_recv_type_name`, a projected receiver whose base is NOT a named-root chain (e.g. an element of a METHOD-returned collection `f().v[0].get()`, or a root local absent from `ctx.locals`) resolves to "" → the guard returns false. Fix = a fuller projection-type oracle (or route the scan/gate through the typechecker's per-link `expr_link_types` where present), re-validating RSS since a conservative "unresolved ⇒ mutating" fallback here is the re-bomb the whole pass avoids.
- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual] self reborrowed into a local then mutated is not traced by the scan.** `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method — the scan does not recognise `r` as self-rooted (`mutinf_expr_is_self_rooted("r")` is false), so `r.set_name` is not attributed to self. VERIFIED HARMLESS in the self-host's CURRENT model: a bare `Holder r = self` bind COPIES (r is an independent value — `r.set_name` mutates the copy, not self), so the enclosing method is GENUINELY non-self-mutating → read-only classification is CORRECT, no divergence. The residual only bites IF a future `&`-reborrow-into-local (`auto v = &self.field; v.push(..)`, an aliasing bind that writes through) lands (the `&`-bind write-through itself landed round-38 — T-D flipped `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` to MATCH; its scalar-field/element sub-case is the deferred-capability residual filed below); then this path must default to MUTATING (belt-and-braces: the EMutableBorrow arm already flags a direct `&self`/`&self.field` formation as mutating). ⚠ SEPARATE DISCOVERED BUG (Rust, PRE-EXISTING, orthogonal to T-B): `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method makes **Rust gg PANIC** (`src/main.rs:2434` "gg main thread panicked"); the self-host compiles it and prints the read-only-correct result. Own scout (Rust `&self`→owned-local bind path).
- **[LOW — self-host; T-D adv(c) deferred capability, NOT a regression] scalar/struct-field & element `&`-bind write-through is VALUE-COPIED, not aliased.** T-D's `auto r = &b` / `&b.field` write-through fix (landed, `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` MATCH) GATES its bare branch on `inner == EIdentifier`, so a projection of a SCALAR/non-resource field or element (`auto r = &b.scalar_field` / `&b[scalar_i]`) routes to the SAFE value-bind fall-through (a copy) rather than a write-through mut-ptr. This is a DEFERRED CAPABILITY, not a regression (pre-fix it also cloned) — zero corpus occurrences today. Implementing the Ptr write-through for scalar-field/element `&`-binds (resolve the field/element slot address for value-typed fields) is the follow-up. Reference: `lower_stmt.gg` SVarDecl EMutableBorrow intercept.

- **🧩 CoW MATERIALIZATION — durable reference (THE RULE + GROUND TRUTH).**
  - **THE RULE (owner-decided):** *Never implicitly clone except when absolutely necessary for safety.* Order **borrow > move > clone**. An implicit clone is legitimate ONLY when BOTH: (1) the destination must OWN (a real ownership boundary), AND (2) the source can be neither aliased nor moved (lives past this point, OR is a collection element / `.get()` result / view-method result / parameter). Any clone NOT forced by (1)∧(2) is a bug. Explicit-type bindings BORROW (identical to `auto`). For collection-element/view/param sources, ownership-taking is clone-NOT-move. (= the `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" contract.)
  - **GROUND TRUTH (re-cite before acting):** Enforcement = `validate_consume_sites` (`src/ir/validate.rs:2400`) runs UNCONDITIONALLY in `lower_module` (`mod.rs:1892`) and `panic!`s (`:1977`) on the first owning position receiving a borrow with no preceding clone/move ⇒ a MISSING materialization CRASHES, doesn't miscompile; ANY clone-removal must keep it GREEN. Taxonomy: `ImplicitCloneReason` (`src/ir/mod.rs:183`, 11 variants, 39 sites) via `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`context.rs:1752`), `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` (`context.rs:1923`), `emit_enum_init_owned` (`context.rs:1243`) + write-side `cow_before_mutation` (`context.rs:2658`) + a GorgetString-ONLY runtime materialize net. Var-decl tree `lower_var_decl_assign_mode` (`stmts/mod.rs:1081-1273`): A/C→Borrow, F/G→Move, **B/D/E→Clone** (B = cross-type live `Str→GorgetString`; D = CoW-unsafe live; E = `View`→owned String). Clean references: `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/book/11-ownership.md:282-300` + `language-reference.md:2321`/`:1624`.
  - **Branch B is DEAD CODE — not an over-clone (empirically confirmed agent aa18fc8b).** Branch B (`stmts/mod.rs:1170-1179`) fired 0 times across 1121 fixtures — guard `!is_resource_type(rhs) && clone_fn_for_ptr(rhs).is_some()` is the EMPTY SET (every type with a registered clone fn carries `copy_semantics=Resource`). FOLLOW-UP (evidence-first): delete with a tombstone (after confirming no `GG_BACKEND=llvm`/non-fixture path reaches it), OR find the uncovered shape. Do NOT "convert B to Copy".
  - **Branch E over-clone (`stmts/mod.rs:1235-1241`) — RE-VERIFY IT FIRES FIRST.** Flagged (eager clone of a `View`-source same-type `String b = view`) but never confirmed firing. Instrument E + build the corpus to confirm a non-zero count + real over-clone BEFORE any fix. Defer-to-mutation infra exists (`set_view_of`/`views_of_source`/`cow_before_mutation` Case 4); higher risk (past naive view/alias removals regressed 50+ fixtures). Evidence-first.

The ratified convergence path is the D2-rider dead-write warning ("writes to a private copy that is never read — did you mean `&o`?") extended to these method-rooted temps. Not this round's impl; file as a D2-rider follow-up. When it lands, PROMOTE `cow_2d_method_result_dead_write.gg` out of known_gaps → a top-level corpus fixture (ggdef-adjudicated) once the shapes are in-subset and self-host parity is confirmed (subset gap: slice/container-method/struct-view outside ggdef phase-0).

- **🧹 [MED — MaterializePlan campaign follow-up, filed 2026-07-28 (owner-suggested during Track I close)] Extend auto-move-at-last-use to POST-MATERIALIZE parameters.** Today docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md excludes parameters from last-use move ("not from a parameter — those bind borrows") because the parameter slot is a borrow view of caller-owned data. AFTER a §3.1 materialize fires on a bare-param root (`p` becomes locally Owned), the slot IS owned; the analysis doesn't pick that up and treats the post-materialize `p` as a borrow through end-of-frame. Consequence: in the recursive-materialize pathology (branch-per-frame, N=12→~250K clones), the FINAL bare-forwarded self-call in each frame would be a move-eligible last-use, saving one clone per node (half-win for branching; full-win for the terminal frame of linear). MaterializePlan campaign fit: read Phase-D `LocalOwnership` post-materialize; classify per-arg as OWN-post-mat + last-use → emit MoveWithoutOperator elision + IR `MoveZero`. Requires callee-ABI extension for bare-param callee optionally receives owned-in, or call-mode specialization at the call site. Owner-suggested during Track I close (2026-07-28); Track I chose to WARN not FIX, so this is the compiler-side complement (a fn the user writes to be `&`-forwarding runs the whole gauntlet; a fn the user leaves bare-recursive still gets fewer clones under this optimization). Measurement floor: with the compiler-side auto-move, the recursive linear case at large N should drop dramatically (one clone per final frame). Charter meter directly measurable: the `CoWMaterialization` reason volume in the Rust `array_clone`-stats readout on a self-host bootstrap should decrease.

- **ensure_owned_at_boundary migration — remaining specialized sites.** 5 remaining each have logic beyond pure boundary-clones (fresh-string elision, last-use move, MutPtr wrapping, pattern extraction, field_access checks). Struct + enum-variant init done.

- **🧹 [LOW — Track F sibling audit, filed 2026-07-28; latent perf shape, not a bug today] `lower_call_arg` `MutableBorrow` arm (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/calls.rs:55-82`) has the SAME UNCONDITIONAL `cow_before_mutation` code pattern that Track F just guarded on the `Ownership::Borrow` arm at `:103`.** The MutableBorrow arm (`f(&b)` for a write-through callee) calls `ctx.cow_before_mutation(builder, local_id, arg.span)` first, then only later checks `is_ref_local || is_param_borrow_unique || is_already_ptr`. ⚠ UNLIKE the Track F case, the clone here is arguably BY-DESIGN — CoW G2 site 1 materialize on a bare-alias / bare-param source so the callee's write-through lands on a PRIVATE copy, not the caller's slot (the arm's own header comment states exactly that). So this is NOT a Track F-style bug to fix, it is a POTENTIAL CLONE-BOMB SHAPE if a user program does recursive `f(&b)` where `b` is a bare resource param — each recursion level would materialize a fresh private copy (which is semantically correct per CoW, but arbitrarily expensive). Direction if a witness surfaces: consider a scoped memoize / borrow-check-driven up-front materialize so recursive `&b` shares one copy across levels, rather than the mechanical Track F guard restructure — which would BREAK G2 site 1's write-through semantics. ## Self-host parity

### High
- **🆕🏗 [HIGH — CLASS FIX (Core #4), SH lane; ARCHAEOLOGY 2026-08-05 via `32dbe09e`] `record_method_targs_preserving_expr_types` is wired at TWO of N expression positions; f-string interpolation is a missed sibling.** Call sites: `self_host_typechecker/typecheck.gg:4383` (SVarDecl) and `:4506` (SExpr). **There is no third.** DONE.md PARITY CHAIN #6 added the first, CHAIN #7 added the second and named the pattern in its own entry — *"Sibling-site drift: `caf102fd` wired ... into `type_check_stmt`'s SVarDecl arm ONLY"* — then stopped at two. A method call inside an f-string interpolation reaches neither arm, so the method-targs side-table stays empty and the lowerer emits a **called-but-never-defined** symbol; C infers `int` for the undeclared function and the assignment to the real struct type fails to compile. **That is the exact signature CHAIN #8 records** (*"body DCE-dropped → C implicit-int-return → `incompatible types … from int` CC-FAIL"*) and the exact error the 2026-08-05 battery hit on `stdlib_iter_terminals` when its local-binding workaround was removed. **FIX SHAPE — a ONE-PRODUCER CHOKEPOINT, not a third call site.** This file already uses that pattern for the D32 `!`/`&` operand-position rejects, whose comments enumerate the positions a walker must cover (*"binop, cond, match scrutinee, index, augassign RHS, f-string interp, `as`-cast, comprehension body, ..."*). Route method-targ recording through one producer covering that same set; a third arm guarantees a fourth gap. Ships with an arm-count or chokepoint-invocation guard (Core #6). ⚠ **SECOND, SEPARATE defect found in the same area — do not conflate:** `infer.gg:460`'s `is_terminal_method` is a hardcoded six-name set (`fold`/`any`/`all`/`find`/`find_index`/`for_each`) that OMITS `count`/`sum`, while `:554` encodes overlapping knowledge (`len`/`count` → `int_id`) on another path. Its own comment says a typed discriminator should replace it ("1b-iii ... the typed closure-bearing-adapter discriminator"; `bound_has_closure` already reads real `RTFunction` variants for ADAPTERS). Whether this contributes to the CC-FAIL is UNTESTED. **DISCRIMINATOR RUN 2026-08-05 — RESULT: THE RECORDER, NOT THE NAME SET.** Inlining ONLY `any`/`all`/`fold` — all three PRESENT in `is_terminal_method` — while leaving `count`/`sum` as locals still CC-FAILs, with the identical error. So the name set is NOT implicated in this failure and the missing recorder is confirmed as the cause. ⚠ Sharper still: the failing assignment target is `__gg_VectorIter__int64_t` — the ITERATOR's own type, not a terminal's result — so it is **`v.iter()` itself** that is emitted called-but-never-defined. The gap is therefore not terminal-specific: ANY method chain inside an f-string interpolation that needs method-targ recording should fail this way, which is worth measuring against the non-MATCH set's `X from type 'int'` CC-FAIL family before scoping the fix (that family was ~85 strong at round-32; REGENERATE, never quote it). The `is_terminal_method` name-set defect below stands as an INDEPENDENT cleanup, not a cause. Chain context: the `#39` plan was *demand-gate relax → adapter-struct mono → 1b-iii closure-adapter emit*; the first two landed (CHAIN #8, TASK #39), and "1b-iii" appears in no ledger entry. ⛔ **FIX SHAPE IS UNPROVEN — DO NOT BRIEF IT WITHOUT A SCOUT (established 2026-08-05 by two brief-review passes).** Three findings, each fatal to the obvious fix: **(1) THE CAUSAL CHAIN IS BROKEN.** The missing arm is real — `EFString` is absent from BOTH `record_method_targs_in_expr` (`typecheck.gg:3944-3976`) and its duplicated gate `expr_has_method_call` (`:3989-4016`), which between them cover **6 of 45 `Expr` variants** — but adding it would NOT fix the headline fixture. The undefined symbol is `__gg_VectorIter__int64_t`, i.e. `v.iter()` ITSELF, and `.iter()` is neither in `infer.gg:460`'s terminal set nor closure-bearing, so the `(is_terminal_method or bound_has_closure) and generic_params.len() > 0` gate at `infer.gg:482` is FALSE for it — no targ entry is produced either way. Skipping the walk is real; that it CAUSES this CC-FAIL is unestablished (Core #15e Q6). **(2) THE CITED CONSTRAINT IS STALE.** `typecheck.gg:3971-3976` blames "the parser's synthetic f-string span offsets"; `:3718-3720` states that collision is FIXED (the sub-parser threads the shared counter). The live reasons are `:3721-3735`. **(3) THE REAL HAZARD IS `expr_link_types`.** `record_method_targs_preserving_expr_types` snapshots/restores only `types.expr_types` (`:4020-4022`); `expr_link_types` is written by `infer_expr_type` (`infer.gg:551`, `:607`), keyed on the same `span.end`, read FIRST for every `EMethodCall` (`lower_types.gg:559-563`), and has NO name cross-check — so a widened walk persists entries that mis-type rather than merely miss. Also: routing through `walk_expr_closures` (the intuitive one-producer site) reintroduces two RUN-confirmed regressions the snapshot exists to prevent (`typecheck.gg:3921-3934`). **Scout must establish the causal chain BEFORE any fix is briefed.** Ready-made guard when it is: `tests/lints.rs:5247 self_host_safety_walker_is_exhaustive` already derives the full `Expr` set from `ast.gg` and asserts an arm per variant.

- **🆕🐛 [MED-HIGH — Core #9 LANE GAP, measured 2026-08-05 by the round-close battery] The SELF-HOST mis-lowers an iterator terminal called INLINE inside an f-string interpolation; Rust gg compiles the same shape fine.** `print(f"count={v.iter().count()}")` and siblings (`any`/`all`/`sum`/`fold`) make the self-host emit C that will not compile: *"incompatible types when assigning to type `__gg_VectorIter__int64_t` from type `int`"* — the iterator's own type is used for the terminal's `int` result, so the receiver type leaks into the result slot. Rust gg handles it (`tests/fixtures/fstring_method_chain.gg` pins the shape green on the C lane). **Discovered by REMOVING a workaround and watching `self_host_runtime` fail**: `tests/fixtures/stdlib_iter_terminals.gg` binds these five terminals to locals first, and the comment justifying it cited a Rust-side f-string re-parse that is FALSE at HEAD — the comment was wrong about the mechanism while the dodge was still load-bearing for the other lane. The binding is restored with the true reason recorded in-fixture. **Not yet discriminated:** which of the five terminals break (all were inlined at once) and whether a non-terminal adapter chain (`.map().collect()`) inside interpolation breaks too — narrow before fixing. Re-inline the fixture when the self-host handles it; that fixture is the regression net. **ROOT CAUSE: see the entry above — its DISCRIMINATOR RUN falsified the `count`/`sum` name-set hypothesis (inlining only `any`/`all`/`fold`, all three PRESENT in that set, still CC-FAILs), and the recorder chain is itself now unproven. SUPERSEDED HYPOTHESIS (kept only so it is not re-proposed) as the phase-1b-iii CLASS FIX above** — `count`/`sum` are absent from `infer.gg:460`'s hardcoded terminal-method name set. Fix the class, not this symptom.

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — silent WRONG output, SELF-HOST LANE ONLY; filed 2026-07-29 by Round XIV ride-along scout] A top-level `static` whose initializer is a struct constructor with a NESTED struct constructor argument reads `0` from every field on the self-host lane, BEFORE any write. Rust gg (both backends) reads the initialiser correctly.** RED-VERIFIED 2026-07-29 at HEAD (`21214388`): Rust C stdout `42` exit 0, Rust LLVM likewise, SH driver→cc→run stdout `0` exit 0 (silent wrong output, not a crash). Discriminator is the NESTED CTOR ARGUMENT: flat statics (`static int G = 42`) work on both lanes; single-level struct ctors with primitive args (`static Point G = Point(3, 4)` or `static Outer G = Outer(42)`) work on both lanes; the outer field being itself a struct-typed nested ctor (`static Outer G = Outer(Inner(42))`) is the failing cell. Likely a sibling of the Rust-lane α cluster filed near TODO L326 (`S-C: extend self-host static-init synthesis to enum-variant + non-literal-arg struct ctors`, via `build_static_init_fn` `lower_types.gg:1622` + caller-side widening in `lower_static_ref_ident` + materialize-global-into-MutPtr) — landed Rust-side (`743412da`/`6ac72607`); the SH `IStaticDecl` (`self_host_lowerer/lower.gg:3182`) still only routes literal array/dict initialisers, so a nested-ctor initialiser falls through to a zeroed global. - **🐛 SELF-HOST MISCOMPILES in-place `dict.get(k).unwrap().push(v)` → SIGSEGV stage-1 binary, CONTEXT-DEPENDENT** (discovered 2026-06-28 by the `implements`-predicate scout). Mutating a `Vector` obtained from a `Dict.get(k).unwrap()` IN PLACE and relying on the dict seeing the mutation (the in-place idiom `gmod.trait_impls_by_type.get(k).unwrap().push(v)`) mis-compiles into a faulting stage-1 binary specifically in the `lower_module` pre-pass context (`tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`, the trait-impl-registry build loop). The SAME idiom compiles fine elsewhere in the self-host (`lir_ssa`/`scope`/`lir_codegen`), so it is context-dependent — likely an alias/temp-lifetime interaction between the `Dict.get().unwrap()` view and an in-place `.push()` reallocation in this particular function/scope shape. The `implements` pre-pass DODGES it via rebuild-then-put (`get → owned local → push → put(k, !local)`); the miscompile itself is UNFIXED. Repro: revert the rebuild-then-put in `lower_module`'s `trait_impls_by_type` loop to the in-place idiom and run `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` → stage-1 SIGSEGV. Proper fix lives in the self-host lowerer's view-vs-owned / `.get().unwrap()` aliasing path (mirror Rust's treatment); until then keep the rebuild-then-put shape at this site.

- **🐛 SELF-HOST MISLOWERS `None()` (with parens) → mistyped `Option[int]` temp** (#14 P0). The PARSER accepts both `None()` and bare `None` (`self_host_parser/parser.gg:1478`), but the LOWERER lowers a bare-variant CALL `None()` as a constructor defaulting to `Option[int]` regardless of context → mistyped dead temp → stage-1 cc fails (`incompatible types … int64_t from Option__int64_t`). Bare `None` lowers correctly (type flows from context). Rust gg tolerates `None()` → `fixed_point`-only. PROPER FIX = infer `None()`'s Option type from the consuming position (route through the same context-typed None path); OR (lesser) reject `None()` at check time. Likely the same root as `project_selfhost_none_typing_bug`.

- **SELF-HOST lexer bug (NOT a Rust bug): string literal inside an f-string interpolation truncates the enclosing `match`.** A nested string literal in an f-string interpolation (`f"... {f(\"add\", x)} ..."`) is mis-lexed: the `\"` inside `{...}` ends the f-string early → everything after that `case` arm is silently dropped (nasty: no parse error at the site, a downstream non-exhaustive-match error one function over + a phantom extern). The Rust compiler does NOT have this (`src/lexer/mod.rs:691-694` calls `skip_quoted_string` which skips `\\` escapes). Fix the self-host interpolation-segment scanner (`tests/fixtures/self_host_*/lex.gg`) to mirror `skip_quoted_string`. Workaround: bind the inner call to a `String` local first.

#### [DEEP track — NOT single-zone] Full loop-else (for-else all paths + while-else)
Loop-else scout (round-8) proved the full feature is NOT bounded to lower_loops.gg. Ship the zone-clean `lower_for_iterator` slice first (+1 `iterable`); the REST is a dedicated serialized broad track:
- **for-else** across `lower_for_range`(:701)/`lower_for_vector`(:224)/`lower_for_dict`(:491)/`lower_for_string`(:325) — mechanism proven (copy `lower_for_set` lower_loops.gg:606-688: `else_bb=exit_bb; if else_body.len()>0: else_bb=new_block`, header-false/`next()→None` edge → else_bb, break pushes exit_bb so it skips else, emit else tail → jump exit). Centralize via a shared `emit_loop_else_tail(...)` helper. DELETE the dispatcher `lower_fail` guards at lower_loops.gg:176-177 (vector) + :188-189 (dict) as each path gains real handling. SPILLS into `lower_expr.gg` (12 comprehension call-sites: 3×range, 7×vector, 1×dict, 1×string need an empty-`[]` else arg).
- **while-else** = DEEP cross-file AST-arity change: the gap is at the PARSER (`parser.gg:2962-2965` parses then DISCARDS the while-else block), and `SWhile(SpannedExpr, Vector[Stmt])` (`ast.gg:149`) has NO else field. Needs: ast.gg new field + parser bind/emit + ~14 `case SWhile(...)` match sites (meta.gg×4, lower_generics.gg×8, lower_closures.gg×4, lower_cow.gg, resolve.gg×2, typecheck.gg, format.gg, loader.gg×4, lower.gg×2). This is the load-bearing blocker for `for_else` + `break_nested`.
- Rust ref: `src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs` `alloc_for_blocks`(:33-44)+`emit_else_arm_tail`(:68-81); `mod.rs:2290-2343` (while dual-target). Docs: `docs/language-reference.md:1276` (else runs on natural exhaustion, break skips it).
- Yield when COMPLETE: `iterable`(+via slice) + `for_else` + `break_nested` = +3 total. Bootstrap-safe (self-host source has zero loop-else).

#### [follow-up, from none_literal review] map_ast_type registration side-effect is a latent footgun
`map_ast_type` calls `lookup_or_register_named` (lower_types.gg:~645) — a REGISTRATION SIDE EFFECT, so it is NOT a pure type-query. The fn_param_sigs pre-pass guard `not fsdef.is_extern_stub` (lower.gg:3550) LOCALLY contains it for stub PARAMS, but the unguarded return-path registration (`map_fn_return_type`→`map_ast_type`, lower.gg:3555) would re-trip it for a future `extern Guard[bool] make()` (a stub whose RETURN is a named resource). Principled fix: split a side-effect-free pure type-query path from the register-on-demand path, OR make the pre-pass use the query-only variant. Not urgent (no current fixture trips the return path), but the footgun is real.

#### [defensive, from Increment-2 review] is_move guard on ByMutRef reclassify
The ByMutRef reclassify (lower_expr.gg:~3186) emits a `MutPtr(&outer_slot)` env field. Escape-unsafe by design (like Rust closures.rs:131) — safe ONLY because Rust gg rejects escaping mutating closures at borrow-check + the parity harness gates on the Rust oracle, so such programs are excluded. The self-host driver OMITS the borrow-check diagnostic (pre-existing, separately-filed gap), so for a Rust-REJECTED escaping mutating closure it would emit dangling-pointer C (vs stub garbage pre-Increment-2). Unreachable from valid Gorget, but defensive hardening = add an `is_move`/escape guard at the reclassify mirroring `closures.rs:102` (only ByMutRef a NON-escaping closure), OR wire the self-host borrow-check diagnostic. Belongs to the borrow-check-diagnostic-gap track.

#### 🆕 DISCOVERED DEFECTS (R5/R6 parity wave, 2026-06-27 — filed, parity-neutral/latent)
- **🐛 ROUND-31 FOLLOW-UP (Core #8) — SELF-HOST semantic-reject port (scope WIDENED round-32).** Round-31 (`79c3ee30`) made the RUST compiler REJECT unknown methods on primitives; the SELF-HOST is a lowerer with NO semantic-reject mechanism (`guess_return_type` name-match; unknown→`I64_TYPE` default, `lower_types.gg`/`lower_closures.gg:838`) → it still SILENTLY accepts what Rust now rejects. The port's reject list has GROWN to three classes, all landed Rust-side in rounds 31-32: (1) unknown primitive methods (round-31); (2) non-int/non-String positional 1-arg `String(x)` ctor args (round-32 `0dc391c6` — self-host currently identity-coerces, which incidentally matches the future cast-via-construction RFC, but the DIVERGENCE stands until the port); (3) definite-return / noreturn-divergence (round-32 `f7c828f3` — self-host accepts non-void fall-off). Port all three behind one mechanism (method/ctor/return-path existence checks against the typed tables). Own scout→brief.
- **🧹 ROUND-31 FOLLOW-UP (Core #1/#3, layering) — the `builtin_method_type` vs IR `GORGET_STRING_VIEW` two-list drift.** Round-31's reject uses `builtin_method_type` (`typecheck.rs:5777`, 50 String methods) as the method-existence oracle, and had to ADD `clone`/`upper`/`lower`/`slice`/`ord` to it because they were IR-protocol-only (`GORGET_STRING_VIEW.methods`, 25) — TWO parallel String-method lists that disagree (the protocol MISSES 31 valid methods `contains`/`split`/`replace`/etc.). The stronger single-oracle fix: have the reject (and return-type inference) consult ONE authoritative registry (the IR protocol, completed) instead of the hand-synced typecheck table. Reconcile the two lists / make one derive from the other. Own scout.
- **🐛 [MED — WIDENED TO THE CLASS 2026-08-11 (T-PRUNE gauntlet pass-2, orchestrator-arbitrated on both backends): float math-runtime symbols escape the C-backend's NAME-PREFIX include gate — ≥3 defects, one mechanism]** `f"{abs(x)}"` on a float still fails C-lane cc with `implicit declaration of 'gorget_fabs'` (measured 2026-08-11: C build exit 1, LLVM build+run exit 0 prints 3.500000 — a **cross-backend divergence**, not just a cc failure). ROOT CAUSE (No-name-matching violation, Layering rule 2): `src/lir/lower/insts.rs:616`/`:730` rewrite `gorget_abs`→`gorget_fabs` for float operands, but the RUNTIME_MATH include gate at `src/backend/c_lir/emit_types.rs:2363-2374` tests `starts_with("gorget_abs")` / `== "gorget_min"` — `gorget_fabs`/`gorget_fmin`/`gorget_fmax` match nothing, so the header is never emitted. **The class**: float `min`/`max` in the same position fail identically (`gorget_fmin`/`gorget_fmax`, probe-verified). **Second defect underneath (accept/reject asymmetry)**: outside an f-string the same call REJECTS (`E_TypeMismatch: expected int, found float` — `lib/std/math.gg:19` declares `int abs(int x)`); inside an f-string it is ACCEPTED and miscompiled — the f-string path bypasses the typecheck that the plain path applies, so the C failure is the SYMPTOM of an accept that should not happen (or, if float `abs` is intended, the plain path should accept too — owner/design call on which). Reference-grade fix: route the float rewrite through the typed runtime registry (`src/lir/runtime.rs` — `Fabs` exists at :256) so emit inclusion is registry-driven, not prefix-matched, AND reconcile the f-string vs plain accept surface. Was filed as a single-instance ROUND-31 entry; verified identical on base `9243ef44` then and still live at `a0bfe594f`.
- **🐛 [MED — REPHRASED 2026-08-11 by the T-PRUNE fold, orchestrator-verified: the filed ICE is GONE but the site is NOT clean] `s.debug()` on a plain struct with no Debug equip now passes `gg check` and FAILS AT LINK with a raw `undefined reference to S__debug` leaking to the user** (was: Tier 2a panic at `mod.rs:2084` — no panic reproduces on any receiver shape at HEAD). This is the check⇒build violation class A38-e guards (a C-toolchain error reaching the user); the reference-grade fix is a check-time reject ('S does not equip Debug') or a derived default — decided at the A38-e/T-E0b landing. #### [closure 4e-2 — ⚠ NO LONGER "LATENT": MEASURED LIVE, Core #8 silent-wrong-output] Option/Result combinator adapter SKIPS the payload's user `Drop` and LEAKS
**⚠ PREMISE CORRECTED 2026-07-25 by the Track-B2 scout — the "Rust handles it" claim below is REFUTED.**
Measured at HEAD, C backend: `Option[Money].map(...)` with `equip Money with Drop` prints the value but
**NEVER prints "dropping"** — the user `Drop` is silently skipped. That is **silent-wrong-output**
(Core #8), not merely a latent leak. LSan on a 2000-iteration `Option[Vector[int]].map` probe:
**176,000 B in 4,000 allocations**. It is combinator-SPECIFIC — the same program written
`if o is Some(v)` is clean. **D-2T also fails to reject a drop-tainted value at this materialize
position**, so the drop-purity gate does not cover it either. Reproduces at baseline AND under the B2
prototype, i.e. pre-existing and independent of the consume-position migration. Per Core #8 a
pre-existing known defect is NOT a licence to ship past it.
**DURABLE REPRO (committed 2026-07-25):** `tests/fixtures/security/sound_option_map_user_drop_leak.gg`
+ `security.rs::sound_option_map_user_drop_no_leak` (`#[ignore]`d, `security_safe_no_leak`, asserts
the INTENDED `Drop`-runs / no-leak state). RED-verified at HEAD: prints
`512/control-end/dropping/512/map-end` — the trailing `dropping` MISSING — plus
`LeakSanitizer: 8192 bytes in 2 allocations` (`Money__clone` via `Option__Money__clone`, and the
payload). The fixture carries the `if o is Some(v)` CONTROL LEG in the same program, which is clean,
so the map leg's silence is pinned as a divergence rather than a design choice.
⚠ AMBIGUITY recorded in-fixture: the expected stdout asserts ONE `dropping` per leg (the CoW-charter
answer — the combinator's implicit `Option__Money__clone` is itself excess implicit cloning). A fix
that keeps the clone and drops BOTH copies prints `dropping` twice and fails — the intended signal,
not a false red. Out of ggdef's phase-0 subset. **Un-ignore when the combinator adapter owns its
payload.**
**ORIGINAL FILING BELOW — its "LATENT / Rust handles it" framing is superseded:**
The (B) ownership/double-free tail of 4e is LATENT: the whole corpus returns only STATIC-LITERAL Strings (cap=0, .rodata) from map/map_err/unwrap_or_else closures → no heap double-free. It only surfaces with a HEAP String return (e.g. `.map((x): f"{x}")` or `.map((s): s.to_upper())`). Rust handles it (clone-receiver + Move-mode + scrut-zero, methods.rs:2924-2970/:3083-3109). To pursue: (1) ADD a heap-return combinator fixture, (2) port Rust's ownership adapter into the inline-C template/dispatch, (3) ASan-gate (a wrong fix trades the absence-of-crash for a silent double-free — worse). DEFERRED: no corpus fixture currently fails on it, so it's +0 parity until a fixture exists; do it when a heap-return combinator program is added or surfaces. The combinator sub-track (4a/4b/4c/4e-1/4d + R2-class) is otherwise COMPLETE.

#### [nit, non-blocking, from B output-review] `byte` alias on signed arm in resolve_field_gir_type
`resolve_field_gir_type` (lower_types.gg) keeps `byte` on the signed arm (`int8_t|i8|byte→I8_TYPE`), but Rust treats `byte` as a `uint8` alias (`src/lexer/token.rs:471`, `src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:2566`) and the sibling `prim_name_to_type` (lower_types.gg:56-58) correctly maps `byte→U8_TYPE`. Currently DEAD (field type names reach this fn in canonical `uint8_t` form, not surface `byte`), so not a regression — but tighten for sibling-site consistency: move `byte` to the unsigned `uint8_t|u8|byte→U8_TYPE` arm. Verify no fixture flips before/after (should be a no-op).

#### [micro-guard, from C output-review] range/string for-paths drop else_body SILENTLY
Unlike vector(:177)/dict(:189) which emit a `lower_fail` else-guard, `lower_for_range`(:103) and `lower_for_string`(:206) drop a loop-`else` body with NO marker — so a range/string for-else silently vanishes instead of failing loudly. PART OF the deep loop-else track (those paths get real else-handling there), but as a CHEAP interim ratchet, add a `lower_fail("for-range/string with else-body not yet implemented")` guard to both so the gap can't hide. Do this when picking up the deep track (or as a 2-line standalone).

#### [nit, from D output-review] stale docstring on decide_operand_at_consuming_arg
`lower.gg:~1808` docstring still says "Status: dead code in this commit. No caller exists." — FALSE: the fn is wired via `wire_one_operand` (lower_liveness.gg:970). Pre-existing, comment-only (no codegen impact). Correct the docstring to reflect it's the live CFG-aware wire-pass operand-mode decision. 1-line cleanup.

#### [nit, non-blocking, from throws-expr-body review] Defect-A peel is single-level
`lower_stmt.gg:545` peels `GtPtr`/`GtMutPtr` off `val`'s type ONCE (not recursive like `peel_ptr_tid`). Sufficient today (return-site operands are at most single-Ptr-wrapped Result; green 813-net), but a future borrow-of-borrow Result return would under-peel. If `peel_ptr_tid` is cheap to call there, prefer it for robustness. Inconsequential now.

#### [closure follow-up, from Increment-3 review] closure_value_ret_type channel follow-the-value gap
The Increment-3 `closure_value_ret_type` channel is populated only at the closure make-site + the SVarDecl-MoveDirect copy-forward. A closure value reaching a callable local via a NON-MoveDirect VarDecl branch (CoW/Branch-C), a reassignment (`f = (...)`), or store-in-collection-then-retrieve MISSES the channel → falls back to `expected_type` (same as pre-Increment-3, no regression — just unaddressed). The fn-ARG-then-called path IS handled (GtFnPtr param fires the FnPtr-recovery arm first). Extend the copy-forward to the other VarDecl branches + reassignment when a later increment needs it.

## ggdef / define-gorget

### High

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Core #9 lane divergence, oracle ACCEPTS 3 programs the language REJECTS; filed 2026-07-31 by Round XX pass 6, measured at HEAD] ggdef runs `non_printable_interpolation_error.gg`, `missing_return_error.gg`, `missing_return_no_return_error.gg` to a clean `Value` where `gg check` correctly rejects them.** These are NEGATIVE fixtures — invisible to `self_host_runtime_diff` adjudication by construction (adjudicated set is MATCH-side only). The missing-return pair (2 of 3) needs a **definite-return check** at ggdef elaborate time — a WRITE-site defect; a render-site raise would only cure the two fixtures, not the class. The `non_printable_interpolation_error` half rides the composite-print widening entry (`docs/language-reference.md` §2904's `E_NonPrintableInterpolation` — already exists in production; ggdef doesn't model it). File-first; pin each via a `spectests/run/` reject-outcome or a per-fixture ggdef driver test. Own small track.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — Core #4 sibling of Track M's fix; filed 2026-07-31 by Round XX output-review] The `E_ClosureKindMismatch` class has a CALL-ARG sibling that ggdef still accepts.** Production's `validate_closure_arg_kind` (`src/semantic/typecheck.rs:8770`) rejects `f(!(x): x+1)` when the callee's declared param type is a non-consuming `Callable`/`MutCallable`. Track M's fix only classifies against the VarDecl/Assign `dest_ty_hint` — call-arg positions don't populate that hint, so `elaborate_closure` falls into its documented "absent hint → stay silent" branch (`elaborate/mod.rs:2529 comment`). A pre-existing divergence not introduced by Track M, but Core #4 says fix the CLASS. Fix would thread a per-arg destination hint through the call-arg elaboration path (mirrors production's per-arg kind check). Own small track; likely needs a new hint channel or `elaborate_arg` variant that carries the callee's param type.

- **🆕⚙ [MED — probe owed, precedent-driven; filed 2026-07-31 by Round XX pass 6] Does ggdef model `is` (pattern test, §7.17) correctly, including bindings?** Round XII's only BOTH-WRONG fix was a missing `match_pattern` arm for `Value::Struct` (`eval.rs::match_pattern`, mirror-of-enum-arm, closed `struct_value_match_bind`/`_bind3`). An `is`-with-bindings gap would be the same class in a new costume — `if x is Some(v): v` etc. Nobody has checked this path. Own small scout: enumerate the `is`-pattern shapes (bare · with-binding · nested); build a fixture per shape; ggdef-adjudicate; if a MATCH row disagrees, that's a Core #4 sibling in a new costume.

- **🐛 [SUBSET GAP — Track B 2026-07-21, Core #9 explicit] Non-Add `OP=` / binary op static reject not in ggdef.** Rust gg lands check-time `E_UnsupportedOperator` for String non-Add (`s.name -=`, `s - "x"`) and user types without matching equip. ggdef elaborates compound to `x = x OP e` and eval still `IllFormed`s unsupported ops (Str only Add — defense-in-depth, `eval.rs`). Prefer a cheap static ElabError for in-subset String non-Add when convenient; user `equip` types may stay out-of-subset. Until then: eval IllFormed is the oracle for the shape; not silent.

- **🧹 [D29 FOLLOW-UPS — core feature complete 2026-07-17; remaining self-host residual gaps (C/LLVM cover them):]**
  - **(d)** METHOD-call fallibility classification (`d29_call_kind` returns kind-0 for method callees — needs a method-throws registry; pairs with RV-A).
  - Filing list residual: general `assign_pattern_types` wrong-constructor-vs-scrutinee gap (interim targeted ResultArmsOnPeeled landed) · statement-if/match-branch-TAIL discard residual (R4 covered fn/loop tails) · lib xtd gpu/query pre-existing standalone-check errors (non-D29).
  - **📐 [xtd disposition DESIGN SLICE — owner ruled robust-API-over-compat 2026-07-17; implement later] Per-site matrix (not migration):** (1) dht_get/dht_store fan-outs — same loop shape, dispositions must agree; (2) stream-open ×4 — SYN-retransmit self-heals dropped first send; `streams.push` happens BEFORE send → propagate would report failure while state-1 stream stays queued (push/send ORDERING rides disposition); (3) `p2p_hole_punch` Ok(true)-on-failed-send **lie** — redesign honest return; (4) xtd.ssh write sites.   - **🆕🐛 [MEDIUM — Rust compiler correctness gap, discovered during CallArg-core landing 2026-07-14] The Rust gg semantic checker ACCEPTS an enum-variant pattern with MORE sub-patterns than the variant has fields (over-arity tuple-variant pattern).** After CallArg dropped `EMethodCall` from 5→4 fields, `self_host_typechecker/parser.gg` had two `case EMethodCall(receiver, method, args, _, _)` (5 sub-patterns) and `self_host_check/loader.gg` had `case EMethodCall(_,_,args,_,_)`+`case ECall(_,args,_,_)` that BUILT WITH ZERO ERROR against the now-narrower variants (the extra `_` was silently tolerated). Core-#8 "program that should be rejected but isn't": an over-arity tuple-variant pattern must be an `E_` pattern-arity error. Fixed the self-host patterns to correct arity as part of the landing; the COMPILER leniency remains. Fix = enforce `sub_pattern_count == variant.field_count` in `src/semantic` pattern checking + a negative fixture; verify UNDER-arity too (likely also unchecked). Own small track.
  - **🆕🐛 [HIGH — drop-registration gap / lost side-effect, from A2-R1 execution 2026-07-12] A closure-returned OWNED Drop temp is not registered for drop/ownership at the call site.** Two facets of one pre-existing lowering gap (untouched by A2-R1 — it lives entirely in IR lowering, not the safety pass): (1) binding the result to a local — `auto f = (): R(7); R b = f()` (R with a custom Drop) — PANICS the build: `Tier 2a consume-site violation … AssignIntoOwnedSlot(dst: R) — untracked source consumed (ownership not decided)` at `src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:2105`; (2) passing the result directly to a borrowing call — `use(f())` — compiles but SILENTLY LOSES the drop (measured: prints `7`, never `drop 7`, where the equivalent named-fn `R make(): R(7)` correctly prints `7\ndrop 7`). Core invariant #3 (register ownership at the value's birth): the closure-return value's ownership is never decided, so main never registers it for drop. Exposed by the D12 legal fixture `tests/fixtures/d12_drop_purity/legal_closure_fresh_temp.gg` — wired as `d12_legal_closure_fresh_temp_accepts` (check-only, asserts D12 acceptance) + `#[ignore]`d `d12_legal_closure_fresh_temp_run` asserting the CORRECT `7\ndrop 7`; un-ignore when fixed. Own scout→brief→gauntlet; ASan + drop-count fixtures. NOTE this is a MISSING-drop (leak / lost side-effect) class, distinct from A2-R1's implicit-COPY (double-drop) enforcement.

What remains is the THROWS return's own hand-rolled `GirType::Ptr(inner)`-only clone (`stmts/mod.rs`, the `current_throws_result_type` branch), which is still MutPtr-blind. NOTE the accept/reject polarity is owned by the value-position-`&` reject entry below — do NOT close this by materializing without reading that ruling first. Own scout→brief→gauntlet; ASan fixture non-negotiable.

#### ====== 🏛 DEFINE GORGET — THE ACTIVE FLAGSHIP TRACK (owner GO 2026-07-05) ======
**The executable definition of Gorget's semantics.** The normative ledger is `docs/define-gorget/decisions.md` (owner decisions D1–D29 + standing directives + the open decision queue A31/A32/A33) — read it FIRST for this track. The former orchestration scaffolding (`HANDOVER.md`, the `rfc-ggc-ggdef.md` architecture RFC, `phase0-brief.md`, and every per-round scout/brief/proposal) was retired to git history in the 2026-07-17 repo-hygiene slice; recover any with `git log`/`git show`. Phase 0 and the phase-1 enforcement wave (Batches A/B + D11/D23/D29) have LANDED; the live forward work is the RATIFIED ENFORCEMENT-WAVE PLAN entry above (Batch C: C1 operators → C2 fault-catch removal → C3 sigil sweep, then the out-of-repo coordination round + D13/D14/D17 + riders) plus the CoW WAVE 2 queue. Owner directives: run subagents with `model: "opus"`; briefs are written to be Opus-executable, and an Opus stall is a BRIEF defect to fix.
- **[MEDIUM — after phase 2's context pack] 🤖 LLM-correctness KPI (owner directive 2026-07-05, recorded in decisions.md):** a measured, ratcheted "LLM zero-shot correctness" benchmark — fixed task suite, model given ONLY the generated semantic context pack, score compiles-first-try + runs-correct rates; wire as a diagnostic-always-pass report with a monotone floor (the house ratchet pattern). A first-class language-quality metric aimed at the LLM audience; no other language measures this.

- **[HIGH — ggdef phase-1 MUST, from B2 output-review 2026-07-06 + XXVI Track E scout depth §2 2026-08-02] 🐛 ggdef transitive drop execution: `run_custom_drop` skips droppable FIELDS and COLLECTION ELEMENTS of the dropped value** — nested Drop types miss the inner drop; `Vector[R]`-of-Drop misses all element drops (verified vs correct production, which now handles both post-elemdrop). RFC §2.2 makes drop count/order NORMATIVE, and drop-count spectests (phase 1) cannot gate implementations until the definition itself is transitively correct. Not exercised by the phase-0 corpus (gate legitimately green); disclosed in spec/ggdef/reports/increment_b2.md CORRECTION. **XXVI Track E scout depth §2 measured scope: G-class row 2 (`drop_collection_custom_elem_leak` BOTH-WRONG fixture) — fix is a `drop_value_transitively(ctx, state, val)` helper (~40-60 LOC) walking Vector items / Struct fields / Enum payload / Dict entries / Set items / Tuple fields. **⚠ ORDER — the earlier prescription here was WRONG and cost a round; do not restore it.** It said "reverse-decl / LIFO order (mirror production, e.g. `drop_collection_custom_elem` expected `drop 40/30/20/10` for push order 10/20/30/40)". Those four values were **never pushed into one container** — that fixture declares four SEPARATE locals (`va`/`vb`/`dv`/`sk`) each holding ONE element, so its `40/30/20/10` is reverse-*declaration* order across locals and says nothing about element order. The XXVII scout implemented that sentence, produced `drop 2 / drop 1`, hit `drop_collection_custom_elem_leak`'s expected `drop 1 / drop 2`, and escalated the contradiction as an owner design call. **RULED by D37 (owner 2026-08-02): container elements drop FORWARD** (index order for Vector/Deque, insertion order for Dict/Set); locals keep reverse-declaration. The two are different axes and compose. ⚠ Only the **Vector** cell was verified when D37 landed — struct fields / enum payload / dict entries / tuple fields must be **MEASURED against production** before the helper hardcodes an order for them; that is a measurement task, not a design call. Fits at `spec/ggdef/src/eval.rs:510-551` (existing `run_custom_drop` gains the recursive step after the user body). Ripple risk MEDIUM — every ADJ-MATCH container-of-droppable emits new drops; needs full parity re-measure. Per-container-kind axis-completeness (Core #12): one fixture per Vector / Struct / Enum / Dict / Set / Tuple of Drop.** Also from the same review: D4 position-6 plain-`self`-write detection needs method-body write analysis (the `&self` sibling was closed post-review with pinning tests); position-5 capture check should consolidate into the centralized helper; CLI should exit non-zero on elaboration errors (provisional codes); `-- gen` should skip/error on non-`run`-mode fixtures (today it would clobber a static-error expect block — harmless while error tiers are empty). One phase-1 track. Own scout→gauntlet.

- **[HIGH — ggdef G-class row 3, filed XXVI Track E 2026-08-02; DEPENDS ON the transitive-drop row above] 🐛 `drop_struct_collection_fields` BOTH-WRONG: five interacting ggdef sub-defects around collection-of-Drop with mutation.** Sub-defects per XXVI Track E scout depth §3 + XXVII Track A re-triage:
  (a) **Transitive drop** (row-2 prereq — the entry above).
  (b) **Pre-drop-on-set for `Vector.set`/`Dict.set`**: `spec/ggdef/src/eval.rs::apply_mut` `M::Set` arm (`~:1807-1814` for Vector, `~:1816-` for Dict) overwrites `items[i] = x;` without dropping the replaced value. Fix ~5 LOC per arm: call `drop_value_transitively` on `items[i]` before `items[i] = x;`. The `drop old len=2` / `drop old-inner len=0` output lines depend on this.
  (c) **Return-position `!expr` kill** — NEW SCOUT FINDING XXVI Track E: `spec/ggdef/src/elaborate/mod.rs:1613-1618` (`ast::Expr::Move` in read position) treats bare `!x` as a plain READ. But `Stmt::Return { value: Option<Expr>, span }` (`ggc.rs:149`) takes an `Expr`, NOT a `Source`, so `elaborate/mod.rs:1056-1094` `ast::Stmt::Return(opt)` hits the Move-in-read-position arm and NEVER kills the inner local. Concretely: `return !c` fails to kill `c`, so `c` drops at `make_container` fn exit → ggdef prints `"drop third first"` at wrong time. Fix (~15-30 LOC): either detect `ast::Expr::Move { expr }` at the top of Return's Expr and emit a synthetic kill, or promote `Stmt::Return.value` to a shape that carries kill-provenance.
  (d) **Intricate 15-drop LIFO ordering** across nested Vector[Vector[Container]] / Vector[Wrapper] / Vector[Container] — expected string at `tests/integration.rs:6961-6979`; getting the order right in ONE round with no ripple to ADJ-MATCH is fragile.
  (e) **`.get(0).unwrap()` returns a full CLONE, not a borrow/Ref** — 5TH DEFECT, found by the XXVII Track A scout. The extracted local then Drops alongside the collection's own item, duplicating `drop wrapped` / `drop new` / `drop first`. Distinct from (a)-(d): those are drop-emission defects, this one is a value-model defect that manufactures an extra droppable.
  Estimated total ~100-150 LOC across 5 sites; interaction risk HIGH. Sequence AFTER row 2's transitive-drop scout+gauntlet lands + settles. Own scout→gauntlet (per Core #12 axis-completeness on the LIFO cascade). **No longer blocked on an owner design call** — D37 (owner 2026-08-02) rules container elements drop FORWARD; see the ⚠ ORDER note on row 2 above for the false premise that produced the block.
- **[HIGH — ggdef native-recursion SIGABRT; separate track split off from the P1-A throw-drop fix 2026-07-06] 🐛 ggdef's big-step eval recurses on the NATIVE stack, so a deeply-recursive fixture SIGABRTs (stack overflow) instead of reporting `FuelExhausted`.** `stack_guard_deep_recursion.gg` blows the real stack — fuel bounds STEP count, not eval/elaborate DEPTH; RFC §2.2's "ggdef is total via fuel and never exhausts a real stack" is currently FALSE. Fix = add a recursion-depth guard (tied to fuel or an explicit depth counter) → `FuelExhausted`/`Trap`, OR soften the RFC §2.2 text and keep this fixture in the RFC-C11 implementation-defined exclusion set (owner call). The two promoted P1-A gates (`spec/ggdef/tests/coverage_histogram.rs`, `converter_agreement.rs`) currently EXCLUDE `stack_guard_deep_recursion.gg` by name + run in a 2 GiB-stack thread to avoid the crash; that exclusion is the workaround this fix retires. (The bundled throw-drop half of this entry LANDED — see DONE.md 2026-07-06.)

(b) **Call-site-`&` sigil discipline is ASYMMETRIC in production — an owner-facing design question for the sigil-consolidation review** (found by the stage-1a output-review's probes): FREE-FN call sites statically REJECT a bare place arg into a `&` param (`E_OwnershipMismatch` — `check_call_ownership`, `src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs:1258`, wired to the `Expr::Call` arm ONLY at `check_expr.rs:315`), while METHOD calls never reach the check and silently accept + WRITE THROUGH with no warning (`c.add_all(v)` into `&vals` — the ratified `method_mut_borrow_arg` "correct 6" rests on this face). ggdef now mirrors BOTH faces (review-folded: method path write-through, free-fn path loud ElabError). The owner question: is the asymmetry intended (method signatures as API contract, extending §3.1's receiver exception to all method params) or should one face become uniform? Either ratification is a both-compiler + ggdef + book change. - **⚙️ [D18 RATIFIED 2026-07-06 → implementation track] Const-eval fault mirror: runtime faults become compile errors.** The general rule (ledger LOG): const-eval mirrors runtime exactly, except faults → rejection. (1) `src/semantic/meta.rs:1278` drops `wrapping_add/sub/mul` for checked ops → `E_` compile error on overflow (message mirrors the runtime trap text); (2) sweep the const-eval fault surface for the CLASS: div-by-zero, mod-zero, shift-overflow if runtime-checked — each rejects; (3) const `+%`/`-%`/`*%` wrap (verify support exists in const context; add if missing); (4) const float overflow → `inf` (verify mirrors runtime — no fault either side); (5) fixtures: negative (const INT_MAX+1 rejected; const 1/0 rejected) + positive (const `+%` wraps; const float inf); (6) docs write-through: the meta/comptime section states the one-sentence rule; (7) self-host meta-eval mirror (verify its const-eval path — same rule, both compilers). Small track; probe evidence /tmp/recover_scout_errmodel/q6_*.
  - **[folded 2026-07-21 — the same meta.rs:1278 site, from the error-model decision scout]** **🐛 [SILENT COMPILE-TIME WRONG, found by the error-model decision scout 2026-07-06; coupled to batch-4 decision] const-eval / meta integer overflow WRAPS silently where runtime FAULTS.** `const int BIG = 9223372036854775807 + 1` → check OK, prints `-9223372036854775808`; the same op at runtime faults ("integer overflow", exit 1). `src/semantic/meta.rs:1278` still uses `wrapping_add/sub/mul` (the global wrap-mode knob was retired but const-eval kept wrapping). Scout rec (batch-4): compile ERROR — const inputs are determinate, there is no runtime boundary to catch a compile-time fault, "the type never lies." Probe: /tmp/recover_scout_errmodel/q6_const_overflow.gg.
