#### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-rust follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-rust executor)
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_shift.gg` conformance fixture (BLOCKED on ggdef modeling shift).** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift (`x << 64`) trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the missing LLVM shift-range check was ADDED, owner ruling 2026-07-10). There is currently an integration test pinning both-backend agreement (`tests/integration.rs` shift test) but NO conformance fixture, because ggdef's evaluator (`spec/ggdef/src/eval.rs`) does not yet MODEL a shift operator / its out-of-range trap. To add `trap_shift.gg` (ggdef-adjudicated), first extend ggdef to evaluate `<<`/`>>` and Trap `Overflow` when the count is out of range, then generate the fixture (crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors). Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_assert_cmp.gg` conformance fixture for the MESSAGE-LESS comparison assert (`assert a == b`).** T2a-rust rerouted BOTH assert forms to `trap[T_AssertFailed]` + exit 101: the message form (via `gorget_trap`) AND the message-less comparison form (via `gorget_assert_fail_values`, now a 4-arg sig carrying the `T_AssertFailed` code — approach (a)). The existing `trap_assert.gg` exercises the MESSAGE form only; the message-less form is currently pinned by integration tests (`assert_fails`, `test_should_panic`, `test_failure`) but not by a conformance fixture. ggdef already models `Stmt::Assert`, so it's generatable — but adding a spectests fixture crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors, so do it as a separate small T1 follow-up.
- **[MEDIUM — structural guard] The LLVM `block_exit_labels` twin pre-pass silently drifts from the emit.** `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:3008` reconstructs each block's exit label (for phi predecessors) by RE-RUNNING the emit's `trap_counter`/block-splitting logic in a SEPARATE match — a hand-maintained twin. T2a-rust's new Shl/Shr shift range-check (which splits the block + bumps `trap_counter`) had to be mirrored there by hand; forgetting it produced `llc: use of undefined value '%ov.*.ok'` that ONLY the self-host bootstrap caught (all trap fixtures + spec_conformance passed). This is a latent class: any future block-splitting `Inst` variant added to `emit_inst` without a matching arm in `block_exit_labels` silently miscompiles under a phi. Two options: (a) a `tests/lints.rs` guard asserting the two match-arm sets agree (the `Inst` variants that bump the counter must be identical in both), or (b) the real fix — have `emit_inst` RECORD each block's exit label into a map as it emits (single source of truth), deleting the twin entirely. Prefer (b). **⚠ NOW ≥2 CONFIRMED INSTANCES + a latent sibling (2026-07-10 — escalate off low-pri):** (1) T2a-rust's Shl/Shr shift check (fixed). (2) **T2b's `gorget_array_get_at` early-return** (`llvm/mod.rs:~4647`) skipped the generic-CallExtern `*trap_counter += 1` the pre-pass counts → 12 conformance fixtures BUILD-FAIL on the LLVM lane (`arr[i]` before an overflow-checked op, e.g. `sum += a[i]` in a loop); output-review caught it, fixed with a manual bump (`feee30d3`). (3) **LATENT, pre-existing:** the newtype-constructor early-return directly below (`llvm/mod.rs:~4658`) ALSO returns without bumping while the pre-pass counts it — builds+runs correctly TODAY but is the same class, one early-return away from a miscompile. (4) **LATENT, from the xhigh review 2026-07-10 (CONFIRMED mechanism):** the `gorget_trap` CallExtern early-return (`llvm/mod.rs:~4625`) bumps `trap_counter` 0/1/2× (once per GorgetString-typed arg via `marshal_cstr`) while the pre-pass counts every CallExtern as exactly 1 — masked ONLY because every current producer ends its block with `Term::Unreachable`; any future mid-block `gorget_trap` emitter (warn-then-continue trap, a pass appending a drop after the call) desyncs the `ov.*.ok` labels → `llc: use of undefined value`. Fix (b) retires ALL of these (incl. the two manual bumps). **⚠ FIX-(b) MEASURED (R-C scout 2026-07-10): NOT drop-in.** The pre-pass is 227 lines, single consumer (`mod.rs:3297`); (b)'s mechanics are ~25 lines (phi placeholders + record `current_label` per block + post-loop patch) MINUS the 227 — but correctness INVERTS to "every label-creating site must update `current_label`", and at least one already violates it: `gorget_str_clear` creates `scl.done` labels and returns WITHOUT updating (`mod.rs:5356-5361`; the pre-pass covers it at `:3238`). ~44 label-writing sites need that audit; the FULL LLVM sweep is the only honest gate. Own track, bootstrap-gated; instance count when it runs: 2 fixed + 2 latent + the R-C guard's mirrored arm to retire.
- **[LOW — cosmetic, not conformance-compared] Message-form `assert` trap line renders `at <unknown>:0:0`.** T2a-rust reroutes assert-with-message to `gorget_trap(T_AssertFailed, msg)` → `gorget_trap_at` threading the emit-site span (like `panic`). The code + exit 101 are correct and BYTE-IDENTICAL across C/LLVM, but the source LOCATION on the assert trap line resolves to `<unknown>:0:0` (Q-D wanted a real span). Root cause is a PRE-EXISTING GIR→LIR span-propagation gap for these branch-target trap blocks — the old `gorget_panic` assert emitted `<unknown>:0:0` too, and `condition.span` IS a real byte range (verified), yet the LIR instruction carries no span (both `call_extern` and `call_void` reproduce it, so it is NOT the Call/CallExtern distinction). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, Q1), so this is cosmetic. Fix belongs in the GIR→LIR span-map plumbing for branch-target blocks, not trap normalization.

#### D23-throws smith follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T3b executor)
- **[MEDIUM] 🎲 T3c — a POSITIVE throws DIFFERENTIAL smith tier.** T3b (LANDED, see DONE.md) is the NEGATIVE/rejection tier: `GG_SMITH_TIER=1` generates programs well-formed EXCEPT for ONE unhandled `throws` call and asserts production REJECTS with `E_UnhandledThrows` (an inverted, CHECK-ONLY oracle — `classify_throws` in `tests/smith/main.rs`, `program_throws_positions` in `tests/smith/generator.rs`). T3c is the complement and is arguably HIGHER-value: generate programs with HANDLED throws (auto-propagate inside a `throws` fn, `catch`/`rethrow`, `Result[T,E]` capture, the §10.3 capture forms) that COMPILE, then run the FULL backend differential (production `gg check`→C build/run, ggdef in-process, self-host, LLVM) exactly like tier 0. ggdef already MODELS handled throws (`Propagate`, §10.3 capture, `spec/ggdef/src/elaborate/mod.rs`), so it can adjudicate — this would catch a backend MISCOMPILING the throws→`Result` desugar at RUNTIME (a class the negative tier structurally cannot reach, since those programs never compile). It is a NEW tier number (not `TIER_THROWS=1`) reusing tier 0's differential `classify` path, so no new oracle shape — the work is the generator grammar (a `throws`-helper set + the handling forms) plus a tier dispatch arm. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews before launch (the generator MUST emit programs that are well-formed AND actually handle the throws — the false-positive discipline from T3b's scout applies in mirror image).
- **[LOW] 🎲 T3b equip-method receiver-position extension (deferred from T3b).** T3b's generator ships the free-fn `risky()` form only; the `equip`-method receiver position (`s.m()` where `int m(self) throws String:`) was OMITTED because it needs a synthesized `struct S` + in-scope instance + `equip S:` block to be well-formed, and the scout found the false-positive trap that a bare `int risky(self) throws String:` WITHOUT `equip` is ACCEPTED (`self` parses as a plain param) — a mis-resolving shape would mis-fire the inverted oracle as a spurious slip. T3a's `d23_unhandled_method{,_traitdefault,_xmod}` fixtures already cover the method-reject, so this is incremental hardening, not a gap. If added: spell out + verify the struct+instance+equip scaffolding produces a well-formed-except-for-the-one-throws program before wiring it as a new position in `emit_throws_position`.
#### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-selfhost follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-selfhost executor)
- **[MEDIUM — self-host-shift-parity, Core-#8] The self-host emits an UNGUARDED out-of-range shift — the lone backend with silent UB on `x << 64`.** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the LLVM shift-range check was added, owner ruling 2026-07-10). The self-host lowerer does NOT guard shift at all: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_codegen.gg:4536` (IShl) / `:4539` (IShr) emit raw `v = lhs << rhs;` / `>> ` with no range check, so a self-host-compiled `x << 64` is silent UB where both Rust backends now trap. This is PRE-EXISTING (the self-host never guarded shift) and NOT in the conformance corpus (ggdef does not model shift yet — see the T2a-rust `trap_shift.gg` item above), so it does NOT affect the 7-fixture flip and it is NOT a "reroute an existing guard" (there is no guard to reroute — it needs a NEW one). Fix: add the self-host shift-range guard emitting `gorget_trap("T_Overflow", "shift out of range")` (mirroring the Rust C emit `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:2649/2656`: `if ((uint64_t)rhs >= (uint64_t)(sizeof(ct)*8)) { gorget_trap(...); }`) + a self-host-lane shift test, so all three backends agree. Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).

#### Self-host reject-diagnostic-rendering alignment (filed 2026-07-15; split off from the ggdef elab∘eval landing, owner-confirmed — the COMMITTED next track that completes four-lane-green)
  - **🆕🐛 [HIGH — DEFINITION-INTEGRITY (oracle under-rejection), from the coarse-kind scout 2026-07-16] ggdef ACCEPTS (exit 0) programs BOTH production compilers reject on the range/signature/main-throws axes — and its `reject_code` verdict axis is MAY-MOVE-ONLY (`spec/ggdef/src/lib.rs:148`).** Measured: `ggdef run` on the real coarse-kind reject corpus yields NO `reject: E_X` verdict for ANY of them — buckets: FrontendError-SKIP (deref/default-op/positional/throw), eval-IllFormed-codeless (string-index/break/continue), and outright ACCEPT (value-range / required-after-default / main-throws — the oracle blesses ill-formed programs, same under-rejection class as the ConsumeCallable bug). Per the ratified `verdict = elaborate ∘ eval` boundary (2026-07-16): **ggdef-elaborate must model every ratified static rejection within its subset** — extend elaborate beyond may-move to the type/control-flow/signature axes (each E_ code as a typed rule, static walk, no execution), which THEN unblocks the coarse-kind four-lane spectests migration (floors move only after this). Sequencing: elaborate-extension track AFTER the coarse-kind split lands (the split is floor-neutral and independent). Own scout→brief→gauntlet (Rust, spec/ggdef/, not bootstrap-gated).
  - **🆕 [LOW — registry-prose completeness, from the self-host reject-diagnostic pass-1 review 2026-07-16] `E_LocalBorrowBind` is emitted by BOTH production (`src/semantic/errors.rs:715`) and (post-reject-diagnostic-landing) the self-host, but is ABSENT from the registry-prose table `spec/prose/diagnostic-codes.md` (which the briefs call "the source of truth").** Add the missing `E_LocalBorrowBind` row so the registry is actually complete. Zero conformance impact today (no d10a spectest exists), pure doc completeness. Trivial — fold into the next diagnostic-codes doc touch.

- **[MEDIUM — D8 float chain, filed by P1-infra review 2026-07-06; ~63-79 fixtures held behind it] Three sequenced prerequisites for float-fixture migration:** (1) **author the D8 formatting appendix** in spec/prose — encodes D9 (SETTLED 2026-07-06: `3.0` → "3.0"; ggdef's provisional `format!("{f}")` prints "3", NOT D9-compliant — fix in prereq 2); (2) **ggdef `format_value` to the appendix** (spec/ggdef, small); (3) **production print + float_to_str to the appendix, BOTH backends** (heals the %f-fixed-6 vs %g inconsistency; regenerates ~63-79 fixture expectations via the P1-D converter, which is exactly the planned mechanism). Order is mandatory 1→2→3; float spectests migrate only after all three.
- **[MEDIUM — ggdef, owner-question-driven 2026-07-06] 🔍 HOST-INHERITANCE AUDIT: enumerate and PIN every place spec/ggdef's eval delegates to Rust behavior** — the D9 class generalized (ggdef's float printing WAS Rust's Display until D9 pinned it). Sweep eval.rs for semantic delegation to the host language: integer division/modulo SIGN conventions, shift semantics, float NaN handling + comparisons + casts, string/Unicode ops, container iteration order, sort stability, hash behavior, format conversions. Each becomes an explicit spec rule (prose + unit test) or a documented Nondet — never an implicit inheritance. Deliverable: an audit table in spec/prose (rule | pinned-by | test). Rationale vs multi-language ggdef reimplementation: the definition must stay SINGULAR (Knight-Leveson correlated-error result; Wasm/SML/MiniRust precedent — one definitional artifact); redundancy comes from prose-parity review, this audit, the phase-2 Lean mechanization (the principled second implementation), and eventually a post-1.0 Gorget port of ggdef as a confirmation artifact. Fits phase 1-2; small.
### Low

- **🆕🧹 [MED-LOW — typed-metadata smell in OUR OWN test infra, self-filed 2026-07-16] The ggdef corpus out-of-subset mechanism is TWO name-keyed EXCLUDE lists (`corpus_b.rs` + `corpus_b1.rs`) — sibling drift by construction** (the 1B fixture needed both edited; 1C will again; a missed twin = a bricked lane, exactly the parallel-list class rule 2 forbids). Reference-grade shape: ONE typed frontmatter key on the fixture itself (e.g. `# ggdef-subset: false` + reason) read by both corpus harnesses — set at the source, no name lists. Small slice; retires both EXCLUDE lists.
- **🧹 [P1-B review follow-up, 2026-07-06] ggdef frontmatter reader: unknown TOP-LEVEL keys with nested/`|`-block values error (`MalformedKey`) instead of being tolerated.** Loud, never silent — no seed trips it today — but RFC §4's `files:` is a future block-shaped key. Fix: make the `other` arm (`spec/ggdef/src/frontmatter.rs:192` at filing) consume-and-discard an unknown key's indented children. Doc-comments were narrowed to inline-only at integration (module doc LIMIT note cites this entry).

- **🧹 [P1-B review follow-up, 2026-07-06] ggdef frontmatter `Expect{exit,stdout}` is RUN-TIER-ONLY — not the all-tier contract.** RFC §4 static-error/parse-error tiers express `expect:` as a diagnostic code (`expect: code: E_…`, no exit/stdout). The tier-track that populates those directories must refactor `Expect` into a tier-discriminated enum (breaking change to the 4-lane-shared type, `frontmatter.rs:41-46` at filing) — do not treat the run-tier shape as final.

- **🧹 [ggdef hardening, from P1-A pass-2 review 2026-07-06; PRE-EXISTING, unreachable via `gg check`] Closure bodies inherit the enclosing fn's `current_fn_throws`.** A closure body containing a bare throws call is silently evaluated by ggdef (garbled `ok Error(neg)` on the throwing path) where production REJECTS at typecheck (`E_TypeMismatch`) — so no check-accepted program reaches it, but the loudness invariant should hold unconditionally. Fix: scope/reset `current_fn_throws` (and capture_ctx state) at closure boundaries, or make unmodeled closure-throws a loud ElabError. spec/ggdef elaborate/mod.rs.

- **🧹 [P1-G follow-up] Extend diagnostic codes to lex/parse time.** `LexErrorKind`/`ParseErrorKind` still render bare `error:` (noted in `spec/prose/diagnostic-codes.md` §Follow-up). Same recipe as P1-G: catch-all-free `code()` per enum, thread at the reporter, register in the prose table.

- **🆕 [LOW — T1-zone, from the R-C scout 2026-07-10] Combinator-route spectest twin for `trap_unwrap_error_on_ok`** — the existing conformance fixture exercises only the Tier-2a LIR route; the combinator route (static/global receiver, `Result__T__E__unwrap_error` mangle) is pinned only by the new integration fixtures. A spectests twin (ggdef-generated, static receiver shape) makes the route conformance-compared on all lanes. #### Trap-normalization (D11) T2b follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2b executor)
- **[LOW — reference-grade span plumbing; not conformance-compared] The RARER bounds-class sites still trap `trap[T_Bounds] at <unknown>:0:0` (span-less) — thread real `file:line:col` into them, mirroring the flagship `gorget_array_get_at`.** T2b gave the flagship Vector element READ (`v[i]`) a REAL source location via `gorget_array_get_at(arr, idx, code, file, line, col)` + an emit-boundary reroute on BOTH Rust backends (`src/backend/c_lir/emit_call_extern.rs`, `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs`), but the rest of the index/bounds class stays SPAN-LESS (owner ruling: flagship-only in T2b): the runtime helpers `gorget_array_set/remove/insert/swap/swap_remove/slice`, `gorget_str_index/slice/byte_slice/byte_at`, `gorget_shared_array_get/set`, and the ~16 `gorget_bytes_*` offset checks all call the span-less `gorget_trap(GG_T_BOUNDS, detail)` → `trap[T_Bounds]: … at <unknown>:0:0` + exit 101 (correct code + exit; only the LOCATION is `<unknown>`). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, D11 Q1), so this is reference-grade polish, not a correctness gap. Fix = per-site `_at` variants (arr/idx/…, code, file, line, col) + an emit-boundary reroute for each of the `v[i]=x` write / `.remove` / `.insert` / `.slice` / `str[i]` / `shared[i]` / bytes-offset call sites, exactly as `gorget_array_get_at` did for the read. This is the same per-runtime-fn span-plumbing as the broader runtime-side item (see the "Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites)" entry) — could fold into it. Pairs with self-host span-threading (`<unknown>:0:0` there too, existing LOW entry).

### High
- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — MEMORY-UNSAFE misparse, `gg check` clean, BOTH backends; found by the R41 S4 scout 2026-08-10, orchestrator-verified (exit 135 = SIGBUS)] A parenthesized BINOP key in a dict literal misparses as a CLOSURE param list.** `Dict[int, int] m = {(a & b): 99}` → the literal becomes an array-of-closures (`ClosureParam { type_: Named("a"), ownership: MutableBorrow, name: "b" }`), the unknown param type `a` never rejects, array-of-closures into `Dict[int,int]` never type-errors, runtime SIGBUS — ≥3 stacked bugs on the `looks_like_closure` shape heuristic (`src/parser/expr.rs`, patched 4× per DONE.md, still holed; the identical hole opens for `^` the moment Caret leaves the deny-list — measured by the scout on an isolated build). DURABLE REPRO: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/dict_key_paren_binop_closure_misparse.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `dict_key_paren_binop_closure_misparse` asserting the INTENDED bitwise-AND key (`99`). - **🆕🐛 [LOW — lexer gap; R41 S1 scout 2026-08-10] Exponent float literals (`1e10`) do NOT parse** — a lexer gap, not an fmt defect (found during the fmt sweep triage). File-only: verify intended surface (language-reference float grammar) and wire the fix + fixtures in a numerics round.
- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — VISIBILITY MODEL: spec-vs-implementation divergence + OWNER DESIGN CALL REQUIRED; measured 2026-08-08 by direct build+run probes on `target/debug/gg`] `docs/language-reference.md` §12 specifies three different defaults with three different enforcement levels; two of the three are NOT enforced at all.** Measured cells (each probe built AND ran, non-f-string per Core #15(d) MIND THE PROBE): **(a) ENFORCED —** item-level `private` on fn/struct/enum/trait/const/static/typealias/newtype. Explicit import of a private name → `E_PrivateImport` (`src/semantic/resolve.rs:303`); use without import → `E_UndefinedName` (private names excluded from `export_non_private`, `resolve.rs:918`). Verified against real stdlib: `from xtd.json import escape_string` → `E_PrivateImport`. **(b) NOT ENFORCED AT ALL — struct FIELD visibility.** `src/parser/mod.rs:1030` parses `field_vis` into `StructField.visibility`; the ONLY readers are `src/formatter/mod.rs:613` and nothing else — zero semantic checks. Cross-module private-field READ *and* WRITE both compile and run: `Point p = Point(1.5, 2.5); p.internal_id = 99.0` prints `99.000000`. Spec says fields are "private by default … internal layout is an implementation detail"; in reality every field of every struct is fully public and writable from any module. **(c) NOT ENFORCED — method visibility.** `private` inside an `equip` block parses (`src/parser/mod.rs:1321`) and is read only by `check_private_in_public`; a cross-module `p.hidden_method()` call compiles and runs. **(d) `PrivateTypeInPublicSignature` covers only 2 of ≥6 leak surfaces** (`src/semantic/safety/validation.rs:83-148` matches `Item::Function` and `Item::Equip` only): a private type leaks unchecked through a **public struct field**, an **enum variant payload**, a **trait method signature**, and a public `const`/`static` type — all measured accepted. **(e) `private` in the ENTRY file is a no-op** — `private_names` is computed per `Item::Module` (`resolve.rs:889`) and the entry file's items are NOT wrapped (`src/loader.rs:1380`). **(f) Imports do not FILTER, and privacy is the only namespace boundary.** `from lib import Point` also makes `public_helper` visible without naming it; a 3-module chain leaks `deep_fn` into the app that only imported `mid`; there is no qualified `lib.foo()` form (`E_UndefinedName: undefined name 'lib'`). Every public name in the transitive module graph lands in one flat global scope, so two modules exporting the same public name → `E_DuplicateDefinition` (private names correctly do NOT collide and dispatch right — verified `12`). **(g) MINOR —** `E_PrivateImport` is emitted TWICE for one bad import; the `E_UndefinedName` for a non-exported name points its span at the *defining* module's EOF (`lib.gg:26:1`) instead of the use site. **(h) THE `parse_item` MODIFIER-DISCARD FAMILY — widened + corrected 2026-08-11 (re-measured on a fresh build; the old (g) clause said `private` on an enum VARIANT was a silently-accepted no-op, which is WRONG).** `parse_item` consumes a visibility modifier and the attribute list, then dispatches to sub-parsers that take NEITHER — so the modifier and the attributes are discarded BEFORE any later pass, `gg fmt` included, can see them. Four members, one mechanism: **(h1) equip blocks** — `public equip Foo:` parses and checks clean (exit 0); `parse_equip_block()` takes no visibility. **(h2) `test`** and **(h3) `bench`** — `public test "…":` / `public bench "…":` likewise accepted and dropped. **(h4) enum VARIANTS — NOT a no-op, and the sharpest cell:** `enum Color:` with `private Red()` MINTS A PHANTOM UNIT VARIANT named `private`, so the enum has variants `[private, Red, Green]` — and `gg check` accepts it. The parser reads `private` as a variant name, not a modifier. **(h5) ATTRIBUTES on equip / extern blocks** — `@link("libm")` written above `equip Foo:` (or above `extern "C":`) is silently deleted; `gg fmt` re-emits the block without it. ⚖ DIRECTION for h1-h3 is RATIFIED (ledger 2026-08-11): visibility on equip/test/bench is a parse-time REJECT with a teaching diagnostic (the Rust E0449 shape). The W2 reject-rider ships that fix WITH its cross-lane NEG conformance fixture per Core #11, so no `known_gaps` repro is owed for that half — this entry's on-purpose-no-repro logic is discharged by ratification there. ⚖ h4 RULED (owner 2026-08-14, ledger): parse-time REJECT, same family + diagnostic shape as h1-h3; variant-level visibility may be promoted to a real feature later if need arises. Joins the W2 reject-rider; its NEG conformance fixtures ship WITH the reject per Core #11, discharging this entry's deferred repro for h4. ⚖ h5 RULED (owner 2026-08-16, ledger): attributes above `equip`/`extern` blocks are a parse-time REJECT with a teaching diagnostic, joining the h1-h4 rider; the `@link`-as-missing-CARRY-feature premise is WITHDRAWN as unfounded (measured: `@link` occurs once tree-wide, in a formatter escaping fixture; no manifest link directives; native linking hardcoded in `src/main.rs`). Its NEG conformance fixtures ship WITH the reject per Core #11. Repro for h4/h5 deferred with the rest of this entry, per its no-repro-until-ratified logic. ⭐ **OWNER CALL FIRST, then fix (Core #15(e) Q1):** whether (b)/(c) are bugs-to-fix or spec-to-change depends on the ratified default. Census for the decision: `lib/xtd` has ~1109 top-level decls, 89 explicit `private`, **0** explicit top-level `public` (all 46 `public` uses tree-wide are struct fields); per-module the marking is wildly inconsistent (`toml.gg` 19/27 private, `json.gg` 2/17) — evidence that public-by-default under-marks the intended API surface. **No `known_gaps` repro committed yet ON PURPOSE:** a `known_gaps` fixture must assert the CORRECT/intended output, and the intended accept/reject for (b)(c)(d) is exactly what the design call decides. File the repros the same round the direction is ratified; probes live at `/tmp/claude-1000/-workspace-gorget/*/scratchpad/vis/` (evaporates — re-derive from this entry's descriptions). Recommendation on record: ONE uniform rule — private-by-default, single `public` keyword, applied to every declaration form, enforced everywhere (see the visibility consult, 2026-08-08).

- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — Core #8 BOTH BACKENDS, filed 2026-08-06, sibling of Round XXXII OPAQUE-HANDLE RECEIVER-ABI class fix] `Vector[T]` where T is a SelfConvention::ByValue opaque handle SEGVs on both backends — DIFFERENT layer from the Round XXXII fix.** Repros: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/atomic_int_collection_elem_segv.gg` (wired `#[ignore]`d as `atomic_int_collection_elem_segv`, asserts `2`) + `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/vector_mutex_elem_lock_segv.gg` (wired `#[ignore]`d, asserts `42`), both RED-verified. `gg check` ACCEPTS them, both backends SEGV 139. ⭐ **MECHANISM:** `gorget_array_push(arr, __v5)` where `__v5` is a `GorgetAtomicInt*` (opaque handle pointer) treats `elem` as pointer-to-value-to-copy and memcpys `elem_size` (8) bytes STARTING AT the handle pointer into the slot — reading the FIRST 8 bytes of the AtomicInt struct (its `__val` field, initial 0) instead of storing the handle POINTER itself. Slot ends up holding 0; original handle is leaked; subsequent `v[0].add(1)` reads slot value (0) and calls `atomic_int_add(0, 1)` → SEGV. The Round XXXII receiver-ABI chokepoint (`methods.rs:2315-2337`) correctly LOADS the slot value and passes it as receiver, so the receiver-ABI half is right; the SLOT is corrupt because push mis-serialized. **CORRECT FIX LAYER:** push-side (and put/insert/send) arg-building — for scalar-sized value types (bool, int, opaque handle) `push(arr, val)` currently emits args like `gorget_array_push(__v3, __v5)` for handles vs `gorget_array_push(__v3, &(int64_t){__v5})` for int — needs an equivalent `&__v5` wrap for opaque handles. Owner-decided fix layer in a follow-up scout. **CLASS:** 12 by-value protocols (Shared, Weak, Mutex, RWLock, Thread, AtomicInt, AtomicBool, Barrier, WaitGroup, Semaphore, OnceFlag, TaskGroup) — two representatives measured. Graduating the two known_gaps = +2 closes.

DEFERRED-NOT-DECLINED — the enforcement pass is worth doing, just AFTER parity (it's parity-orthogonal + reads more-trustworthy types once inference is maximally correct + the dominant risk is FP). #### [rejection follow-up, invariant #8 — from Rejection-#1 review] positional-after-named on METHOD calls accepted by BOTH compilers
Rust gg's `check_named_args_and_defaults` (PositionalAfterNamed) is invoked at ONLY the free-function ECall path (typecheck.rs:1569), NOT for method calls — Rust gg ACCEPTS `s.compute(a=1, 2)` (exit 0), and so does the self-host. So Rejection #1 (self-host ECall, not EMethodCall) faithfully mirrors Rust's CURRENT scope. But per invariant #8 this is a ≥2-bug defect BOTH compilers share: reject positional-after-named on METHOD calls in Rust gg (extend the check to the EMethodCall path) AND the self-host (add the same walk to the EMethodCall typecheck arm) + a negative fixture asserting both reject. Separate follow-up.

Fix production ctor paths + negative/positive fixtures; both-compiler check (self-host ctor named-arg behavior UNVERIFIED — probe in the same pass). Own scout→brief→reviews.
### Medium

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — SIGSEGV on an accepted program, Rust gg, found 2026-07-26 by sigil-prose gauntlet pass 11, parent-reproduced] A comprehension over a NESTED `Vector` segfaults, with NO sigil involved.** `Vector[Vector[int]] a = [...]; Vector[int] r = [x.len() for x in a]` → `gg check` OK, `gg build` OK, binary **exit 139**, no output. ⚠ **The ALL-BARE form is the one that proves it is not a sigil bug** — all four spellings crash identically (`for x in a`, `for x in &a`, `[grow(&x) for x in a]`, `[grow(&x) for x in &a]`), so this is a pre-existing comprehension-over-nested-collection defect and a fix in the `&`-write-through family will NOT touch it. ⚠⚠ **THE AXIS IS THE ELEMENT TYPE, and it was widened 2026-07-26 by gauntlet pass 12 — a nested `Vector` is just one cell.** Measured, all `gg check`-clean, no sigil anywhere: `Vector[int]` (COPY elements) → correct, exit 0 · `Vector[String]` with a non-value-producing element expr (`[1 for s in a]`) → **prints the CORRECT answer then SIGSEGVs at TEARDOWN** (C and LLVM) · `Vector[String]` with a value-producing one (`[s + "!" for s in a]`) → **ICE, "resource-moves … shallow copy of resource _19 : GorgetString"** (`ir/lowering/mod.rs:1766`) · `Vector[Vector[int]]` → SIGSEGV. So a comprehension over any RESOURCE-element collection is broken, in two modes depending on whether the element expression produces a value. ⚠ **`stdbuf -o0` IS REQUIRED TO SEE THIS CORRECTLY** — stdout is buffered, so the pre-crash output is lost and a correct-then-crashing program reads as "crashed with no output"; that artifact alone made four correct programs look instantly broken during triage. Distinct from the filed self-host CRASH census (that is the SH lane; this is the reference compiler). ⚠ **Triage-hazard case study, worth reading before dismissing a non-reproducing crash:** an earlier probe of a different comprehension shape (a `void` element function) exited 0 with the write merely lost, so the crash looked unreproducible and was nearly written off; the shapes differ in whether the element expression produces a VALUE. Vary the shape before concluding a crash claim was wrong. ⚠ `gg run` masks it as exit 1 (filed separately). Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_comprehension_nested_vector_segv.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_comprehension_nested_vector_segv` (RED-verified: "Binary exited with error … status=None").

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — MEMORY-UNSAFE, `gg check` ACCEPTS; found 2026-07-26 by sigil-prose gauntlet pass 10, parent-reproduced] Assigning a `String` element inside `for … in &coll` is a HEAP-USE-AFTER-FREE.** `Vector[String] a = [...]; for e in &a: e = e + "!"` → `gg check` OK, `gg build` OK, binary aborts `free(): double free detected in tcache 2`; **ASan reports `heap-use-after-free`**, so it is worse than the plain double-free the runtime prints. ⚠ **THIS IS THE UNSAFE CELL OF A TYPE AXIS THAT IS OTHERWISE MERELY WRONG OR CORRECT** — measured on the same loop shape, all four `gg check`-clean: `int` element → write LOST (1, want 101) · **struct element → WRITES THROUGH correctly (101)** · **`Vector[int]` element → WRITES THROUGH correctly (len 2)** · `String` element → UAF. So the reference's former blanket "element write-through through a loop iterable is lost" was wrong twice: false for struct/Vector, and understating heap corruption as a lost write for String (Core #8 on top of Core #12). Doc corrected same commit. **🔍 PROVENANCE — BISECTED 2026-07-26. This is a REGRESSION, ~6.5 weeks old, and it was introduced BY A LEAK FIX.** First bad commit **`a367f2c3` (2026-06-11)**, *"fix(lowering,lir): register print/format String temps for drop at their birth — the print-temp leak class (5 producer sites, one doctrine)"*. Verified both sides with the committed repro: parent `43a58519` prints **`x`** (write silently LOST, but memory-SAFE); `a367f2c3` **double-frees**. Confirmed still bad at 2026-06-12, 2026-06-27 and HEAD; clean at 2026-05-27. So the shape was never CORRECT — it has always dropped the write — but it degraded from wrong-output to memory-unsafe at that commit. ⚠ **The instructive part: the commit is a correct application of Core #3 (register ownership at the value's birth) that over-registers in THIS shape** — the loop-element slot and the freshly-built String temp end up both owning the buffer. A fix for the UAF must not simply revert it or the print-temp leak class returns. ⚠ **WHY IT SURVIVED 6.5 WEEKS:** no fixture exercises `for e in &Vector[String]: e = e + "..."` — the same no-coverage-existed story as the `&`-write-through regression, and the reason this fixture is now committed. Bisect is reproducible: build at `a367f2c3^` and `a367f2c3` and run `tests/fixtures/security/sound_loop_string_elem_assign_double_free.gg`. **Likely mechanism (NOT chased to the writer site — do not treat as established):** the element binding is a borrow into the collection's storage, and a whole-slot assignment stores the new buffer without releasing the old one, so slot and temp co-own it; the struct/Vector cells survive because their mutations are in-place through the borrow rather than whole-slot assignments. **DISTINCT from the two filed neighbours** — the `int`-element write loss (D33's second open item) and the comprehension int-to-pointer warning are LOST WRITES; this is heap corruption. ⚠ **CORRECTION (Family-1 round, measured): that `int`-element cell is NOT "the Family-1 projected-TYPE bug"** — the same mis-attribution this file carried at the D33 entry, corrected there too. The for-loop element face is **TYPE-INDEPENDENT** (`Vector[int]` → `1` and `Vector[String]` → `q`; both sides of the resource/value split lose the write on the nested-`&`-call shape), whereas Family-1's whole discriminator is that 4 of 10 types worked. Both survived the Family-1 chokepoint unchanged. ⚠ Note also that this entry's own axis table and the D33 cell describe DIFFERENT shapes of the for-loop family and must not be merged: the **direct-assignment** shape (`for e in &a: e = e + "!"`) is this entry — write lost for `int`, heap-UAF for `String`, and struct/`Vector[int]` elements write through CORRECTLY — while the **nested-`&`-call** shape (`for x in &a: bumpi(&x)`) loses the write on every type. Two shapes, different symptoms, one family. Repro: `tests/fixtures/security/sound_loop_string_elem_assign_double_free.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_loop_string_elem_assign_double_free` (RED-verified under ASan). ⚠ ggdef cannot adjudicate this class at all (Core #13) — ASan is the instrument.

- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — check-passes / debug-ICE / RELEASE SILENT MISCOMPILE; found by the R42 Track-D brief-review pass 6, orchestrator-verified 2026-08-15] Indexing a TYPE NAME in expression position — `int a = int[2]` — passes `gg check` ("OK: no semantic errors"), then the DEBUG build panics in IR lowering (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs` consuming-position arm: `debug_assert!(false, …)` + `Operand::Constant(Unit)` — re-derive the line with `grep -n "debug_assert!(false" src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs`) and the RELEASE build SILENTLY MISCOMPILES (prints `0`).** Core #10 (check must reject what lowering cannot lower) + Core #8 (debug-ICE vs release-silent-wrong is ≥2 defects; the `debug_assert!(false)`+Unit-fallback pattern is itself the lower-or-reject violation). Intended: check-time reject, `E_NotIndexable` family (`int` is a type, not an indexable value); distinct from the two filed `E_NotIndexable` MESSAGE-TEXT entries (those are Set/Map accessor ergonomics — this is a missing REJECTION). Pre-existing at HEAD `89c54c825`; surfaced by Track-D's speculation probes, unrelated to the paren work. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/check_passes_type_index_miscompile.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `check_rejects_type_index` (RED-verified at HEAD: check succeeds where the test asserts the reject). Sibling question for the fixer (Core #4): what OTHER type-name-in-value-position shapes reach lowering — `String[0]`, user types, generic instantiations? Enumerate before fixing the instance.

- **🐛 A `Callable`-typed `&`-PARAMETER ICEs the compiler (filed 2026-07-26).** `gg check` passes, then `gg build` panics: "GIR validation errors: function @with_it, bb0: call to undefined function @cb" (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:1737`). The indirect-call lowering does not recognise `cb` as a callable local when it arrives as a `Callable[..] &` PARAMETER, so it emits a direct call to a function named `cb`. **DISTINCT from the FIVE already-filed `Callable` defects — and the discriminator is the PANIC SITE, not the shape.** Full census of what is already filed (⚠ an earlier version of this entry said "the two siblings", which was a selection, not an enumeration — Core #15e Q3): (1) Callable-typed LOCAL bound to an `&`-param fn -> **SEGFAULT** at runtime; (2) PROJECTION arg through a bare Callable PARAM -> **SEGFAULT**; (3) Callable-in-enum-payload `E.Wrap(!f)` -> panic at **`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs` Tier-2a consume-site validator (re-derive: `grep -n "consume site" src/ir/lowering/mod.rs`)** (Tier 2a consume-site validator); (4) bare callable identifier at a collection-put `v.push(f)` -> panic at **`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs` Tier-2a consume-site validator (re-derive: `grep -n "consume site" src/ir/lowering/mod.rs`)**; (5) the single-owner carve-out missing at 3 of 6 consuming positions -> panic. THIS one panics at **`mod.rs:1737`** (GIR *validation*, "call to undefined function"), a site none of the five reach — (1) and (2) get all the way to the backend, and (3)-(5) die in the consume-site validator. So a fix for either existing family may leave this cell live. ⚠⚠ **ROOT ESTABLISHED 2026-07-27 (supersedes this entry's earlier "possibly the same root as the sigil-ignoring census" hedge — IT IS NOT).** The dispatch in `src/ir/lowering/exprs/calls.rs` has exactly two indirect arms, one keyed on `local_type_id == UNIT_TYPE` (→ `__callable_N`) and one on `GirType::FnPtr` (→ `__gorget_closure_call_N`). A `Callable[..] &` PARAMETER is **neither**: measured with `gg build --emit-gir`, `int with_it(Callable[int(&int)] &cb, …)` lowers to `fn @with_it(*mut unit, …)` with `_1: *mut unit ; cb`. Both arms test the local's type WITHOUT peeling the pointer, so both miss and the call falls through to the ordinary direct-call path on the parameter's NAME → `@cb` undefined. **This is structurally the SAME SHAPE as the `Guard[T]`-through-a-parameter root** (a dispatch test applied before the `pointee_type` deref), which is the answer to the handover's open scout question — the FIX SITES differ (guard: `guard_inner_suffix` vs `current_type`; here: the two arms in `calls.rs`), but the mistake is one class, so brief them with that framing. Verification command: `gg build --emit-gir tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_callable_amp_param_ices.gg` (it panics after printing, so read the GIR from a variant whose body does not call `cb`). **Decide:** (a) the shape is meaningful → lower as an indirect call, prints 11/11; or (b) `Callable` may not take `&` at all (single-owner carve-out, borrowed at calls) → check-time reject. The ICE is wrong under both (Core #10: `gg check` must not pass what the lowering panics on). Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_callable_amp_param_ices.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_callable_amp_param_ices` (RED-verified: panics as described).

- **🆕🐛💥 [HIGH — TWO CELLS REMAIN post-Track-K; MEMORY-UNSAFE, `gg check` ACCEPTS; measured 2026-07-28] Non-identifier-callee class RESIDUAL after Track K.** Track K retired the DIRECT-SUBSCRIPT cells (`arr[0](&a)` and `d["k"](&a)`) by extending `GirType::FnPtr` with `param_ownerships`, populating `TypeMapper::callable_alias_sigs` at `register_callable_inner_if_any`, teaching `infer_collection_element_type` to look up the sig, and routing the non-identifier arm's args through `lower_call_arg`. Live regression fixtures: `callable_amp_arr_indexed_callee.gg`, `callable_amp_dict_indexed_callee.gg`, `callable_bang_arr_indexed_callee.gg` (mixed `&`+`!` sigils). ⚠ ggdef structurally cannot adjudicate this class (Core #13 — memory-invalidation is invisible to value-semantics adjudication); ASan on the real backends is the instrument. **TWO CELLS REMAIN, both `#[ignore]`d with durable repros, distinct root sites from the retired cells:** (a) **RETURNED-CALL-RESULT** — `make_bumper()(&a)` where `make_bumper()` returns `Callable[void(int &)]` from a FUNCTION CALL (not an identifier). Fix requires teaching `infer_operand_type_full` at `calls.rs:2191` to consult the callee-function's return-type metadata for a full-sig FnPtr rather than the current Named alias. Currently blocked upstream by (1) closure-literal `&`-param parse-error (`known_gaps/sound_closure_amp_param_write_body_parse_error.gg`) AND (2) return-of-callable-value `E_UnresolvedBorrowOrigin`; pinned at `known_gaps/sound_callable_amp_returned_callee_segv.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_callable_amp_returned_callee_segv`. (b) **METHOD-CALL-RESULT** — `arr.get(0)(&a)` on `Vector[Callable[void(int &)]]` (and the analogous `d.get(k)(&a)` on `Dict[String, Callable[void(int &)]]` — both shapes SEGV post-fix, measured 2026-07-28). Method-call result-type inference doesn't route through `callable_alias_sigs` the way the container-element inferrer now does. Fix: route method-inference through `callable_alias_sigs`. Pinned at `known_gaps/sound_callable_amp_shared_get_call_segv.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_callable_amp_shared_get_call_segv` (Vector representative; Dict shape shares the fix).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — SEVENTH `Callable` costume, and the only one that fails at LINK; `gg check` ACCEPTS; measured 2026-07-27] Calling a `Callable`-typed STRUCT FIELD (`h.f(&a)`) is lowered as a USER METHOD CALL.** `struct Holder: Callable[void(&int)] f` + `h.f(&a)` → `gg check` clean, then `/usr/bin/ld: undefined reference to 'Holder__f'`. The emitted C declares `void Holder__f(void*, void*);` and calls it — note the TWO parameters: a `self` pointer plus the argument, i.e. the method-call ABI. **ROOT: the PARSE.** `h.f(...)` becomes a `MethodCall`, not an `Expr::Call` whose callee is a field access, so the entire indirect-call family is bypassed and the safety pass reaches `check_method_call_ownership` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs`) with no resolvable `DefId` and checks nothing. **DISCRIMINATOR vs the six filed siblings — the FAILURE STAGE** (enumeration taken from the `&`-PARAMETER entry above, which carries the full census): the two SEGFAULT costumes reach the backend and crash at runtime; the enum-payload and collection-put costumes panic in the consume-site validator (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs` Tier-2a consume-site validator (re-derive: `grep -n "consume site" src/ir/lowering/mod.rs`)), as does the missing single-owner carve-out; the `&`-param costume panics in GIR validation (`mod.rs:1737`); THIS one compiles and dies at LINK, a stage none of the others reach. A fix for any of them can leave it live. ⚠ **SECOND, SEPARABLE DEFECT IN THE SAME SHAPE:** the field's declared function type is not checked against what is stored in it — `Callable[void(int)] f` initialised with `bump` (`void(&int)`) is accepted silently, while the identical mismatch at a LOCAL binding correctly rejects `E_TypeMismatch: expected void(int), found void(&int)`. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_callable_struct_field_call_link_fail.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_callable_struct_field_call_link_fail` (RED-verified; declares the field with the MATCHING type so the link failure is isolated, and records the mismatch cell in its header).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg check` ACCEPTS, C backend then FAILS TO BUILD; found 2026-07-26 by sigil-prose gauntlet pass 15, parent-reproduced] `spawn child(&cfg)` passes check and does not compile.** `void child(Config &cfg): cfg.retries = …` + `spawn child(&cfg)` → `gg check` "OK: no semantic errors", then `gg build` fails with a raw C error: `incompatible type for argument 1 of '__gorget_spawn_child'` (expected `__gg_Config`, got `void *`). So a `&`-argument crossing `spawn` is **neither rejected nor compiled** — the spawn wrapper's parameter type does not account for the borrow. ⚠ This is the shape `book/14` §3.9 documents as the borrowed-ref-crossing-spawn error, and the reason that section's status note previously read "the compiler accepts it" — accepted by CHECK only. Distinct from the two dead-guard entries below: those are about a diagnostic that never fires; this is a lowering/ABI mismatch that surfaces as a C compile error. Likely a sibling of the filed spawn-wrapper items. Note the round's docs now distinguish "accepted by `gg check`" from "fails downstream" at this site. 🆕 **A COMMITTED REPRO FOR THE SIBLING SHAPE NOW EXISTS (Family-1 round):** `known_gaps/sound_amp_spawn_projection_write_lost.gg` pins the **projection** costume of this same face (`spawn f(&c.fd)` on an `int` field), which does NOT fail the C build — it compiles and silently drops the write, and after the Family-1 chokepoint it DIVERGES from the direct call (`110` vs `10`). Same root as this entry (`spawn.rs` never reads `arg.node.ownership`), different symptom by field type: `int` → silent write loss, `Vector` → silent lost `push`, `String`/struct → the C-build failure this entry files. Fix all three by routing the three spawn arg sites through `lower_call_arg`; see the consumption-limb census entry.

- **🐛 `E_BorrowAcrossAwait` HAS NO POSITIVE CONTROL EITHER — and the two dead guards PROP EACH OTHER UP (filed 2026-07-26).** `language-reference.md` §7.24 documents a borrow held across an `await` as an error; at HEAD both probed shapes (`&`-param across await; collection-element view across await) are ACCEPTED. Every reference to the variant in `safety/tests.rs` is a NEGATIVE assertion. ONE raise site (`safety/origins.rs:81`) gated on `await_invalidated`, whose ONE insert (`safety/check_expr.rs:853`) needs `BorrowOrigin::contains_local()` — shapes the StringView removal appears to have eliminated, the same root as the spawn sibling. ⚠⚠ **The filter gating that insert is justified by `check_expr.rs:838-840`: "This is sound because Change 1 (Spawn enforcement) prevents borrowed refs from reaching fire-and-forget spawns"** — i.e. it points at `E_SpawnWithBorrowedRef`, which has no positive control either. So the async guard justifies its own narrowness by naming an enforcer that does not demonstrably fire: Core #14 squared. **INVESTIGATE THE PAIR TOGETHER** — fixing or deleting either changes the argument for the other. Same fork as the sibling: (a) live but the shape was missed → NEG + positive control; or (b) the borrow model changed under it → delete the raise site, the variant, and `await_invalidated`, and rewrite §7.24. Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_borrow_across_await_never_rejected.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_borrow_across_await_never_rejected` (RED-verified).

- **🐛 `E_SpawnWithBorrowedRef` HAS NO POSITIVE CONTROL AND MAY BE A DEAD GUARD (filed 2026-07-26).** `docs/book/14-concurrency.md` §3.9 documents `spawn child(&cfg)` as rejected; at HEAD it is ACCEPTED. Four borrow shapes probed, all accepted: `&`-of-a-local, `&`-of-a-`&`-param, owned-String param, `&`-param collection. Every reference to the variant in `src/semantic/safety/tests.rs` is a NEGATIVE assertion (`!has_error`) — not one test asserts it FIRES — and two were named `..._rejected` while asserting acceptance (bodies explain: "With StringView removed, String params are owned, not borrowed"); renamed to `..._accepted`. Raise sites still exist (`helpers.rs:1695` args, `check_expr.rs:933` method receiver), so this is "no reachable shape found", NOT "code deleted". **Decide WHICH before fixing:** (a) guard is live and the shape was missed → keep the NEG test, add a positive-control unit test; or (b) escape-safety is subsumed by the closure capture/escape analysis (D34) → DELETE the raise sites + the variant and rewrite §3.9. Core #14 defect either way (a book-documented safety rule with nothing enforcing it). Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sound_spawn_borrowed_ref_never_rejected.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sound_spawn_borrowed_ref_never_rejected` (RED-verified: asserts the reject, gets acceptance). ⚠ **The four probed shapes are all NON-PROJECTION; a fifth, PROJECTION shape is now committed separately** (Family-1 round): `known_gaps/sound_amp_spawn_projection_write_lost.gg`, `spawn f(&c.fd)` — likewise accepted, and it demonstrates the CONSEQUENCE of the missing rejection concretely (the write is silently dropped, and now diverges from the direct call, which Family-1 fixed). Useful to decision (a) above: it is a reachable shape whose acceptance is observably unsound, which is evidence the guard is live-but-missed rather than subsumed. Note `safety/tests.rs:1984` had leaned its `param_str_across_await_ok` soundness argument on this guard — comment corrected. ⚠ **PAIRED with the `E_BorrowAcrossAwait` entry above**: that guard's own narrowness is justified by a comment naming THIS one as what makes it sound, so the two must be investigated together.

- **📄 `thread.spawn` IS A DOC FOSSIL — 6 sites across two docs (filed 2026-07-26).** `thread.spawn(...)` does not exist: `gg check` gives `E_UndefinedName: undefined name 'thread'`. Decide per site: rewrite to `thread_spawn` + a module-level-state shape, or drop the example. Grep: `grep -n "thread\.spawn" docs/language-design.md docs/language-reference.md`.

PRE-EXISTING (reproduces at base `9e308a5c`), BROADER than `??` (swallows unwrap/deref/any operator-on-wrong-type inside f-strings). **The self-host is already MORE correct** — its exhaustive `check_carrier_ops` walker recurses into `EFString`, so it REJECTS `f"{int ?? 5}"`. String}"` shape now surfaces. Sibling gates still missing from the whitelist are silently swallowed. **⚠ BLAST RADIUS IS WIDER THAN THIS ENTRY SAID (measured 2026-07-26): it swallows OWNERSHIP/sigil-gate errors too, so an f-string is a UNIVERSAL `gg check` bypass, not a carrier-op one.** `print(abs(&a))` -> `E_OwnershipMismatch`; `print(f"{abs(&a)}")` -> accepted, then C prints a raw pointer and `llc` hard-fails ("'%v2' defined with type 'ptr' but expected 'i64'") — Core #8. - **🆕 POST-CLOSURE ROUND FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-27, discovered by the GO #2 v2 + Rejection #1/#2 output-reviews):**
  - **🐛 Rust-gg method default-arg-fill bug (REFERENCE DEFECT, invariant #8 — own track, fix BOTH compilers).** Rust gg does NOT fill default arguments for equip METHODS at the call site: `equip P: int add(self, int a, int b = 2)` called as `p.add(5)` (omitting the defaulted `b`) → Rust `gg run`/`gg check` REJECTS with `error: wrong number of arguments: expected 2, found 1`. The SAME default on a FREE function (`int add(int a, int b = 2)` called `add(5)`) works (prints `7`). `p.add(5, 2)` (all explicit) → `17` correct. So the call should compile + use `b`'s default, not be rejected. Reference defect in `src/` (method-call default-fill missing) + presumably the self-host mirrors/diverges — scout BOTH, fix BOTH, add a fixture. NOT bounded (touches Rust method-call lowering). Repro confirmed by review `a11f57374c`.

- **🎨 CAST-VIA-CONSTRUCTION LANGUAGE REDESIGN (owner-approved DESIGN 2026-06-20; impl deferred) — RFC `the cast-via-construction RFC (git history)`. ⚠ §7+§8 are the AUTHORITATIVE resolution layer (supersede §3–§6); read those, not the old §3–§4 sketch.** Retire `as`, `Into`, `TryFrom`, `TryInto`: conversion IS construction. `T(x)` is the sole spelling; the `From` registry survives UNDER THE HOOD (a 1-arg ctor `Self(T)` auto-registers it — `From` = the capability name + the generic-bound name, §7.4 Seam B). Fallibility = the `throws` effect, TYPE-level (widening ctor = non-`throws`, narrowing = `throws`) — NO value-flow analysis. **RESOLVED 2026-06-20 (§7.3):** (1) narrowing **overflow → THROW** (recoverable; off the strict-panic stance) — arithmetic overflow is a FAULT not a contract error, see error-model doc; (2) **CORRECTED 2026-06-22:** flavors + float→int modes are **NAMED-ARG CONSTRUCTORS** (`byte(clamping=x)`/`int(truncating=f)`/`int(rounding=f)`) — type=callee, mode=static label (Swift's `UInt8(clamping:)`); the 2026-06-20 source-method detour (`x.clamped(byte)`) was REVERSED (it conflated a static arg-LABEL with a runtime mode-PARAM AND put a type in value-arg position); (3) value→String is **`String(T)`** (= `Displayable.display()`, infallible), `str(x)` stays rejected. **Seams (§7.4):** float→int has no bare `int(f)` form (mode-labeled construction `int(rounding=f)` etc.); generics relocate the From/TryFrom distinction to bound-form (not deleted). **§8:** 9-language survey — Into/Try* dissolve (Into = From-backwards; TryFrom only exists because Rust's From can't fail — a `throws` ctor IS the "Try"); no silent-lossy default (5-language lesson); the `throws` annotation = C#'s implicit/explicit (no new keyword). Remaining work = implementation only; both-compilers + spec + book + negative-fixtures, staged per §6; the EAs `as` fix (parity #1) built the reusable `GICast`→LIR machinery. Own scout→brief→reviews when funded.
- **🧭 ERROR-MODEL LANGUAGE DIRECTION (owner brainstorm 2026-06-20; DRAFT — SCOUT + 3 SEQUENTIAL REVIEWS DONE, clean SIGN OFF `999a54a1`; several owner decisions folded) — `the error-model design notes (git history)`.** Spawned by the cast RFC's arithmetic-overflow sub-decision. Model: value channel + a typed error channel; error TYPES classified **contract** (parse/IO/conversion — mandatory-handle, on the API surface) vs **fault** (overflow/bounds/div0/OOM). Core argument = the IMPOSSIBILITY (§3): recoverable-default-overflow + informative-row + universal-typed = pick two → faults MUST be a non-contract kind. **OWNER DECISIONS (2026-06-21):** (1) **§0.5 reversal ACCEPTED** but ADDITIVE — overflow STAYS panic-by-default (safety rationale intact for the uncaught case); we ADD opt-in recovery, so docs get "panics by default AND catchable," not a full overturn. (2) **Q14 RESOLVED** — faults = a structured closed **`Fault` enum** (never String), the "typed/untyped" framing was a conflation of value-repr (A) / in-signature (B) / static-exhaustive-catch (C); decided A=enum, B=out-of-signature, C=dynamic-match. (3) **§9.1 phased (BOTH, PHASED)** — TYPING vs PROPAGATION are orthogonal; **PHASE 1** = `Fault equip Error` (unified `catch` for both kinds) + panic-by-default + **LOCAL-only catch** (`(a*b) catch Overflow: …` lowers to a checked-op-branch-to-handler = the existing `--overflow=checked` op, no unwind, no ubiquity) + exhaustiveness-via-implicit-panic-default; **⭐ Phase 1 sidesteps EVERY unwind-dependent blocker** (B2 greenfield-unwind, Q9 drop-across-unwind, Q15 FFI, Q16 main-boundary, §3.1 partial-state — all PHASE 2). **PHASE 2** = deep/boundary (server-keeps-serving) catch, needs the greenfield unwind infra, strictly additive over Phase 1. Precedents: Zig/Koka/Swift-6/Midori/Erlang/Java-checked (all accurate per scout). **Open (§9 Q1–Q16 + §9.1):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow three-way split (wraps silently today `meta.rs:1278-1280`), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation, full fault set, self-host parity. **§1/§4 "one channel/two kinds" framing KEPT (owner-decided 2026-06-21, "minimal clarify"); a §4 clarification + neutralized-forward-notes landed `4a785ef8`.** Touches the WHOLE language — Phase 1 is the small, shippable increment; the self-host (wall-to-wall arithmetic) needs a parity story first. **PHASE-1 SPEC (§11) REVIEWED 2026-06-21: 4 fresh SEQUENTIAL passes → clean SIGN OFF (`29f6357b`); briefable.** Central impl item = a **NEW LIR checked-faultable-op-with-handler-branch shape in BOTH backends** (no existing trap to re-point — all checks trap inline at C/LLVM emit, after drop passes; model the branch as GIR/LIR CFG like `lower_catch_expr` so drops stay correct). Phase-1 faults = Overflow+DivByZero (inline) + Bounds via `gorget_array_safe_get`+NULL-branch (brief decides Phase 1 vs 1.5); OOM→Phase 2. Also: `Fault equip Error` (3 synth methods display/debug/source), closed-`Fault` scrutinee + panic-default match rule, catch-on-non-throwing = NEW AST/grammar/typecheck form (don't perturb contract `catch`), per-expr overflow-mode override, handler-bb constructs the `Fault` variant. **FINAL pre-impl gate PASSED 2026-06-21 (`a9b212c7`): plan+code review + self-host scout + a confirming pass → clean SIGN OFF, IMPLEMENTATION-READY for both backends.** Final review caught + fixed a stale contradiction (§3.1/§9 Q1 asserted blanket "never inline" — now carved out: boundary-only = Phase-2 deep; Phase-1 local catch is safe, branch-before-store). **SELF-HOST (§11.8, scout-verified): NO prerequisites to fix — the key dependency is already satisfied (self-host emits CHECKED arithmetic today, per-instruction overflow field, `lower_catch_expr` CFG template, enum/trait construction all present); Rust-first regresses NO self-host gate (own source keeps panic-default); self-host fast-follows with the SAME new LIR shape + a greenfield fault-catch AST/typecheck path (`infer.gg` has no ECatch handler) + the `map_binop` unknown-op→OP_ADD footgun to guard.** Sequencing (§11.7): shared LIR shape FIRST → one backend → typecheck → second backend → fixtures; the overflow-mode override is ADDITIVE (don't mutate the `overflow_wrap` global). **ERROR-MODEL — SUPERSEDED BY DECISION BATCH 5 (2026-07-11 ledger LOG): the fault-catch frontier is CLOSED** — D25 removes fault-catch entirely (self-host Inc2, `Fault equip Error`, Phase-2 deep/boundary catch all cancelled; deep catch rejected on the merits, boundary containment = D24's Task-join `TaskFault`, OOM rides D24/phase-3; the fault-participation machinery is deleted by the Batch-C2 track). **SURVIVING open §9 Qs (unaffected by the fault redesign, each still needs an owner ruling eventually):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow split (`meta.rs:1278` — partly covered by D18), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation.

The whole corpus incl. the migrated self-host is already full-strict-clean, so the check should land at zero migration cost — a Core-#9 lagging-lane note until then.

#### 🆕 D31 FULL-STRICT FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-07-20 by the D31 ADDENDUM-2 executor; the full-strict flip itself LANDED — record in DONE.md)
- **[MED — DX tooling, the ADDENDUM-2 rider] `gg fmt`-adjacent auto-insertion of the missing call-site sigil.** The `E_OwnershipMismatch` message is now kind-aware + carries the exact-span fix ("add `!` here" / "add `&` here" / "remove the `!`"), so the edit is machine-actionable. - **[LOW — pre-existing, zero blast radius, Core #8-adjacent] `&` on a TEMPORARY into a `&` param is accepted but is a dead write.** Probed 2026-07-20: production ACCEPTS `f(&Tok(1))` / `c.m(&Ctor())` (found==expected==MutableBorrow, so the strict check passes) — but a write-through to a temporary has no owner to observe it (a dead write). The ledger's ADDENDUM-2 rider expects a REJECT. Blast radius is ZERO (grep of corpus+lib+self-host: 0 `&<ctor/call>` sites), so it was FILED rather than landed with the flip (adding a new reject class + message + NEG + ggdef mirror for a zero-site pre-existing acceptance was out of the flip's scope). Recommendation: reject in `check_call_ownership` + `check_method_call_ownership` when `found==MutableBorrow && !expr_is_place(arg)`, with a "cannot mutably borrow a temporary — bind it to a local first" message; mirror in ggdef; one NEG fixture. NOT introduced by D31 (pre-existing).

Add arity to builtin method decls + convert the `lir/validate.rs` arity panic (`:253`/`:137`) into a clean `WrongArgCount`. Independent; can ship separately.

  - **Safe order:** (1) enumerate builtin methods into the checker (b2+b3) — give `builtin_method_type` a "exists but type unknown" return or a `builtin_method_exists` set; (2) make `error_id` receivers stay silent (b1); (3) resolve `via`; (4) ONLY THEN widen the gate. `self_host_runtime`/`bootstrap_fixed_point` are the canaries (go red on `String.clone` alone).

- **🐛 Self-host lowerer driver OMITS a borrow-check pass** (scout agent af99281f). Pipeline is parse→resolve→typecheck→lower→validators — NO borrow-check. A move-then-use of a named local that Rust correctly REJECTS (`use of moved value`) is SILENTLY lowered to a leaking MOVE (LSan-confirmed; latent UAF), no diagnostic. The CoW *lowering* is correct (the `Some(name.clone())` idiom is language-MANDATED, must STAY). The gap is purely the MISSING DIAGNOSTIC GATE: port/wire a borrow-check pass into the self-host driver. Only bites invalid programs (lower urgency than a miscompile).

- **Safety-pass branch-divergence audit (Cluster C, post-Snag-#39 audit). Defer.** Snag #39's fix added `save_branch_state`/`restore_branch_state` around `Expr::Catch`/`Expr::Rethrow`. No other actively-leaking sites (`DefaultOp`/`And/Or`/`OptionalChain` checked clean — `self.diverged` is only set by `Return/Throw/Break/Continue`). If divergence tracking is extended to noreturn-call expressions (with `panic`/`exit` typed-Never work), audit these. Also consider upgrading Catch/Rethrow from save+restore to save+merge.

- **`caller_location()` builtin + multi-frame stack walking [LOW].** (a) a `#[track_caller]`-like attribute / implicit `caller_location()` so helpers inherit the call site without manual `line, col` plumbing (typed sidecar on `CallExtern`/`Call` carrying caller span, read by the builtin). (b) full backtrace needs DWARF/libunwind in the C backend or LLVM stackmap; async/spawned-task panic locations need the spawning call's span carried through spawn context. Both multi-week; defer until v1 is battle-tested.

- **Result→T auto-propagation — retire the residual consumer-side `maybe_auto_propagate` safety nets.** Producer-side centralization done (`lower_expr` auto-applies on Call/MethodCall + typechecker gates for if/while/elif/index; Snag #49 holdouts closed). The 7 remaining consumer-side calls (`stmts/assigns.rs:112`, `stmts/mod.rs:129/317/1422`, `calls.rs:151/1030/1065/1206`, `methods.rs:224`, `exprs/mod.rs:1788/1823/1854`) exist for the *Identifier-of-Result* case (`Result[T,E] r = ...; T x = r;`) — the producer hook can't fire on identifiers without breaking `body_r.unwrap()` (the receiver MUST stay `Result`). Principled fix = plumb the typechecker's `expr_types` (post-auto-prop type per span) through monomorphization into IR-lowering, check `op_type` vs `expr_types[span]` at `lower_expr` exit, auto-prop only on disagreement in the throws-sugar direction → retire all 7. Estimate 1-2 days (mostly the monomorphization span-plumbing).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — module-loader leniency, found by the CoW-1A output-review probe 2026-07-17] Importing ANY name from a module silently exposes ALL of that module's names.** `lower_expr.gg` called `for_iterable_mode` (defined in `lower_loops.gg`) WITHOUT it appearing in the `from lower_loops import …` list, and the build succeeded — the loader resolves unimported names once any name from the module is imported. Accepts what the import system implies should be rejected (an unimported-name use should be `E_UndefinedName`); it also lets import lists silently rot. Fix in the loader/resolver's import-visibility check + a negative fixture; sweep existing `.gg` sources for other unimported-name uses that the leniency currently masks (each is a latent build break). The 1A remediation added the missing import explicitly.

- **🆕 [D10(b) self-root follow-up, filed 2026-07-14 — OWNER QUESTION] For-loop iterator-invalidation is ROOT-granular for BOTH self and locals — adopt field-path disjointness?** `check_expr.rs:501` (`for_loop_iterables.contains(&recv_def_id)`) compares only the ROOT DefId, discarding the projection path — so `for x in c.a: c.b.push(…)` over-rejects DISJOINT fields (unlike the projection-aware call-arg check `check_call_aliasing`, which uses `find_root_def_id_with_path` + `paths_overlap`). Affects `self` AND identifier roots IDENTICALLY (the self-root fix made self consistent with this pre-existing local behavior, not worse). Should the for-loop check adopt the same field-path disjointness (reuse `find_root_def_id_with_path` + `paths_overlap`) so `for x in c.a: c.b.push()` is accepted? Own scout; measure the in-repo blast radius first (self-root scout found 0 in-repo `for … in self.<field>` sites — all iterate method-call results).
- **🆕 [D10(b) self-root follow-up, filed 2026-07-14 — OWNER QUESTION] Moving a field out of a bare (borrowed) `self` (`take(!self.items)`) stays accepted.** A partial-move-out-of-borrowed-self case — related to the D12/partial-move family and the undecided destructuring-partial-move widening (2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM). Separate from the self-root place-overlap fix; needs an owner ruling on whether/how partial self-moves are checked. Own scout when the partial-move family is worked.
- **🆕 [D10(b) B1 follow-up, filed 2026-07-14] Disjoint-sibling MOVE `f(!m.a, !m.b)` over-rejects — undecided partial-move widening.** Moving two DISJOINT sibling sub-places today trips `E_UseAfterMove` because the move-tracker root-marks the whole DefId `m` on the first `!m.a`. Whether this SHOULD accept (Rust-style destructuring partial move) is the WIDENING left UNDECIDED by the 2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM (`decisions.md`). D10(b) place-overlap deliberately keeps `(Move,Move)` out (E_DoubleMove/E_UseAfterMove own it), so this is NOT B1's call. Fixture documents the question: `tests/fixtures/place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d test `place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error`. Needs an OWNER RULING; if widening is ruled in, flip the fixture to a build+run POSITIVE and relax the projection-move root-marking to path-precise.
- **🆕 [D23/T3a follow-up, filed 2026-07-10, MEDIUM-design] General must-use on `Result`.** D23/T3a pins ONLY that an unhandled `throws` call in a CONSUME position rejects (`E_UnhandledThrows`). It does NOT catch a `Result` VALUE that is captured then ignored — `auto r = throws_call()` bound but never matched, or a `Result`-returning fn's value discarded even inside a `throws` fn. Whether Gorget wants a general `#[must_use]`-style must-use-on-`Result` (warn or error on a dropped `Result`) is a separate language-design question — deliberately OUT of D23 scope (owner Q4 ruling). Design + owner-decide before implementing; pairs with the error-model residuals.
- **⚖️ [language-design question, from P1-A §10.3 fold 2026-07-06 — route to decisions.md open queue at the next owner batch] Return-position §10.3 capture asymmetry.** Block-body `return throws_call()` into a Result-declared return type CAPTURES (production), but the IDENTICAL expr-body tail is REJECTED (`type mismatch: expected Result[…], found int` — probes g/f3, durable at /tmp/recover_p1a_r1probe/). ggdef mirrors production (loud ElabError on the expr-body shape). Decide: expr-body should capture too (uniformity — an expr-body is sugar for `return <expr>`) or reject is correct; whichever is ratified → fix production + ggdef + language-reference §10.3 write-through + fixtures.

- **🐛 [SILENT MISCOMPILE — D20 RATIFIED 2026-07-06, ruling settled: reject with the f-string fix-it; fold with the to_string fix-it Low entry + the language-design.md:80 doc fix into ONE small D20 track] `String(3)` check-passes, builds, and prints an EMPTY string** — but `docs/language-reference.md:3224` says `String(value)` IS a compile-time error ("use an f-string"). The `to_string` rejection is complete but this sibling constructor path slipped through. Fix: reject `String(<non-string>)` at check with the f-string fix-it (same class as the A7 batch-4 item). Probes: /tmp/recover_scout_resources/q4e,q4g.

- **🧹 [round-33 alloc scout filing — DIRECTION RATIFIED 2026-07-06 as D13 TWO-STEP (decisions.md LOG): step 1 = REJECT bare allocator locals at check + docs rewrite, briefable now (resources-scout evidence /tmp/recover_scout_resources/); step 2 = full RAII drop-registration w/ borrow_deps ordering, filed target. The 'Decide:' below is DECIDED.] Bare (non-`with`) allocator locals are never destroyed — guaranteed leak class.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` outside `with` is lowered via plain `call_extern` (no drop registration, `calls.rs` allocator-ctor branches) and there is no scope-exit destroy → ASan LeakSanitizer reports 80B handle + 4096B block (regenerate: bare-arena probe with either Vector or String `alloc=`; identical shape both). PRE-EXISTING for collections (the docs' own §15.3 `alloc=` example uses a bare PoolAllocator). Also NOTE: at arena_depth 0 the escape gate doesn't track bare-allocator-backed values (`check_stmt.rs:194` gates on `arena_depth > 0`), so today the leak is what makes the bare idiom "safe". Decide: drop-register allocator handles (destroy at scope exit + extend escape tracking to bare allocators) or document the leak as the bare-idiom contract. **ESCALATION (round-33 allocator-class scout, 2026-07-02, ASan-verified): not just a leak — manual `.destroy()` of a bare allocator BEFORE scope exit while an RAII value backed by it is still live is a heap-UAF from safe code.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` + `String s = String("…long…", alloc=a)` + `a.destroy()` → the scope-exit drop of `s` frees through the destroyed arena → ASan heap-use-after-free in `gorget_string_free` (the plain run prints fine — silent). The decide-branch must also cover destroy ordering: drop-registration with ordering fixes both; merely documenting the leak contract does NOT fix this one. (The round's `allocator_ctor_alloc_keyword.gg` fixture uses the with-idiom specifically to sidestep this.)

- **🐛 LATENT: `is_collection_assignment` accepts ANY array/set literal regardless of ELEMENT type.** `is_collection_assignment` (`src/semantic/typecheck.rs:3800`) returns true for any `ResolvedType::Array(_,_)` against a `Vector`/`Dict`/`Set`/`HashMap`/`HashSet` dest WITHOUT checking the element type → `Vector[int] v = ["a", "b"]` + `Vector[int] f(): return ["a", "b"]` both pass `gg check` then misbehave. Pre-existing in VarDecl; the Return/Assign extension inherits the laxity (doesn't introduce it). Fix = unify (or check-compatible) the literal's element type against the dest's declared element type. One helper; affects VarDecl + Return + Assign.

- **`Ordinal` built-in trait never registered → wrong-signature `equip X with Ordinal:` compiles clean** (latent validation gap). `builtin_traits` carries `("Ordinal", …)` with `int ordinal(self)` (`src/semantic/traits.rs:603`), but registration is gated on `scopes.lookup(name)` (`traits.rs:824`) and `Ordinal` is absent from the resolver's reservation list (`src/semantic/resolve.rs:127`) + no `lib/` declares `trait Ordinal` → the entry is dropped, the registry never holds an Ordinal `TraitInfo` → `equip X with Ordinal:` falls to the inherent-impl path (`trait_def_id=None`), signature NEVER validated (`@derive(Ordinal)` desugars to one, `derive.rs:87`). Repro: wrong-sig `equip Color with Ordinal:` → "OK"; same on `Comparable` is correctly rejected. Fix: add `"Ordinal"` to `resolve.rs:127` (cheapest), OR declare `trait Ordinal` in a lib. Both book (`appendix-traits.md:92`) + spec (`language-reference.md:~2705`/`~2783`) document it as real.

- **Compiler footguns (latent type-system validation gaps, same class as `Ordinal`):**
  - **(C3) Phantom static methods pass `gg check`, fail at C build/link.** `String.from("x")`/`int.from(5)`/`int.try_parse(s)` all report "OK" but `gg build` fails (implicit-decl / undefined-reference / phantom symbol). Root: `resolve_static_method_type` (`typecheck.rs:4717`, called `:1740`) returns `None` for an unknown `Type.method()` + the call FALLS THROUGH → the backend name-mangles into a nonexistent runtime symbol (rule-2 violation). Fix: emit a `NoSuchMethod`/`UnknownStaticMethod` diagnostic at typecheck. Canonical working: `String("x")`, `int.parse(s)`→Option, `parse_int(s)`→Result.
  - **`.unwrap()`/`.expect()`/`.unwrap_or()` on a NON-Option/Result is a silent no-op pass-through** (`methods.rs:588`, the `matches!(method_name, "unwrap"|"expect"|"unwrap_or")` arm returns the receiver unchanged). A genuine type confusion (e.g. `channel_exec(...).unwrap()` on a plain `CommandResult`) is silently swallowed. Fix (design call; verify no stdlib/self-host relies on the no-op first): make it a typecheck error or at minimum a lint.

- **Drop the imported-module typecheck-error truncate (writer-site fixes).** `check_items_recursive_tc` (`typecheck.rs`) truncates ALL errors from `Item::Module` to silence foreign-scope false positives. Fix (a) shipped (BinaryOp threads `decl_type_hint` for byte-literals). What's left blocking removal (a diagnostic run with truncate disabled surfaced these — none match the brief's (b)/(c)):
  - **Stale self-host code missing newer enum variants** at non-exhaustive `match` sites in `self_host_{parser,resolver,typechecker,lowerer,check}/{parser,resolve,format_gir,lower}.gg`: `EFString`/`ETry`/`EMove`/`EMutableBorrow`/`EAs`/`EAwait`/`ESpawn`/`ESpawnBlocking`/`ERethrow`/`ECatch` (Expr); `SMeta`/`SAssertReturn`/`SSnapshot`/`SMetaForMatch`/`SWith`/`SAssert`/`SNamedScope`/`SSelect`/`SOnError`/`SItem` (Stmt); `KwCatch`/`KwOn`/`KwSnapshot`; `GTNone`. Add catch-alls/handling across all 5 dirs.
  - **Real bug:** `no field 'span' found on type 'SpannedToken'` in `self_host_{parser,resolver}/parser.gg:2512`/`2485`.
  - **(b)/(c) NOT OBSERVED** — the self-host parser.gg has no `throws` functions; `eval.gg:1528`-style mismatches are in gorget-js code. Re-survey against current code before fixing.
  - Then: retire the stale self-host code + fix the SpannedToken.span access, THEN remove the truncate (`errors.truncate(error_count)` ~typecheck.rs:6071 + the `hard_errors` field + `hard_type_mismatch` call sites ~1364-1394). These aren't compiler gaps — fix the self-host code, don't perma-mask.

- **🆕 [LOW — self-host over-reject, DORMANT + UNREACHABLE; from the flip-tracks landing 2026-07-17] The self-host EStructLiteral single-owner-init arm lacks production's ref-typed-field skip** (`check_expr.rs:1183` `!target_is_ref` skips a `Ref[Callable]` field init — a borrow, not a copy). This arm rejects ALL args with no ref-field gate → would OVER-REJECT the exotic `S{cbref: f}` (cbref borrow-typed). Over-reject-only (never UAF); the arm is also structurally UNREACHABLE today (the parser never builds EStructLiteral — `S(...)` is ECall, `{...}` a dict/set). The per-field ref-flag mirror (`compute_struct_field_ref_flags`) is deferred. Note is at the EStructLiteral safety-walk arm in `typecheck.gg`.
- **🧹 [resources scout 2026-07-06, LOW] `to_string`/`String(int)` rejections need a FIX-IT** pointing at the canonical `f"{n}"` (and `.display()`). Today `E_NoMethodFound` is generic. Pairs with the A7 batch-4 ruling (reject-with-fix-it; f-strings outnumber alternatives ~97× in the corpus).

- **🔵 TAIL-CALL OPTIMIZATION (TCO) — LATER (owner-set; a real language feature).** gg has no TCO. **Phase 1 (tractable, high-value): self-tail-recursion → loop** — detect a tail-position call to the SAME function (`return f(args)` returned DIRECTLY; ⚠ `return deep(n-1)+1` is NOT tail) THROUGH if/match/block tails, lower to param-rebind + jump-to-entry. Both compilers (Rust GIR/LIR + self-host `lower.gg`). **Phase 2 (defer): general/sibling tail calls** (`musttail`/trampoline). **Design Qs FIRST:** (a) GUARANTEED (Scheme-like, stack-safe — recommended given the safety pillar) vs best-effort; (b) ⚠ DROP/CoW ordering hazard — the tail jump REUSES the frame, so dead droppable locals must drop BEFORE the jump + a live droppable that outlives the call BLOCKS the TCO (UAF/leak otherwise); (c) the `assert return` postcondition runs AT the return. Non-tail deep recursion (`stack_guard_deep_recursion`) intentionally NOT covered (owner chose honest OS-default-stack). Gate: a tail-recursive fixture runs at 1e7+ depth on an 8MB stack; `fixed_point` GREEN; no ASan drop/leak regression.

- **🆕 [MEDIUM — same staleness family as R-A's Fix 1, from R-A pass-1 review 2026-07-10] `ast_type_to_resolved` binds stale Import placeholders in TYPE positions.** It uses value-first `lookup` (`src/semantic/types.rs:403`) and ACCEPTS `DefKind::Import` (`:413`) — so a cross-module imported trait in a type position binds the stale placeholder DefId, and e.g. `Box[ImportedTrait]`'s trait-object detection (`:424-433`, requires `kind == DefKind::Trait` at `:428`) silently fails cross-module. Same import-placeholder-staleness family as the R-A trait-registry keying fix, but a DIFFERENT read path — needs its own scout (what breaks: trait objects? equip resolution? generic bounds?) + fixtures. Own track.
