### Low

- **🆕 [LOW — diagnostic ergonomics follow-up from Round XXIX Track A close 2026-08-03] `E_NotIndexable` message text should name `.nth(i)`/`.key_at(i)` once those Set/Map accessors land.** D39 ratified `.nth(i)`/`.key_at(i)` as the named ordinal accessors for Set/Dict/HashMap/HashSet (`docs/define-gorget/decisions.md:1413`), but neither is implemented on the Set/Map builtins today (grep at HEAD: only `Iterator::nth` exists on the iterator side). The current `E_NotIndexable` message says *"use the collection's ratified named accessors (see D38/D39)"* — deliberately generic so the compiler doesn't advise code it rejects (the exact `sound_move_suggestion` / `E_EnumerateOnNonIterator` family trap). Update the message text to name `.nth(i)` / `.key_at(i)` concretely once those methods land on Set/Map. File: `src/semantic/errors.rs::NotIndexable::fmt`. Very small; ships alongside whatever round adds the accessors.
- **[LOW — A2-R2 M2 polish 2026-07-12] `E_MoveWithoutOperator` field/index sub-place message renders the ROOT name, not the exact sub-place.** `tainted_place_name` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs`) uses `find_root_def_id`, which returns only the ROOT binding's name — so a field/index sub-place (`hh.r`, `v[0]`) renders `hh.clone()` / `v.clone()` instead of `hh.r.clone()` / `v[0].clone()`. The A2-R2 M2 message sidesteps the inaccuracy ("copy the sub-place with `hh.clone()` … a bare `!` on a field/index sub-place is a partial move"), but the exact sub-place text is nicer. Fix = thread the place SPAN through `tainted_place_name` and slice the source (or have a place-printer walk the identifier/self/field/tuple/index chain). Not required by the capture-no-`!` GATE. Own scout.
- **✨ FEATURE — support forward / cross-module const-refs in a `const` initializer (fixpoint registration; Low)** (filed by the const-foldability round, 2026-06-25, DONE `316d818a`). A `const A = B + 1` where `B` is a const declared LATER (or imported, or a meta-const referenced from a `meta if` elif/else arm — the Track-9 re-review confirmed these ALL fold to 0 pre-fix, a pre-existing lowering gap now surfaced consistently at check time) currently REJECTS ("not a compile-time constant") because the lowering const-scan (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:546`) AND the matching typecheck pre-pass (`check_module_const_foldability`) fold in a SINGLE source-order pass — `B` isn't in `module_constants` when `A` is folded. Rejecting is correct-for-now (it used to silently miscompile to 0; reject is strictly better + the typecheck/lowering agree). To SUPPORT it: make the const-fold loop a FIXPOINT (iterate until no new const folds) in BOTH the lowering scan and the typecheck pre-pass (keep them mirrored — one source of truth). Then `const_forward_ref_error.gg` flips from reject to `11`; update it + its test. Pure ergonomics; no correctness gap today.

- **🌀 DEFERRED: lexical `wrapping { … }` scope** (filed when `--overflow` was retired 2026-06-21). After the flag retirement, `+%`/`-%`/`*%` (per-op) are the SOLE wrapping escape. A lexical `wrapping{}` block (scoped wrapping for a hot loop body, mirroring how `catch` is lexical) would be the ergonomic analogue — it lowers to the same `AddWrap`/`SubWrap`/`MulWrap` GIR ops (additive, no backend work). **NOT needed now:** `+%` suffices and the self-host self-compile runs fully checked and isn't overflow-bound (proof always-checked is acceptable). Add ONLY if measurement ever demands it; the self-host compile is the benchmark. Own small scout→brief→reviews if funded.

- **(Low/Medium, CORRECTNESS) Static-decl initializers are NOT type-checked against their declared type.** `public static int X = [1,2,3]` is accepted by `gg check` while the LOCAL form is rejected. Mostly latent, BUT Bug-B Stage 1 changed its symptom for collection-literal RHS (`initializer_needs_synthetic_fn` keys on RHS shape → garbage pointer + C warning). Real fix = add static-decl initializer type-checking (mirror the local var-decl check).

- **`@[no_alloc]` function annotation**: compiler error on allocating operations.

- **🆕 [LOW-MED — production OVER-REJECTION (false positive), from RV-D pass-1 2026-07-16] Production's match fall-through suppression keys ONLY on a separate `else_arm` (`check_stmt.rs:1217-1222`) — a bare unguarded `case _:`/`case x:` wildcard arm does NOT suppress it, so "moved-before + reinit in ALL arms incl. wildcard + use-after" is falsely REJECTED** (the arm is genuinely exhaustive). The RV-D-patched self-host is strictly more precise here (sound). Fix production to treat unguarded wildcard/binding arms as exhaustive (mirror the self-host's `match_has_catch_all`); dormant, no corpus hits. Pairs with the may-move §9.5 prose.
- **🧹 [coarse-kind Fable output-review 2026-07-16, LOW — diagnostic-cascade polish] Production emits a cascading second `E_AwaitNonFuture` on the double-await shape** (`await await g()`) where the self-host emits only the primary `E_DoubleAwait`. Both lanes reject; both diagnostics are true; purely a cascade-suppression nicety. Fixture `double_await_error.gg` is the probe.

- **🧹 [LOW — Track I v2, filed 2026-07-28] Widen `W_RecursiveBareParamMaterialize` to MUTUAL recursion.** v1 checks DIRECT self-recursion only (`resolve_callee_def_id(callee) == current_fn_def_id`). A mutual-recursion cycle (`f` calls `g`, `g` calls `f`) that carries a bare param through the cycle exhibits the same materialize-per-frame pathology but v1 misses it. Requires a call-graph pass in the safety checker (SCC on the fn graph, filter to SCCs of size > 1, then classify each cycle-member's params). Sequenced after the direct-self case has soaked in the corpus and the false-positive rate is measured. Files as a widening, not a v1 miss.

- **🧹 [LOW — Track I v2, filed 2026-07-28] `W_RecursiveBareParamMaterialize` downgrade when the reached callee already fires `W_NeedlessMutableBorrow`.** Post-emit filter: if Path Y routes through a callee whose `&self` (or `&`-param) never actually mutates, the callee itself fires `W_NeedlessMutableBorrow` and the recursive warning is a two-hop echo of that lie. Reviewing whether the twin firing is helpful (design lesson) or noisy (redundant); land the filter if the latter. Trivial to implement — post-process `stale_warnings` in `check_function`'s finish block. No corpus data on the twin-firing frequency today; harvest at first sighting.
## Backend / codegen

### High

- **🆕📐 [OWNER DIRECTIVE 2026-07-13 — standing] RETIRE sidecars + parallel structures (audit sweep).** "All opportunities to reduce sidecars and parallel structures should be taken." Upgrades layering rule 3 to a HUNT-AND-FOLD mandate (decisions.md standing directives + the CallArg LOG entry). AUDIT both compilers (Rust `src/` + self-host `.gg`) for index-aligned parallel vectors / hand-synced side-tables splitting a single logical fact — normalize into cohesive records/typed fields. The `CallArg{name,ownership,value}` normalization is the exemplar (retires the parallel names-vector). Known/suspected fossils: self-host `StructRegistry` parallel `Vector[String]`+`Vector[int]` scan; Dict-state-loss parallel-storage; `type_info_keys_safe` wrapper; assess `resolution_map`/side-tables (legitimate vs foldable). **🆕 (2026-07-27, filed by Track B1 A-2 output-review) — Rust `LoweringFuncState`'s three parallel `FxHashMap<LocalId, _>` sidecars for callable metadata: `callable_return_types` + `callable_param_types` + `callable_param_ownerships` (`src/ir/lowering/context.rs:198-216`). Track B1's `set_callable_sig` writer chokepoint (`context.rs:2904`) fused the WRITES (one call, six registration sites now go through it — 4 in `src/ir/lowering/functions.rs`, 1 in `lower_var_decl` at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:543`, and internally by callable's own registration), but the STORAGE remains three maps with public granular setters (`set_callable_return_type`, `set_callable_param_types`) — nothing structurally prevents a future site from populating one without the others. Reference-grade fold: bundle into one record type `CallableSig { ret: TypeId, params: Vec<TypeId>, ownerships: Vec<Ownership> }` (or similar) stored in a single `FxHashMap<LocalId, CallableSig>`, with `set_callable_sig` the ONLY public writer and the granular setters retired. Matches the same "extend an existing record over parallel sidecars" pattern D36's Q2 endorsed for `CallArg`.** Deliverable = ranked list (impact × risk) + fold recs; each fold its own gauntlet. Read-only scout first. **Do NOT conflate with systematic data-oriented SoA (the ECS track below) — that's the disciplined form, not this smell.**
- **🐛 [BACKEND DIVERGENCE — resources decision scout 2026-07-06] LLVM does not honor `alloc=` into a bare Arena the way C does.** The bare-allocator leak (q2a2) and the `.destroy()`-then-use UAF (q2b2) reproduce on C ASan but NOT on LLVM — LLVM appears not to route `Vector/String(alloc=a)` into the arena at all. Backends must be at parity (whichever semantics the A10 ruling picks). Probes: /tmp/recover_scout_resources/.
- **🆕🐛 [gorget-arena snag #1 — filed 2026-08-09, R39] LLVM backend drops the C typedef for FFI-only-module structs.** A plain Gorget `struct` defined in an FFI-only module (`struct` defs + `extern "C"` block + NO Gorget-body functions — e.g. `xtd.sdl`'s `SDLEvent`, `xtd.audio`'s `AudioMusic`) never gets its C typedef emitted by the LLVM backend's runtime shim (`__gorget_runtime_*.c`). Any shim-emitted signature that mentions such a struct fails to compile with `error: unknown type name 'GorgetSDLEvent'`. C backend fine unless the struct is constructed directly (Gorget `SDLEvent(...)`) — related-but-distinct defect, same root. **Verified triggers** (`tests/fixtures/known_gaps/gorget_arena_snag_1_llvm_ffi_only_typedef/`): `mini.gg` = LLVM fails / C ok; `ctor_c_backend.gg` = C fails too (direct construction); `control_ok.gg` = both pass (module has method bodies, isolates trigger to "module contributes no lowered function bodies", not "library struct" or "passed by value"). **Root-cause hypothesis:** the shim's type-collection walks types reachable through each module's lowered functions; a body-less library module is skipped, so its struct typedefs are never gathered even when another module references them by value. **Fix path:** extend the shim's type walk to also include struct defs from modules that only contribute extern signatures — or, treat the collection axis as the extern-reachable types (not function-body-reachable). Reproducer preserved with README + `run.sh` that regenerates artifacts.



- **🆕🐛 [MED — Layering rule-2 debt, GREW 2026-08-01 by Round XXIII γδ 3→5 prefixes; then Round XXIV Track E 5→7 prefixes; MERGED 2026-07-21 (M-infer)] `infer_collection_element_type` decides collection element types from mangled NAME PREFIXES instead of a typed field.** Root/fix (shared): carry K/V TypeIds on the collection TypeDef (fixes read + write at once), not re-parse the mangled name. TWO remaining symptoms after Round XXIII γδ closed the HashMap-of-struct + Deque cells and Round XXIV Track E closed the Set/HashSet empty-literal cell:
  - **[LOW — pre-existing, shared read+write; from R36-C] `infer_collection_element_type` compound-Dict-key first-`__` heuristic** (self-host `index_value_type_name` twin). Dict K/V split on the FIRST `__` in `Dict__K__V` is ambiguous for a COMPOUND key (`Dict[Vector[int], Option[int]]`). Pre-existing + shared (the `d[k]` index-read path has the identical heuristic), so R36-C's put/get_or_put hint is CONSISTENT, not a new regression. Real fix = carry K/V TypeIds on the Dict TypeDef instead of re-parsing the mangled name (fixes read + write at once).
  - **(rule-2 debt) `infer_collection_element_type` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs`) decides element types from NAME PREFIXES** (7 arms as of Round XXIV Track E: `Vector__`/`Deque__`/`Dict__`/`Map__`/`HashMap__`/`Set__`/`HashSet__`; the count is pinned by `infer_collection_element_type_arms_count` in `tests/lints.rs`). Element-type metadata should be a typed field on the collection TypeDef (`element_type` axis set at `register_collection_alias`/`ensure_collection_type`), read via an accessor. The String-base comprehension no longer reaches it; every other caller still keys on the prefix (a new collection family falls to I64). Fix the axis, not the next caller. Round XXIII γδ GREW this debt from 3 arms to 5 as a necessary interim (both siblings had `gg check`-clean SIGSEGV / silent-wrong-output cells); Round XXIV Track E grew 5→7 to close the Set/HashSet empty-literal C-emit-type-mismatch cell; the reference-grade typed-field fix remains queued.
- **🆕🐛 [MEDIUM — RESIDUAL after XIX Track Y Stmt::Expr fix; rephrased 2026-08-01 to reflect Round XXI Track A closing the SH lag half] Guard-temp statement-end drop is Stmt::Expr-only; VarDecl/Assign RHS sequential acquires remain.** Round XIX Track Y landed `DropScopeKind::Statement` around `Stmt::Expr` only (`stmts/mod.rs`) + `pop_statement_guard_temps` (GuardKind drop / non-Guard re-register into parent). Round XXI Track A ported the same to SH (`272e8dcc`) — DSK_STATEMENT + `pop_statement_guard_temps` mirror + `case SExpr` wrap in `lower_stmt.gg`. Graduated `rwlock_seq_lock_scope_deadlock.gg` + Mutex Expr sibling `mutex_seq_lock_expr_stmt.gg` GREEN on all three lanes (C+LLVM+SH); named POS `named_readguard_multi_stmt_hold` / `named_mutex_guard_multi_stmt_hold` pin scope-end hold for named binds. **Still pending:** sequential acquires on **VarDecl / Assign RHS** (and any non-Expr statement face) do not push Statement — a shape like `int x = r.read().get(); r.write().set(v)` can still self-deadlock; MVP deliberately excluded wrap of VarDecl/Assign/`with` on both lanes. Full NLL for all temps remains out of scope (A29 open).
- **🆕🐛 [MEDIUM — codegen type-precision; discovered 2026-07-24 chasing the CI timeout] Self-host emits `int32 → void*` assignments that Apple clang 21 rejects (hard error, un-`-w`-suppressible).** The self-host's `--lir-c` output contains `void* __s2 = __v2;` where `__v2 = (int32_t)0LL` — a NULL-ish sentinel the lowering typed as `int32` but stored into a `void*` slot (repro line: stage1.c:~22866, `__s2 = __v2`). Functionally 0 (benign at runtime) but type-incorrect C: gcc / older clang emit a `-Wint-conversion` *warning* (suppressed by the `cc -w` in the self-host stage tests), so **CI (Linux/gcc) is unaffected**; Apple clang 21 promotes `-Wint-conversion` to a default *error* that `-w` does not downgrade. Consequence: `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling` / `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` / the pinned-8MB self-compile **cannot run locally on recent macOS** (cc step fails at ~135s, well before any assert). Fix = give the sentinel/slot a consistent pointer type at the lowering source (the void* slot should receive `(void*)0`, not an int32), NOT paper over with `-Wno-int-conversion`. Pre-existing (codegen; predates the 2026-07-24 timeout fix). Verify it still repros before fixing.

### Medium
- **🆕🧹 [MED→ small CLASS FIX; SCOUTED 2026-08-05, and the original filing's PREMISE WAS WRONG] THREE parallel hand-lists decide aggregate-return ABI by symbol NAME while the typed answer already exists in the registry.** ⚠ **Correction to this entry as first filed:** it claimed the registry has no return-ABI axis and that an additive `RuntimeFn.return_abi` was needed. **False** — `RuntimeFn.ret: CRuntimeType` (`compiler/data/schema.gg:180`) already discriminates `TStr`/`TArray`/`TMap`/`TSet`/`TVoid`/`TPtr`, which is exactly what the name-lists reconstruct. The owner-approved `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump is still owed, but for a SMALLER reason (below), not for a new field. Also corrected: the LLVM site is the **`else` arm** for externs absent from `module.externs` — the primary path is already typed via `needs_sret`. **The actual defect:** three lists doing one job and DISAGREEING — `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:722-741` (includes `gorget_str_cat`/`gorget_str_index`), `:6941-6962` (omits both), and a 90-name `match` at `src/lir/types.rs:505-545`. **MEASURED** (scout prototype, reverted): converting the `:6941` arm to read typed `ret` produced **0 verdict changes across all 331 symbols that reach it** and **234 LLVM integration tests green** — a proven no-op — while closing **52 of 299 latent holes** the hand-list omits (`gorget_str_split`, `gorget_map_keys`, `gorget_read_file`, …), currently masked only because those symbols always reach `module.externs`. ⚠ **Convert the call AND declare sides together** (`:2027-2035` emits `declare ptr @f(...)` for unknown names, so a newly-sret'd symbol would mismatch) and route through `needs_sret`/`is_small_aggregate` rather than hardcoding. Ships with an arm-count/absence lint (Core #4/#6). **The bump that IS owed:** re-type `gorget_str_to_cstr`'s `ret` to the already-declared, currently-unused `TCStr` variant (`resources.gg:548` carries the comment "CStr ABI on return wire; Ptr return"), and add `ABytePtr`/`AOutPtr` to the schema's `AbiKind` — `src/ir/abi.rs` has them and `gorget_map_iter_key`/`_value` need `AOutPtr`, so the schema cannot represent those rows today. One bump covers both.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — Core #4 sibling drift; found 2026-08-05] The LLVM backend re-implements printf format fixing instead of calling the shared helper.** `fix_printf_format` is the single source (`src/lir/lower/calls.rs:22`), used by the LIR lowerer (`src/lir/lower/insts.rs:4660`) and the C backend (`src/backend/c_lir/emit_printf.rs:199`). LLVM does not call it — it re-derives the `%lld`→`%f`/`%.*s` rewrite inline at `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:6350-6381`. Two implementations of one axis: a conversion added to the shared helper silently misbehaves on LLVM only.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — dead code carrying a live miscompile arm AND an optimizer pessimization; found 2026-08-05] `Inst::InlineC` has ZERO producers but three costs.** `IrBuilder::inline_c` (`src/ir/builder.rs:692`) has no callers anywhere in `src/`, yet the variant threads through GIR, LIR, both backends and the optimizer. (a) The LLVM arm decides by NAME-MATCHING the embedded C string and otherwise emits `%vN = add i64 0, 0 ; InlineC skipped` (`src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:7188-7195`) — a silent wrong-value miscompile if one ever reaches it (Core #10). (b) `src/lir/optimize.rs:2325` conservatively marks ALL locals live because "InlineC may reference any local via _N". (c) SSA declines to promote slots it names, and the lowerer STRING-PARSES the C text to find them (`src/lir/lower/insts.rs:1775-1830`). Deleting the GIR path retires all three. Confirm no callers under `tests/` first.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — Layering rule 1 (lossless on invariants); unfiled until now, found 2026-08-05] LIR `Slot` drops `Local.ownership` at the GIR→LIR boundary.** `src/lir/mod.rs:1392-1395` is `Slot { ty, name }`; GIR carries the rich `Local.ownership: LocalOwnership` (`src/ir/mod.rs:710-717`), documented there as the "source of truth at the GIR/LIR boundary". LIR reads it only indirectly through `slot_kind`. The ratified target shape is D6's final hop: ONE unified `SlotProvenance { Value, Owned, Borrowed(BorrowOrigin) }`, **not** a two-field `slot_kind`+`Slot.origin` split (owner 2026-06-06). ⚠ That ruling attaches a gate: build the layer only once a REAL consumer exists (a cross-pass borrow-aware optimisation) — until then it is unused infrastructure.
- **🆕🧹 [MED — Core #4 sibling drift + Layering "no name matching"; found 2026-08-05, counts re-derived post-rebase] `last_error_fn` and `last_error_fn_lir` are the SAME 8-branch cascade, duplicated VERBATIM in two files.** `src/backend/c_lir/helpers.rs:1013` and `src/lir/lower/lifts.rs:50` each test the same eight conditions in the same order — `gorget_udp_` / `gorget_server_socket_` / `gorget_socket_` / `gorget_tls_server_` / `gorget_tls_` / `gorget_crypto_` prefixes, then exact `gorget_process_spawn` and `gorget_parse_int`|`gorget_parse_float` — returning identical strings, down to a shared comment ("TlsServer before Tls to avoid prefix collision"). They are IN SYNC today; the defect is that nothing keeps them so, and a new thread-local-error runtime fn added to one misbehaves silently on the other lane. Doubly wrong because the routing is `starts_with` prefix-matching on runtime symbols — the axis belongs on the typed `RuntimeFn` registry (`compiler/data/resources.gg`). Fix: drive both from the registry, or failing that add a table-equivalence lint so the pair cannot drift.

- **🐛 RUST-GG CODEGEN — `rethrow (String e): <String-transform>` LEAKS the transformed error String (LeakSanitizer)** (DISCOVERED 2026-06-22 by the throws-codegen-fix output-review's ASan sweep; PRE-EXISTING — reproduces IDENTICALLY on the pristine `717289b4` tree, NOT introduced by the throws-codegen fix). Repro under `--sanitize`: `tests/fixtures/rethrow_catch_binding.gg` (`int caller_rethrow(x): int v = helper_str(x) rethrow (String e): Err(f"transformed: {e}")` → `==…==ERROR: LeakSanitizer: 18 byte(s) leaked in 1 allocation(s)`) AND `tests/fixtures/error_rethrow_transform.gg` (`int port = try_parse(s) rethrow (String e): f"port error: {e}"` → leak through `gorget_string_format`). The freshly-formatted/allocated transform String (the new error payload built in the rethrow arm) is never freed on the error path. ⚠ ONLY the `rethrow`-with-a-String-transform shape leaks — `rethrow_basic`/`rethrow_bare` (no String-build transform) are CLEAN, and the entire 68-fixture throws/error ASan sweep is otherwise leak-free. Likely an ownership-register gap on the rethrow-arm transform result (the new Error payload's String isn't tracked for drop on the rethrow propagation path). Own scout (RUN under ASan, trace the unfreed alloc — `gorget_string_format` at the rethrow arm) → brief → fix at the rethrow-transform OWNERSHIP-register write site (register the transform-result String for drop / move it into the propagated Error). Low-pri (leak, not UB), but it is a real defect on BOTH the reference and self-host shape. NOT a quick guess.

  - **③ collect-target `walk_stmt` `Break(Some(e))` mis-bind (theoretical, low-pri).** Rust pairs `Stmt::Break(Some(e))` with the enclosing fn RETURN type (`typecheck.rs:~6598`); a `break value` yields a loop value, not the fn return (only fires if ret_ty is Set/Dict AND break value is `.collect()`; no corpus fixture; the self-host mirror is MORE conservative — no `SBreak` arm). Reference-grade resolution = align (drop `Break` from Rust OR add `SBreak` to self-host).

- **🐛 PRE-EXISTING match-arm-binding LEAK** (ASan, orthogonal to snag #11). An owned-String error caught by `match … case Error(e):` is NOT dropped at the arm's scope exit (the `From`-converted/built `BigErr.msg` heap String, ~5-6B via `gorget_string_clone_to_owned`). Fix = register the `Error(e)`-style binding's owned payload for drop at its scope.

- **🔧 FIX-THE-ORACLE (Rust `gg` LEAK, low-pri) — Custom-drop struct as a FIELD leaks its inner fields.** `Wrapper{Container inner}` where `Container` has a user `Drop` + droppable fields → Rust's `populate_recursive_drop_structs` (`mod.rs:529`, `Custom(fn)=>fn.clone()`) routes field-drop to the BARE `Container__drop` (user method only, NOT inner `Inner__drop`/`tag`-free) → leaks `inner.label`/`inner.tag`. The self-host ③(c) fix routes to the unified `__gorget_dtor_Container` → no leak. Fix Rust: route a `Custom` field to `__gorget_dtor_{field_type_name}` (verify the unified dtor IS emitted + no double-free). Add a `mem_live` leak fixture. The self-host already exceeds Rust here.

- **🧹 retire the mangled-name inner-type slicing in `unwrap`/`expect`/`unwrap_or` lowering** (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs:~620-650`). The Option/Result inner `TypeId` is recovered by STRING-SLICING the mangled name (`&tn["Option__".len()..]`, `Ref__` strip, hardcoded error-suffix list `["__Str","__int64_t","__bool","__double"]` + a fragile last-`__` fallback). Same name-matching disease (comment `methods.rs:~1876`). Fully retiring = THREAD the resolved inner `TypeId` from the type checker through GIR lowering (checker has it: `builtin_method_type`'s arms return `elem_type()`). Non-trivial (metadata-threading). Phase 1's gate makes the receiver guaranteed-Option/Result, the precondition.

- **🐛 UPSTREAM GAP — `enum_category` NOT registered for Result with a USER error enum** (`Result[float, ParseError]`/`Result[int, ParseError]`). At the unwrap site (`methods.rs`) `enum_category` was `None` for `Result__double__ParseError` → fell to `else { I64_TYPE }` → mis-typed Ok payload (`double` read as `int64_t`, bogus `+8` arith → `dataframe_csv` printed `0`). Worked around with a name-prefix fallback (`tn.starts_with("Result__")`). REAL fix = find why these user-error Result types are registered WITHOUT going through `make_result_type_def` (`types.rs:1186`, which sets `enum_category: Result`) — likely a mono/generic-instantiation path building the TypeDef directly. Then the name-prefix fallback becomes redundant.

- **🐛 LATENT: `Box[Option[String]]` / `Box[Result[T]]` deref-store-of-borrow is a UAF (ENUM-PAYLOAD POINTEES; STRUCT-resource case done).** `is_resource_type`/`is_resource_name` (`src/ir/types.rs:639,678`) only descends STRUCT fields, NOT enum variant payloads → `Option[String]` returns false → the `is_resource_type(pointee)` gate SKIPS the clone on BOTH legs (no validator panic, no clone). The clone helper CAN clone such a Ptr-to-enum-payload RHS (Case 1); blocker is purely the gate predicate. Fixing = widen `is_resource_type` to descend enum variant payloads — LARGER (a blunt attempt regressed 112 fixtures, `types.rs:666-677`, because pattern lowering / collection-element clone / drop accountant / ABI choice depend on the narrow semantics). Needs a coordinated migration OR a separate `is_resource_or_enum_payload` predicate threaded only to the two deref-store gates. `has_resource_fields` (`types.rs:703`) has the right shape but isn't interchangeable. Add an `#[ignore]` `Box[Option[String]]` deref-store fixture when picked up.

- **(FIDELITY) THREE enum-side drop-name sites reconstruct `tn + "__drop"` by name WITHOUT the `drop_collision_types` guard** — `emit_all_drop_clone_forward_decls` enum-drop loop + `emit_enum_drops` fwd-decl (`lir_codegen.gg:~4846`) + def (`:~4855`); same latent class as the guarded Dict-key (`:1389`)/element (`:1437`). NOT exercised today (only collision type `DataFrame` is a struct). A future ENUM with a user 2-arg `Enum__drop` method would mis-route. Add a one-line guard at each.

- **(Medium, LLVM, FLAKY?) `leak_string_heavy` fails under the LLVM backend on x86_64 CI** (run 28223658994, 2026-06-26): prints `p1..p5=0` (every per-pattern leak delta is zero) yet `leaked=true` — i.e. `p1==0 and ... and p5==0` evaluated false despite all operands being 0. C backend passes (aarch64 local + x86_64 CI); not reproducible locally (no `llc` installed). `src/` was UNCHANGED since the last green LLVM run (27923767349, 2026-06-22, where this test passed), so it is NOT introduced by the pushed self-host/docs commits. Contradiction points to an LLVM-backend uninitialized/nondeterministic read or a long `and`-chain/`not` miscompile (latent, likely intermittent). Rerun in progress to confirm flaky vs. deterministic. If deterministic, investigate LLVM lowering of chained `and` + `not` into the `bool all_zero` slot.

- **[Planner round 3, D4(i)] File: `push_char` on a String-view `&` binding ICEs at `src/backend/c_lir/emit_types.rs:850`** (GorgetString ABI got a non-Str value — the String-view mutating-arg ABI). Fixture `known_gaps/string_view_push_char_ice.gg` (#[ignore], asserts dead-temp accept `hello world`). Fix = carry the view through / materialize to an owned copy at the mutating-method boundary; un-ignore.

- **[Planner round 3, D4(ii)] File: a nested index-assign rooted at `windows`/`chunks` (`v.windows(2)[0][0] = 777`) ICEs at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/assigns.rs:1100`** ("index-assign found no setter"); compound sibling at `:1809`. windows/chunks return `Vector[Vector[int]]` (eager, owned), so the write is a dead temp. Fixture `known_gaps/vector_windows_nested_index_assign_ice.gg` (#[ignore], asserts dead-temp accept `1`). Fix = lower a setter over the owned temp; un-ignore.

- **[G3 follow-up] insts.rs `__clone` name-match kill is DEFERRED (ABI-trap, not done in G3).** `emit_extern_call` (`src/lir/lower/insts.rs`, the `ends_with("__clone") && !starts_with("gorget_")` line) Ptr-forces clone-fn args — but it also catches Shared/Weak/Channel HANDLE clones (`Shared__X__clone`, emitted at `exprs/methods.rs`), which `TypeRegistry::clone_fn_name_for_def` does NOT produce. A naive swap to a `clone_fn_name_for_def`-derived set drops the Ptr-forcing for handle clones → arg-ABI change → miscompile. The typed fix must enumerate ALL compiler-generated `{Name}__clone` C fns (user struct/enum + concurrency handles) as a cached registry (or put a typed `takes_void_ptr_args` flag on the emitted clone call), then look up via `set.contains`. Verify against a Shared/Weak `.clone()` fixture (bit-identical arg ABI) before landing. Remaining Core #2 smell.

- **[G3 follow-up] CallExtern reason field for closure clones.** The closure-capture clone at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs` (`gorget_closure_clone_to_owned`) emits `Instruction::CallExtern`, not `Call`, so it is invisible to the Call-only clone-reason validator and stays untagged (left as-is in G3). Extend the `reason` field to `CallExtern` (or a companion sidecar) and route the closure clone through a tagged emitter so the census covers it.

- **Rust frontend: unify the 7 `lower_for_*` functions into a single scaffold + per-type element extractor** (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs`, 1159 lines, ~80% duplicated scaffold). Self-host's `lower_loops.gg::lower_for` (`lower_for_vector` fast path + `lower_for_iterator` protocol) is the reference-grade shape. **Three layers** (ship in order, green tests gating each): (1) helper extraction (`alloc_for_blocks`/`emit_else_arm_tail`/`init_borrow_iter_local`, ~150 lines retired, no semantic change, ~half day); (2) filter abstraction for dict/set (`with_state_filter`, ~30 lines, ~a day total); (3) trait-based unification (`trait CollectionLowerer` + `lower_for_scaffold<L>` + 7 backends, ~300-400 lines, highest win + highest risk, 2-3 days total). Per-kind variability that doesn't unify: `lower_for_string` (source-aware ptr setup), `lower_for_range` (no iter_op), dict/set (state-check filter + output-param destructure), `lower_for_iterable` (no incr_bb). Verification: each layer ships independently with a full integration sweep (1122/1122). Tied-off after: the trait-equipped-methods-in-`fn_sigs` entry + the iter-chain `lowerer_comparison` mismatches.

- **Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites in `src/backend/c/c_runtime.rs`).** Compiler-side emit produces `file:line:col:` for inline traps, but runtime helpers (`gorget_array_index_oob`, `gorget_str_*_oob`, channel-closed sends, alloc failures, `gorget_panic("integer overflow")` at `c_runtime.rs:5042/5047/5052`, +~45) are called from inside other runtime fns with no caller span → fall back to `<unknown>:0:0:`. Proper fix = per-runtime-function span plumbing (every panic-able entry point grows `(const char* file, int line, int col)`, every emit site passes the LIR instruction's span). Estimate 5-8 days.

- **Residual: `Option[Box[T]]` / `Result[Box[T]]` field drops not emitted on enum variants and struct fields.** The Box-field-drop wrapper + struct/enum-variant wiring closed 2026-05-01. The Option/Result ENUM-VARIANT skip (`populate_recursive_drop_enums` mod.rs:471-481) + STRUCT-FIELD skip (`populate_recursive_drop_structs` mod.rs:412-422) kept intentional: enabling it crashes the self-host `resolve_stmt` because `stmts.get(i).unwrap()` returns `Stmt` by value (shallow copy aliasing the vector's interior box/string pointers) → double-free. Proper fix is COMPILER-level: make `Vector[T].get(i)` for resource T auto-clone (deep) or return `Ref[T]`-only. Then the skip can be removed + `option_box_enum.gg`'s 3 leaked Some(Box(...)) free correctly. Today: leak (3 blocks), not unsoundness.

- **Stdlib narrow waist — Phase 2c residual.** `Vector.each`/`for_each` void-return entries retired (user-space `lib/std/iter.gg` wrappers). Typed-return Vector HOFs (filter/map/fold/reduce/any/all/find/find_index/count/enumerate/flat_map/zip) STAY as BuiltinMethodDecl entries — IR-lowering reads their declared return types via `resolve_builtin_method_return_type` when the user-space wrapper sig isn't registered yet (early generic mono). Full retirement blocks on a separate signature source for IR-lowering when BuiltinMethodDecl is absent. Dict.each/Set.each BuiltinMethodDecls stay (no wrapper migration). LIR `HofOp` variants stay (serve Dict/Set too).

- **C backend: retire local val_types/ptr_pointee fixup phases (after the 2026-05-15 seed migration).** Seeds `val_types`/`ptr_pointee` from `func.value_types`/`func.pointee_types`; remaining cross-instruction reasoning to push upstream so the local pass disappears: Guard accessor inference from consumers (`mod.rs:1381-1456`), cross-type Option/Result map combinator override (`mod.rs:1465-1486` via `combinator_result_struct_id`), consumer-driven Add/Cmp peer-type back-propagation (`mod.rs:1490-1570`), InlineC→SlotStore type inference (`mod.rs:1306-1322`), Ret-from-function backfill. Each needs `compute_module_value_types` to gain a fixed-point/multi-phase walk. Half-day each + tests.

- **Decompose emit_call_extern.rs (~908 lines).** Tier 1-3 + HOF (→`emit_hof.rs`) + printf (→`emit_printf.rs`) lifted. Remaining genuinely-backend-specific clusters: out-parameter adaptation (~178 lines), collection drop/clone injection (~70 lines).

- **Phase A's `resources` build-tooling — the cross-language single source of truth** (`docs/devbook/18-runtime-abi.md` + `26-self-host-frontend.md`). Canonical source = idiomatic Gorget code at `compiler/data/{schema,resources}.gg`, baked via `include_str!` + parsed by the compiler's own parser (no build.rs/codegen; self-host imports the same files). Items 1-6 done (runtime_extern_sig retirement, CollectionKind close, spike, Rust loader + AST walker, self-host adoption, self-host `starts_with` consumer-read retirement). Remaining:
  - **Item 7 — retire Rust name-prefix dispatch sites.** PARTIAL (first wave: `ResourceKind::RefCounted` + 3 sites migrated to `resources::table().lookup(name)` reading typed `collection_kind`). Site survey: `starts_with("[A-Z][a-zA-Z]*__"` = 256 hits (TODO's old "9" was the headline). Sub-items (7e is load-bearing — 7b + most 7d retire once it lands):
    - **7b. `emit_collection_constructor`** (`helpers.rs:824-915`) — typed Tier 1 landed (7e Phase 4 `4aa0cdcb`); Tier 2 name-parse fallback retires once 7e-r1 makes Resource operands universal.
    - **7d. residual `starts_with` sites** (`emit_types.rs`, `validate.rs`, `lifts.rs`, `mod.rs`) — mostly WRITER stages reading element/key/value types from mangled names before val_types is computed. Bundled under 7e-r1 + 7e-r3.
    - **7e. LIR element-type parametricity — the writer-site fix that retires name-parsing as a CLASS.** Encoding done (`LirType::Resource { kind, params }` SSoT + `expected_resource_arity`/`validate_resource_arity`; `emit_collection_constructor` prefers typed reads). Residue (all gated on extending the writer-side `Resource` propagation beyond `Inst::CollectionCtor` results): **7e-r1 global writer flip** (`map_gir_type_with_structs` returns `Resource{kind,params}` for every resource-type GIR Named operand; ~64 c_lir `LirType::Struct(sid)` sites need parallel `Resource` arms; helpers `parametric_resource_lir_type`+`classify_resource_name` recoverable from `2a337b6a..490a45b2`); **7e-r3 Phase 5 fallback retirement** (once r1 universal, delete `emit_collection_constructor` Tier 2 + `elem_size_from_monomorphized`/`dict_elem_sizes_from_monomorphized`/`concurrency_elem_size` name-reads in `types.rs`). Encoding (a) `Resource{kind, params:SmallVec<[LirType;2]>}` is the conceptually-clean path (perf to be benchmarked, not assumed); (b) per-mono StructId is the alternative. Spike (a) for half a day + measure compile-time on the lowerer driver before deciding. Estimate 3-5 days, design-attention, schedule deliberately.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — generic-indirection escape, from the RV-A Fable review 2026-07-16] `Box[T].x` / `Guard[T].x` through a generic fn (`T getx[T](Box[T] b): return b.x`) checks OK and prints garbage-0 — pre-existing at base, reproduced both binaries.** The Box shape is the generic-param variant of the deref-backend gap (rides that entry); **the Guard[T] case is its own line**: generic-indirected prints 0 — a monomorphization-side hole in the guards-work premise. Own slice with the deref-backend track. ⚠ **PARAM-INDIRECTION CELLS CLOSED 2026-07-27 (Family-1 Track A):** the four discriminated `Guard[Inner]`-through-a-`&`-param cells previously listed here have collapsed to the pointer-peeling family retired by `guard_of` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/shared.rs`; single funnel at `TypeMapper::register_named` with a differential `debug_assert!`). The `&`-face read, the `&`-face `&g.fd` write and the ASSIGN face `g.fd = v` are now correct and live-regressed by `guard_amp_param_field_writethrough.gg`; the bare-param mutating cell remained on a distinct root and is filed separately (`sound_guard_bare_param_mutate_undefined_symbol`, below).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg check` ACCEPTS, then C BUILD FAILS at the callee body; measured 2026-07-27 in the Family-1 Track A guard-family round] A BARE `Guard[T]` PARAMETER whose body MUTATES a field triggers an implicit declaration of `gorget_guard_clone` — a runtime symbol that does not exist.** DISTINCT from the pointer-peeling family retired the same round: after the peel the field access is correct; this cell breaks at the CoW materialize step, which emits a private-copy clone helper for the bare-param receiver. ⚠ **THE MUTATION IS THE DISCRIMINATOR** — a read-only twin over the same shape builds cleanly and merely prints the wrong value; probing read-only mis-scopes the fix. **Reference-grade fix** (matches the return-position sibling): a guard is single-owner and has no clone path by design (`docs/devbook/11-copy-on-write.md`) — the materialize pass either declines to clone single-owner guards or the checker rejects the mutation, but the runtime does NOT gain a `gorget_guard_clone` symbol. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_guard_bare_param_mutate_undefined_symbol.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_guard_bare_param_mutate_undefined_symbol`, asserting the intended `11`.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — Core #10 SILENT WRITE-DROP, `gg check` ACCEPTS, ALL THREE write faces; found 2026-07-27 in the Family-1 round's guard census] `ReadGuard[T]` field writes are silently DISCARDED instead of rejected.** Measured at HEAD on a guard LOCAL from `RWLock.read()` (no parameter indirection), all three `gg check`-clean and all building cleanly: `inc(&rg.fd)` → **10** · `rg.fd = 99` → **10** · `rg.fd += 5` → **10**. CONTROL, same shape: the `WriteGuard` twin `inc(&wg.fd)` → **11**, correct — so the guard machinery works and it is the READ-ONLY guard that swallows writes. Measured identical at base and tip — PRE-EXISTING. ⚠ **Symmetric across the faces, so it is NOT the Family-1 served-at-one-face asymmetry** — it is a plain lower-or-reject violation: the write is accepted at check time and dropped at lowering. **The intended behaviour is already written down in the producer:** `try_resolve_field_place`'s guard branch returns `None` for a read-only guard with the comment *"ReadGuard: writes are forbidden — don't resolve a write place (type checker should reject in future)"*. **Reference-grade fix is that rejection** — a check-time `E_*` error on any write through a `ReadGuard[T]` place, plus a negative fixture — not a lowering change; silently dropping a write the user wrote is the exact shape Core #10 forbids. Pairs with the `Guard[T]`-through-a-parameter entry above (same producer branch, same round's census). ⚠ **MERGED 2026-07-27:** a separate Low-pri entry filed 2026-06-25 tracked the COMPOUND face (`rg.x += N`) alone, citing `mod.rs:2488` / `exprs/mod.rs:2474-2521` — line refs that have since rotted. One defect, one root, three faces; do not re-split. ⚠ **EXTENDED 2026-07-28 (Track L executor, tuple-face sibling):** the SAME lower-or-reject violation now also lives on the TUPLE face — Track L's fix to `try_resolve_tuple_field_place` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs` new Guard branch) mirrors the struct face's `is_read_only()` early-return per the brief, so `rg.0 = v` / `rg.0 += v` on `ReadGuard[(int,int)]` now behave identically to `rg.field = v`: `gg check` accepts, C compiles (post-Track-L), LLVM prints garbage / write silently dropped. RED-verified post-Track-L: `ReadGuard[(int,int)] rg = r.read(); rg.0 = 99; print(rg.0)` prints `1` (write dropped). Same root class, same reject-at-check reference-grade fix; the rejection must cover both `try_resolve_field_place`'s and `try_resolve_tuple_field_place`'s Guard branches (four faces total: struct=/+=/&, tuple=/+=/& — six once you cross the writer axis with the resolver axis). **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_readguard_write_faces_dropped.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_readguard_write_faces_dropped` (RED-verified: `gg check` exits 0 at HEAD), asserting the intended reject.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg check` ACCEPTS, then NEITHER BACKEND PRODUCES A BINARY; measured 2026-07-27] A `Guard[T]` in any AGGREGATE position fails to compile: `unknown type name 'Guard__Inner'`.** Two costumes, same diagnostic, same root class (a closure environment is lowered as a struct): a **STRUCT FIELD** (`struct Holder: Guard[Inner] g`) and a **CLOSURE CAPTURE** (`int() f = (): g.fd`). Both `gg check`-clean; C emits `Guard__Inner g;` inside the aggregate with no typedef behind it, and the LLVM lane emits the SAME error in its generated runtime `.c` — so this is shared type emission, not a C-backend quirk. ⚠ **THE CAPTURE IS THE DISCRIMINATOR on the closure costume:** the byte-identical program with the capture removed BUILDS AND RUNS (measured `7` / `10`), so neither the guard local nor the closure nor the field read is at fault. **Fix shape (not chased to the writer site):** the guard wrapper typedefs are emitted only for the positions the guard lowering knows about (locals, parameters); an aggregate field of guard type reaches the struct emitter with a mangled name that has no typedef. **DURABLE REPROS:** `known_gaps/sound_guard_struct_field_cc_fail.gg` + `known_gaps/sound_guard_closure_capture_cc_fail.gg` and their `#[ignore]`d tests (both RED-verified on C and LLVM).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — `gg check` ACCEPTS, then NEITHER BACKEND LINKS; measured 2026-07-27] RETURNING a `Guard[T]` calls `gorget_guard_clone`, a runtime symbol that does not exist.** `Guard[Inner] take(Mutex[Inner] m): return m.lock()` → C: `implicit declaration of function 'gorget_guard_clone'` (plus the consequent `incompatible types ... from type 'int'`); LLVM: builds the IR, then `undefined reference to 'gorget_guard_clone'` at LINK. ⚠ **MIND THE PROBE on LLVM — an IR-only check reads as clean; the link step is where it shows.** ⚠ **DISTINCT from the `Guard[T]`-through-a-PARAMETER family above, and the discriminator is that NO FIELD ACCESS IS INVOLVED**: that family's bare-param row surfaces the same missing symbol, but only when a MUTATING field access forces a CoW materialize; here the clone is demanded by the RETURN boundary alone, with no `.field` anywhere in the callee. **Reference-grade fix is almost certainly NOT to add the runtime symbol** — a guard is single-owner and has no clone path by design (AGENTS.md, "Ownership at Consuming Positions"), so the boundary should MOVE it. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/sound_guard_return_position_cc_fail.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `sound_guard_return_position_cc_fail` (RED-verified on both lanes).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — SILENT NAME COLLISION between user code and a builtin wrapper, `gg check` ACCEPTS; measured 2026-07-27] A USER struct named `Guard` collides with the builtin guard as soon as it is GENERIC.** `struct Guard[T]: T x` → `gg check` clean, then C fails: `typedef gorget_guard_t Guard__int64_t;` (`unknown type name 'gorget_guard_t'`) and the field access is rewritten to the runtime guard's member — `(void*)&((Guard__int64_t *)(__v1))->owner`. **The collision is on the MANGLED name**, which is exactly why the live, green `fieldaccess_user_guard_field_ok.gg` does not catch it: that fixture's user `Guard` is NON-generic, mangles to bare `Guard` with no `__` suffix, and never reaches the prefix table. Its header asserted that sharing a builtin wrapper's name "costs the user nothing" — narrowed 2026-07-27 to the cell it actually samples. **ROOT (verified):** lowering decides "is this a guard?" by `guard_inner_suffix` = `strip_prefix("Guard__"/"ReadGuard__"/"WriteGuard__")` on the mangled type name (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/shared.rs`), at FOUR call sites (`stmts/assigns.rs` plain field assign; `exprs/mod.rs` field read, shared `&`-place typing, write-place producer). The typed flag that should drive it — `DefInfo.deref_wrapper_kind`, seeded once at registration in `src/semantic/scope.rs` — never reaches GIR. **Reference-grade fix is Layering rule 2: carry the typed flag down and read it**, not widen the prefix list or reserve the names. ⚠ `ReadGuard`/`WriteGuard` user generics are the unmeasured sibling cells. **DURABLE REPRO:** `known_gaps/fieldaccess_user_generic_guard_collision.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `fieldaccess_user_generic_guard_collision` (RED-verified). ⚠ Two comments asserting the opposite were corrected the same round (`src/semantic/scope.rs`'s "never re-derived from a name downstream" and `exprs/mod.rs`'s "typed detection ... not name-matching of meaning", the latter sitting directly on the prefix test).

Pre-existing, independent of D29 (adjacent to the `v[i]()!` corner). Workaround: bind to a local. Own small slice.

- **🐛 [strmove fix scout 2026-07-06, MEDIUM — ASan-verified, ORTHOGONAL to the concat fix] `String &p` (mut-borrow) param grown by `.push()` LEAKS the grown buffer.** `void f(String &p): p.push("!!!"); print(p)` → LSan 6 bytes. String-specific — `Vector &v` push through the same shared-mutation Borrowed path is clean. This same leaky path is WHY the concat fix deliberately kept `!`-move String params `Owned` (routing them through Borrowed would inherit this leak — measured). Fix the Borrowed-String grow path; the concat track does NOT depend on it. Evidence: /tmp/recover_strmove/FINDINGS.md.

- **🐛 LLVM backend: `--sanitize` is SILENTLY DROPPED — an "LLVM ASan" run is VACUOUS.** `compile_llvm_pipeline` (`src/main.rs:1177`) never reads `options.sanitize`; the LLVM clang/link path passes nothing (binary has zero asan symbols). Any LLVM-side memory gate must use STDOUT PARITY with the C backend. Each iteration's `alloca %GorgetString` is at its use site inside the loop → frame grows with trip count (C backend has block-scoped autos, no cliff). Fix = hoist allocas to the function entry block (also defeats mem2reg otherwise). Until then keep LLVM loop probes under ~100k prints.

NOT the print-temp class — each an other-class drop-registration gap. RUN an ASan witness per fixture, trace the unregistered materialization, register at the producer.

#### [CLASS FIX — layering, from Increment-2 scout + Round-8 Track A] typed is_borrow_ptr flag on GirFieldInfo
Multiple sites reconstruct "is this field a borrow pointer" from the field-type-NAME prefix `Ref__`/`MutRef__` (core-invariant-#2 smell): `lir_lower.gg:4360` GIFieldLoad dispatch (Round-8 Track A added the `MutRef__` read), `resolve_field_lir_type:708`, `resolve_field_gir_type:942`, closure Increment-2's ByMutRef env field (which must be NAMED `MutRef__<T>` to take the load-stored-pointer path), and now (2026-07-27 Track D advisory A2 sibling) `field_storage_holds_pointer` in `lir_lower.gg` — hoisted into a helper by Track D as a strict improvement over the open-coded prefix test, but the underlying `starts_with("Ref__"/"MutRef__")` shape remains and is a member of THIS census, not a new class. All reuse the documented accepted residual because `GirFieldInfo` stores only a type-name String, not a typed id. CLEAN CLASS FIX: add a typed `bool is_borrow_ptr` (or a typed pointee-id) to `GirFieldInfo` (gir.gg), set it at field registration, and read it at all the above sites instead of the name prefix — retiring the whole residual class. Mirrors Rust's typed `field_is_ptr` (insts.rs:1029). Medium track (gir.gg + ~5 consumers + careful review); do AFTER the closure increments that depend on the current residual behavior have landed (so the cleanup is a pure no-op refactor). This subsumes the earlier "MutRef__ symmetry" nit. **🆕 (2026-07-27, filed by Track B1 A-2 output-review) — Rust-side sibling on TYPE-NAME rather than field-name: `callable_local_return_type` + `callable_local_param_types` (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:96/151`) recognise callable ast types by matching the outer name against `"Callable" | "MutCallable" | "ConsumeCallable"` — a name-string test at a semantic decision (Layering rule 2 debt, same class as the field-name-prefix reconstruction above). The three names are the built-in callable-family types; a user `struct MutCallable[T]` or a future callable variant would silently mis-route through this helper. Reference-grade fix: a typed `is_callable_family` (or `callable_kind: Option<CallableKind>`) flag on the resolved type, seeded once at registration (as `guard_inner_suffix`'s planned funnel does for the guard family). Track B1's brief for a `tests/lints.rs` name-list ratchet on these callable name-match sites was noted but never landed — file it in the same ratchet when the typed-flag lands.

- **🆕 [MEDIUM — typed-metadata smell, from the R-C scout 2026-07-10] The LIR extern decl hardcodes an `i64` return for the monomorphized `Result__T__E__unwrap_error` mangles** — the combinator route's return type should come from typed metadata (the Result's error-payload type), not a default; the R-C guard works around it at the LLVM emit (val_types dst = field-2 payload) and the C twin has its own resolution. Root fix = type the extern decl at the LIR producer (rule 2), retiring both backend-side resolutions. Pairs with the `Inst::CallExtern` typed-routing-flag cleanup in the review-residuals (f) bundle.

- **🔭 (low-pri, const-bytes #10) — scalar-FLOAT static const-init uses `%g` (`float_to_str`), not bit-exact.** `scalar_static_c_literal`'s float path round-trips `2.5` exactly, but a future precision-sensitive float static (`3.14159265358979`) would `%g`-truncate. ### Rust gg bugs surfaced by R39 Phase 2e Sub-task 0 probe (2026-08-09)

The 4 bugs below blocked R39's owner-chosen Option C helper design
(`Vector[T] parse_comma_separated_list[T](Parser &, Token, Callable[T(Parser &)])`)
and forced the shipped Path 3 fallback (single boolean `consume_comma_or_tok`
helper, no generics, no Callable). Each has a durable `known_gaps/rust_gg_bug_*/`
repro + `#[ignore]`d integration test asserting the INTENDED behavior. Un-ignore
each test as the underlying bug fixes; graduate the fixture out of `known_gaps/`
when it passes cleanly (per Task Continuity).

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Rust gg, closure-inference gap] `Callable[T(Struct &)]` + closure literal `(p): p.field` — Rust gg infers `p` as `int64_t` instead of `Struct &`, so `.field` returns garbage (or fails at link time with `undefined reference to 'int64_t__<method>'` when the closure body invokes a method).** Fixture: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/rust_gg_bug_callable_amp_struct_closure_literal/repro.gg` + README.md. Integration test: `rust_gg_bug_callable_amp_struct_closure_literal` (`#[ignore]`d, asserts intended `1`). Green cell (scalar-arg): `Callable[T(int)]` + `(n): n * 10` works — see top-level `generic_callable.gg`. **Fix direction:** closure-literal untyped-param inference must consult the ENCLOSING call's expected function type; today it bails to `int64_t` when the expected param is `Struct &`.

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — Rust gg, SIGSEGV, MEMORY-UNSAFE] `Callable[T(Struct &)]` + named-fn callback + `Vector.get().unwrap()` iterator body → SIGSEGV.** Fixture: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/rust_gg_bug_callable_amp_struct_iterator_segv/repro.gg` + README.md. Integration test: `rust_gg_bug_callable_amp_struct_iterator_segv` (`#[ignore]`d, asserts intended `1\n2`). Green cell (direct, no iterator): `with_counter(f, &c)` — see `tests/fixtures/callable_ref_param.gg`. Adding `Vector.get().unwrap()` into the shape flips it to SIGSEGV. **Fix direction:** unclear without instrumentation of the emitted C around `f(&t)` — either the `t` local's stack slot is stale by the time f is invoked, or the fn-pointer wrapper mangles the `Thing &` calling convention, or drop-elab frees `t` in the loop body pre-call.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — Rust gg, closure struct capture wrong-code] Closure capturing a local struct's `&self`-method mutations does NOT persist across successive closure invocations — prints `1 1 1` instead of `1 2 3`.** Fixture: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/rust_gg_bug_closure_struct_capture_no_persist/repro.gg` + README.md. Integration test: `rust_gg_bug_closure_struct_capture_no_persist` (`#[ignore]`d, asserts intended `1\n2\n3`). Green cell (primitive capture): `int count = 0; auto increment = (): count = count + 1;` prints `3` after 3 calls — see top-level `closures.gg`. Isolated (no HOF, no Callable indirection) — the closure is called in-place three times in `main`. **Fix direction:** struct captures inside a closure invoked more than once MUST be stored by-reference in the closure environment (or mutations must write back through the env slot). Related to the two Callable-with-struct-ref bugs above (all three seem to trip on how struct locals cross into a closure environment).

- **🆕🐛 [MED — Rust gg, scale-dependent monomorphization] Generic user-fn monomorphization SKIPPED at parser.gg scale — declaration emitted with `int64_t` return, no definition, `cc` fails with `incompatible types when assigning to type 'GorgetArray' from type 'int64_t'`.** Fixture: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/rust_gg_bug_generic_mono_parser_scale/repro.gg` (SEED, does NOT reproduce in isolation) + README.md (durable REPRO PROCEDURE: revert R39 Phase 2e migration + re-apply a Callable-typed helper on parser.gg). Integration test: `rust_gg_bug_generic_mono_parser_scale` (`#[ignore]`d). Green cell: same shape in a small file monomorphizes correctly (isolated fixture prints `0` — a different bug from the sibling `rust_gg_bug_callable_amp_struct_closure_literal`, but not the scale-dependent mono skip). **Fix direction:** instrument `src/backend/c_lir/emit_types.rs` + `src/monomorph.rs` to log every generic-fn instantiation request + emitted definition; find the missing pair at parser.gg scale. May be a downstream of the closure-inference bug (if the concrete `T=SpannedType` is only reachable through the closure body's inferred return, the mono walker doesn't consult that path).

## Perf / clone-pressure / compile-time

### High

- **⭐🆕 [HIGH — DENSE INDEX-MAP LAYOUT for `Dict`/`Set`; design OWNER-RATIFIED 2026-08-03 as D39 (`docs/define-gorget/decisions.md`), NOT implemented. Pure performance + capability work: NO accept/reject surface change, so Core #9's three-lane tax does NOT apply.]** Today `Dict`/`Set` store hash-slot-primary (`keys`/`states` indexed by slot) plus a side vector `order` of slot indices. `gorget_map_remove` tombstones the slot (`states[idx] = 2`) and **never touches `order`**; `gorget_map_keys` walks `order[0..order_len]` skipping dead slots. So the *i*-th live element is NOT `order[i]` — **ordinal access is O(n)**, and `order_len ≥ count` with the slack clearing only at a rehash. Move to the **dense index-map layout**: a dense `entries` array in insertion order (no holes, length == `count`) plus a hash `indices` table mapping hash → position. Rank becomes O(1); iteration becomes a dense walk instead of a filtered one; memory drops from `keys[cap] + states[cap] + order[cap]` to `entries[count] + indices[cap]`. Well-known design — Rust `indexmap`, Zig `std.ArrayHashMap`, Python's compact dict, Swift `OrderedSet`, .NET 9 `OrderedDictionary`.
  - **RATIFIED SHAPE (D39), do not re-litigate:** six types, two axes, no aliases. Bare = ordered, `Hash` = unordered, `Stable` = ordered-and-stable-under-removal. `Dict`/`Set` → dense · `HashMap`/`HashSet` → **UNCHANGED** (open addressing + linear probing `idx = (idx+1) & mask` + tombstones + tombstone *reuse*, which ordered mode disables; plus no index-table indirection on lookup — that is their whole reason to exist) · `StableMap`/`StableSet` → today's tombstone layout preserved for its real profile (O(1) order-preserving removal + address stability), in its **own module `lib/std/stablemap.gg`** per the `std.slotmap` precedent, which tiers it out of `std.collections` and the book's collections chapter.
  - **`remove` preserves order (O(n)); `swap_remove` is the O(1) marked opt-in.** Not a choice — the reference already promises insertion-order iteration as a global invariant, and `Vector` already ships the identical pair. Keep the names (`fast_remove` rejected: name the hazard, not the reward). Unmarked = safe default, marked = opt-in.
  - **⚠ ORDINAL ACCESS IS `.nth(i)` / `.key_at(i)`, NOT `[]`. D38's `s[i]` reject STANDS — do not implement it as withdrawn and do not add positional `[]`.** The ambiguity is layout-independent and survives this change: `d[0]` = value for KEY 0 while `s[0]` = element at POSITION 0, same operator, same operand type. Consistent rule: `[]` takes the collection's *lookup key*; a set has none, so `[]` is undefined there. Do NOT name the accessor `.ordinal(…)` — `Ordinal` is taken (`src/semantic/traits.rs:627-637`, enum variant index) and runs the inverse direction. **Two sub-questions D39 leaves OPEN for the accessor phase (deliberately last):** (i) the exact name — `.nth(i)` is clean for a set, but on a map it must first answer *"returns what?"* (`key_at` / `value_at` / `entry_at`); (ii) **do `HashSet`/`HashMap` get an ordinal accessor at all?** Recommendation: **no** — they stay open-addressed under D39, so it would be O(n) *and* return an arbitrary element, i.e. a footgun wearing an O(1)-looking name; same reasoning as their `[]` reject. The escape hatch is `.items()`, documented "(unordered)", where the arbitrariness is visible at the call site.
  - **SCOPE, measured 2026-08-03:** ~79 lines touching `order`/`states` in `src/backend/c/runtime/runtime_map.c`, 9 in `runtime_set.c`; struct at `src/backend/c/runtime/runtime_preamble.c:357-377`, **mirrored in `src/lir/types.rs:143,170`** (two shapes carry an `order_len` field — Layering rule 3 duplication to keep in sync or unify). Splitting dense from tombstone also removes today's `if (m->order)` dual-mode branching, so each implementation ends up simpler than the one that serves both.
  - **⚠ INTERACTION THAT NEEDS DESIGN, not mechanical porting:** `try_resolve_index_element_ptr` admits `Array | OrderedMap | Map`, i.e. Gorget hands out **interior pointers into map storage** for `&d[k]` write-through. Tombstoning never relocates; dense `shift_remove` relocates every subsequent entry. Lands on the filed R6 realloc-UAF axis → owes ASan fixtures, not reasoning. (Partly bounded by the ratified transient-view model: no stored borrows, D10 exclusivity, so a view should not outlive the statement.)
  - **MEASUREMENT THE SCOUT OWES (end-to-end, per the scout rule — build AND run, never source-read):** (1) **the lookup-indirection cost.** Dense adds a *dependent* load on every `contains`/`get` (hash → `indices` → position → `entries`) but probes a compact index array instead of scanning wide key storage — so large keys likely favour dense and small keys (`Set[int]`) likely favour today's. Direction is not assumable; measure both, by key size. (2) Does any real corpus/self-host/dogfood workload do **order-preserving-removal-heavy** work on an ordered map? That is the only profile dense makes worse, and it decides how prominent `StableMap` needs to be. (3) Peak RSS + alloc counts alongside wall-clock, per the perf rule.
  - **NAMED FALLBACK, with a lower bound behind it:** O(1) rank *and* O(1) order-preserving removal is **impossible** — order-preserving removal decrements every subsequent rank, so O(1) rank requires materialised ranks and one removal invalidates O(n); this is dynamic partial sums / list indexing, with a known logarithmic-ish lower bound (Fredman–Saks, cell-probe). Therefore a **Fenwick tree over live flags** — O(log n) rank *and* O(log n) removal, tombstones retained so nothing relocates and address stability survives — is essentially **optimal** for wanting both. Carry it as the named alternative if (2) says remove-heavy ordered workloads are real. Not the default: O(log n) `[]` invites the same "is this cheap?" confusion as O(n).
  - **DOC WRITE-THROUGH owed:** `Dict`/`Set` reference entries gain the cost table and the `.nth()`/`.key_at()` accessors; `std.stablemap` gets its own reference section stating its profile AND that it is **not** ordinally indexable ("Stable" does not convey that on its own); book collections chapter stays four types. D38's no-positional-indexing paragraph needs **no** change under the ratified shape.
  - Own scout → brief → ≥3 fresh reviews → gauntlet, **per phase**. The three phases are filed as their own bullets below (one bullet per declared phase — a single bullet covering all three would make each landing read as `net +0`; see `scripts/convergence.sh` header).
- **⭐🆕 [MED — D39 PHASE B of 3: extract `StableMap`/`StableSet` into `lib/std/stablemap.gg`.]** Preserve today's tombstone layout as the specialist type (O(1) order-preserving removal + address stability, O(n) ordinal access), in its own module per the `std.slotmap` precedent so it stays out of `std.collections` and the book's collections chapter. Owes a reference section stating its profile AND that it is **not** ordinally indexable ("Stable" does not convey that). Prereq: Phase A (the dense path must exist before the tombstone path becomes the alternative).
- **⭐🆕 [MED — D39 PHASE C of 3: the `.nth(i)` / `.key_at(i)` ordinal accessors.]** Small and additive, and the phase that actually delivers the capability D38 withholds from `[]`. Two sub-questions still OPEN and to be decided in this phase, not before: the exact name (`.nth(i)` is clean for a set; on a map it must first answer *"returns what?"* — `key_at`/`value_at`/`entry_at`), and whether `HashSet`/`HashMap` get one at all (recommendation: **no** — they stay open-addressed, so it would be O(n) returning an arbitrary element; escape hatch is `.items()`, documented unordered). Also updates `E_NotIndexable`'s message to name the accessors concretely (see the filed diagnostic-ergonomics entry). Prereq: Phase A.
- **🆕 [MED — SCC residual of Round X's Track I, MEASURED at HEAD 2026-07-28] `W_RecursiveBareParamMaterialize` covers DIRECT self-recursion only; a mutual cycle pays the identical cost in silence.** Probe: `ping(d, Big b){ mutate(&b); pong(d-1, b) }` / `pong` symmetric, depth 200 → **201 array clones, ZERO warnings**; the byte-identical shape recursing directly DOES warn. Generalise the predicate from "reaches a bare-borrow arg of a direct self-call" to "…of a call inside this function's SCC" — the SCC is a by-product of the call-graph pass the cost-contract summary layer needs anyway (entry below), so this is the cheapest first consumer of it. Fixtures: mirror the 4 POS / 3 NEG set from `1c594a4f` with a two-function cycle, RED-verify per Core #12.
- **⭐ [HIGH — CoW COST CONTRACT campaign; design note `docs/internals/cow-cost-contract.md`, knob spelling OWNER-CHOSEN 2026-07-28, rest LEANING/unratified, NOT implemented] The 201-clone recursive materialize now WARNS (Round X `W_RecursiveBareParamMaterialize`) but is not ELIDED; the elision precondition is already computed and spent on a diagnostic.** **MEASURED 2026-07-28** (build with `--clones=stats`, run, read `[clone-stats] array_clone=`; probe sources inline in the design note): a bare-param `Big{Vector[int]}` mutated via `mutate(&b)` costs **one clone per frame** — linear recursion depth 200 = **201** clones where hand-optimal (`Big !b` worker + one explicit boundary clone) = **1**; branching depth 12 = **8191** vs hand-optimal **4096**. All variants observationally identical. It is a TIME bomb, not a space bomb (each frame's copy dies at frame exit → peak RSS is O(depth); RSS instrumentation would never show it). **The branching 2× is a hard ceiling** — the exponential is a property of the program's meaning (the first child's copy is genuinely observable), so no optimizer goes below `2^d`; the LINEAR case is fully reclaimable O(depth)→O(1). ⚠ **Same shape blew the self-host bootstrap's 600s stage deadline** (recursive `check_safety_stmt`, deep `ScopeTable__clone` per frame — devbook/11 Consumer #1 row, historical figure), found by a test deadline because no diagnostic exists. **ROOT/OPPORTUNITY:** the `MoveSuggestion` advice + `fn_consumed_params` sidecar substrate were DELETED in Round XXIV Track A (the advice was `E_OwnershipMismatch` at a bare param 100% of the time — the whole mechanism retired per `docs/internals/cow-cost-contract.md` §2's own ruling). The elision consumer now builds fresh typed signature metadata per `docs/internals/cow-cost-contract.md` §1 — no substrate to inherit. **DESIGN (4 layers, in the note):** (1) per-signature ownership summary — `materializes_param[i]` + `returns_view_of[i]` as typed signature fields over the monomorphized call graph with a fixed point for recursion (layering rules 3+4); (2) arg-side elision, predicate `materializes_param[i] ∧ is_last_use ∧ ¬returns_view_of[i]` — **the third conjunct is load-bearing**: for a method `self` IS arg 0, so eliding a receiver whose callee returns a view of self hands away the buffer and the returned view DANGLES (two sidecar tables would have shipped this; one summary cannot); (3) the **guaranteed-elision set** specified in `language-reference.md` + pinned by clone-count fixtures (the C++17 mandatory-elision move — turns `--clones` from a debugging aid into a conformance surface); (4) the knob **`@implicit_clones(allow|warn|deny)`** / `directive implicit-clones=X` / `--implicit-clones=X`, explicit `.clone()` exempt, contract = *"every copy in this function is one I wrote"*. ⚠ **§3 BEFORE §4 IS NOT NEGOTIABLE** — without the specified set, `deny` pins user code to optimizer internals and every analysis improvement is a potential breaking change. **Knob rules:** interprocedural-or-it's-a-lie (un-summarizable extern/`Callable`/vtable callees FAIL the annotation — error-biased checker vs the optimizer's clone-biased materialize-when-unsure; extern gets an *asserted* form kept distinct from the *proven* one); **transitive guarantee, non-transitive obligation** (summaries propagate the fact bottom-up so a wrapper 3 levels up is covered; callees need no annotation — transitive obligation is the `const fn`/`constexpr` annotate-the-world disease, unnecessary here because a summary substitutes). **MERGE RULING:** #13 (return-boundary elision) merges INTO the cost note — same mechanism at the opposite end of the call, same substrate; the transient-view model KEEPS the legality axis and owns `returns_view_of[i]`. **Do NOT merge #13's gates** — #13 is self-described high-risk/UAF-prone at unproven ~3.5% yield; the arg side has no live alias and a measured 201×. **Phasing A(summary+arg elision, zero user-visible change) → B(spec+fixtures) → C(knob, `warn` burn-down before any `deny`) → D(#13 rides A).** Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews→gauntlet per layer.

#### 🆕 OWNER-APPROVED SUITE-SPEED OPTIMIZATIONS (filed 2026-07-20; scout NEXT, not mid-burn-down)
- **[MED — scout] Shared self-compile artifacts across the heavy self-host tests.** ~7 full driver self-compiles today (`self_host_bootstrap` + `bootstrap_fixed_point` [plain stage chain]; `self_host_clone_ceiling` [instrumented stage-0]; `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling` [instrumented stage-1]; `runtime_diff`+`lowerer_comparison`+`c_emit_comparison` [self-host corpus compile]) each rebuild their stage(s) independently — the biggest slice of the ~52-min debug sweep tail. Collapse to ~4 DISTINCT artifacts each computed ONCE, fanning the assertions out. NOT "one compile total": `bootstrap_fixed_point` irreducibly needs a multi-stage chain (a fixed point is proven by consecutive-stage byte-identity), and the ceiling tests need the instrumented (`--clones`) binary, not the plain one. The scout's real problem is test ISOLATION under cargo's parallel model: a memoized read-only fixture (`Once`) vs merging related assertions into one test. Owner-accepted 2026-07-20; keep every assertion — this is dedup, not a coverage cut. Serves self-host iteration speed (north-star-adjacent).
- **[MED — scout] `--clones=stats` unsupported under `--backend=llvm` → the LLVM lane's clone behavior is UNMEASURED by the ceilings (owner-filed 2026-07-20).** `gg build --clones=stats --backend=llvm` errors and exits (`src/main.rs:2703`). Root cause (from the code's own note): under LLVM's linking/interposition model the `__gorget_clone_site_hit` counter table would be sized for the wrong module's `CloneId` range → silent misattribution; the C path dodges this by keeping the hit fn `static`. Consequence: the clone-ceiling de-opt detectors (`self_host_clone_ceiling`, `self_host_stage1_clone_ceiling`) run on the C backend ONLY, so a clone-bomb reachable via LLVM-specific lowering would slip them — a real parity blind spot given backends are meant to be at parity. Compile-time modes (`sites`/`verbose`/`sites-tsv`) already work on LLVM (pre-backend). Fix: per-module `static` counter table under LLVM (mirror the C interposition-safe shape), or a module-scoped CloneId base offset so the table sizes correctly. NOT a runtime-workaround concern (opt-in instrument, off by default in emitted programs). Design the counter-table lifetime from `src/backend/llvm/` module boundaries; cite the C shape in `runtime_clone_sites.c`.
  - **[folded 2026-07-21 from the 2026-07-02 duplicate copy — impl steps, UNION]** **🧱 `--clones=stats` under `--backend=llvm` is REJECTED, not supported (filed by DEEP-1 slice 0, 2026-07-02).** The reject (clean error, was a silent no-op — even the aggregate `[clone-stats]` line never printed) lives at the `TODO(llvm-clone-stats)` site in `src/main.rs` (grep it). Real support path, per the comment there: (a) append `RUNTIME_CLONE_STATS` + `render_clone_sites_runtime(clone_site_count)` to the hand-composed LLVM runtime blob in `compile_llvm_pipeline` (`src/main.rs`), (b) declare `__gorget_clone_site_hit` as an external fn in the emitted LLVM IR, (c) survive that path's `.replace("static ", "")` strip — which would give the counter table + hit fn EXTERNAL linkage and let a shared-lib + exe build bind both to ONE wrongly-sized table (silent misattribution; the C path avoids this by keeping the hit fn `static`). Compile-time modes (`sites`/`verbose`/`sites-tsv=PATH`) already work under LLVM. Bounded; own scout.

#### 🆕 THE WASTED-CLONE DETECTOR (owner-proposed 2026-07-19; the 4th instrument — MED, own scout; lands BEFORE/WITH the SH bare-arg CoW fix as its meter)
- **Dynamic dead-clone detection: count clones where NEITHER source nor copy is written afterward — the clone was provably unnecessary.** The dual of the dead-write warning (write-to-copy-nobody-reads ⟷ copy-nobody-writes). Mechanism (opt-in `--clones=waste` or extend `stats`; zero-cost off): register (site-id, src-buf, new-buf) per instrumented clone + per-buffer dirty bit; every MUTATING runtime entry point (push/set/insert/string-mut — the funnel CoW already forces mutations through) dirties its buffer; at free/exit, an un-dirtied pair = a WASTED clone for that site. Report `[waste-stats]` + per-site TSV → new columns in `clone_attribution.sh`: (site, reason, hits, wasted, waste%). **Per-site waste% = the sharpest charter meter** (100%-waste site = proven borrow candidate; grades even justified categories — a 99%-wasted consuming-position clone = a liveness gap). v0 CAVEAT: direct field stores bypass runtime entry points → v0 under-detects mutations, so waste verdicts are LEADS needing verification, not auto-fix truths; scout decides funnel-only (cheap) vs backend mark-dirty-on-store in instrumented mode (precise). Scalars/trivials OUT of v1. Serves: the SH bare-arg gap burn-down (ranked per-lane waste list), the per-reason budgets (a quality dimension), and the eventual static-warn subset (intraprocedurally-provable wasted clones → the warning family, per the wasteful-warns/unsound-rejects ruling).

## Guards / lints / test-infra

- **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt width, filed by R42 Track C 2026-08-15] `Stmt::VarDecl`'s INITIALIZER has no Doc layer, so a wide declaration cannot break at all** — `String s = "<long literal>"` renders as one un-Doc'd span (`examples/toml_config.gg:4`, 128 cols). This is NOT the doctrinal unbreakable-atom escape and must not be filed as one: the literal FITS ALONE at its indent, and a `Doc` over `<type> <name> = <init>` would break the line. The R42 tail reserve cannot help — there is no width-decided render on the line to charge. Counted in the `Unbroken` category of `tests/lints.rs::fmt_no_new_over_budget_lines`; regenerate the tally with `cargo test --test lints fmt_no_new_over_budget_lines -- --nocapture`. ⚠ Distinct from the three siblings filed with it (pattern wrapping · enum tuple-variant fields · the pre-render start column) and from the `from x import` list, which never reaches a packer at all and is EXEMPT by ratified doctrine (its own entry below).
- **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt width, filed by R42 Track C 2026-08-15] `format_pattern` has NO Doc layer — pattern wrapping is an unimplemented feature, so a wide `case` arm cannot break** — `case Ctor(a, b, c, …)` at `tests/fixtures/parser_trailing_comma_ctor_pattern.gg:23` is 201 cols with no break opportunity anywhere. A missing capability, not a defect with an intended output, so no `known_gaps` repro is owed. Counted in the `Unbroken` category of `tests/lints.rs::fmt_no_new_over_budget_lines`. Sibling of the var-decl-initializer and enum-variant-field entries (same no-Doc-layer family, different arms).
- **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt width, found by R42 Track C's discovery sweep 2026-08-15] Enum TUPLE-VARIANT field lists have no Doc layer either** — `format_variant_tuple_fields` is a hand-rolled comma loop whose members go through `format_type`, and a `Type::Named` with no generic args emits plain text, so nothing on the line is fit-tested (`tests/fixtures/parser_trailing_comma_variant_fields.gg:35`, 142 cols). Same family as the `format_pattern` entry; found because the sweep enumerated every `emitter.write*` whose preceding emission is a render. Fix = route the field list through the delimited-list chokepoint (which also buys it the interior-comment gate), or give it its own Fill. Counted in the `Unbroken` category.
- **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt width, filed by R42 Track C 2026-08-15] A `Doc::Group`-clothed carrier's FIRST piece is pre-rendered for the CONTINUATION column but spliced at the CALLER's** — `sub_render` seeds the throwaway formatter's cursor at `base_indent * 4`, which is right for every later piece and WRONG for the first, because nothing precedes it in the Doc. Measured 23 columns of slack at `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower_expr.gg`'s `df_param_is_result` declaration (128 cols, unchanged by the R42 reserve): the sub-render believes it has 23 more columns than it does and keeps a nested call flat. A SIBLING ROOT of the caller-suffix class on the START-COLUMN axis — the reserve reaching that sub-render is already correct, so no reserve can close it. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_prerender_column_binary_chain.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `fmt_prerender_column_binary_chain_stays_in_budget` asserting the intended property; counted in the `Unbroken` category. Fix = a start-column parameter on `sub_render`, seeded from `current_col()` for the first piece. **Blast radius MEASURED (R42 output-review, independent probe build): 2 changed files, 2 changed hunks, over-budget lines 325 → 323, ZERO over-break and ZERO widened hunks** — regenerate with a probe build plus the over-break comparison over the four roots. The residual reason to defer is the CLASS, not risk: operand zero is one of several first-piece splice sites (the method chain's ROOT and the nil-coalesce LHS are the others), so it wants a scout that enumerates them rather than a one-site patch.

- **🆕🐛 [MED — Core #13 class, filed from the T-RB0 output-review 2026-08-11] FIVE `*_comparison` tests remain FLOORLESS always-pass diagnostics** (lexer · parser · type · check · lowerer — resolver was floored at 1865 by T-RB0 after its floorless gate hid a TOTAL parity collapse, `matched: 0` of 2079, for an unknown duration; c_emit was already floored). Each floorless gate is exactly the instrument class that cannot see its own failure. Fix = seed a floor per test via `parity_floor_active` from a freshly-printed count (the T-RB0 commit is the template); one revert-RED demo per floor.
- **🆕🐛 [MED — SH runtime lane, filed from the T-RB0 output-review 2026-08-11] The INDEXED-CALLEE callable shape TIMES OUT on the self-host runtime lane** — `arr[i](args)` through a `Vector[Callable[...]]` hangs the SH-emitted binary; measured while authoring the fn-type caret fixture, which was reshaped to a parameter-passed callable BECAUSE of this (both rejected shapes documented in that fixture's header; its `!` twin `callable_bang_arr_indexed_callee.gg` has never had a runtime snapshot either — same hang, pre-existing). Exposing fixture already in-tree; no `runtime_snapshots/callable_bang_arr_indexed_callee.out` exists. Root-cause on the SH lowering of indexed-callee dispatch; wire a snapshot when it runs.

### Medium
- **🐛 [MED — fuzz corpus coverage + scratch leak] `fuzz_roundtrip` gates its assert on `parser.errors.is_empty()`, so once D27 Round B REJECTS the retired `!` glyph every `!`-bearing seed in a local corpus will silently skip the roundtrip assert** — a coverage loss that shows up as "no findings", not as a failure. Whoever regenerates a local fuzz corpus after Round B must re-seed with `^` spellings and confirm the seeds still reach the assert (add a counter or a canary seed). Also: the seed set leaks 33 `.gorget/*.test-results.json` scratch files into the tree — clean them or gitignore them at the same time. (The crate's COMPILE bit-rot is separately gated now: `tests/lints.rs::fuzz_targets_still_compile` runs `cargo check --locked` over all three fuzz targets, ungated, ~10s.)

- **🧹 [MED — burn `no_growth_in_phase_d_proxy_reads` back DOWN; the gate itself is GREEN] Migrate the migratable proxy reads to `Local.ownership`.** ⚠ **STATUS CORRECTED 2026-07-27: this entry read "🔴 RED NOW — `cargo test --test lints` fails". It does not fail — the lint PASSES at HEAD** (`cargo test --test lints no_growth_in_phase_d_proxy_reads` → ok), and the handover block above already records why: the red was closed 2026-07-26 by raising the ceiling 85→93 WITH per-site attribution (`0b71e1f0`). The attribution half of this entry is therefore done; only the burn-down remains. **What remains:** `is_named_local` / `is_owned_local` are readable off `Local.ownership` today and should be migrated; `drops.is_registered` / `drops.is_moved` are DROP-ACCOUNTANT state with no `LocalOwnership` equivalent, so they are blocked on making the drop accountant queryable off `Local` — do that first or the burn-down stalls halfway.
