- **🆕🧹 [Core #6 guard — CI-triage 2026-07-23] The ggdef corpus gates drift RED whenever a round adds an out-of-subset `cow_*` fixture, and the STOP-and-report is FAIL-FAST so each fix reveals the next.** `gate_fixtures` auto-discovers every `cow_*`/`deadwrite_*` fixture (by design — a new fixture must be adjudicated or explicitly excluded), but the EXCLUDE lists are hand-maintained **in two files** (`spec/ggdef/tests/corpus_b.rs`, `corpus_b1.rs`) — a parallel-list smell (Layering rule 3). This has now bitten at least twice: corpus_b1's own comment records the CoW-2G rows landing without a refresh, and the 2026-07-21 round landed FOUR (`cow_getref_dict_writethrough` + the three `cow_trait_*`) leaving CI red 2 days. Wanted: (a) report ALL out-of-subset fixtures in one run instead of panicking on the first (collect + assert once, like the corpus tables do for MISMATCH) so a refresh is one pass, not N; (b) one shared exclusion source of truth keyed by fixture with its gate-citation, consumed by both gates; (c) ideally derive the exclusion from a frontmatter/`known_gaps` marker on the fixture itself so adding the fixture and declaring its subset status is ONE edit at the source.
- **🆕⚖ [LOW — adjudication coverage] Promote the `retborrow_*` boundary fixtures into the ggdef adjudication corpus.** They are CoW-boundary fixtures but are deliberately named `retborrow_*` rather than `cow_*` so they do not drift the two auto-discovering ggdef gates (each hard-asserts a fixture count). Promoting them means renaming to the `cow_` family AND refreshing both counts + `EXCLUDE` lists with a cited comment, per fixture, after checking each is in ggdef's subset. Worth doing once the shared-exclusion cleanup above lands — doing it before just doubles the hand-maintained parallel lists.

- **🆕🐛 [macOS dev-box false RED] `security_safe_no_leak` mis-reports on any platform without LeakSanitizer.** The 5 `cow_bareassign_*_no_leak` tests fail on macOS with `AddressSanitizer: detect_leaks is not supported on this platform` — the harness's `has_leak_report` scrape (`tests/security.rs:271-275`) matches the substring `"AddressSanitizer"`, which that very *unsupported* notice contains, so the guard fires on its own inapplicability. Real leaks are still gated on Linux/CI. Fix: detect the unsupported notice and SKIP (loudly) rather than fail, or gate the no-leak assertions on `cfg!(target_os = "linux")` the way `spec_conformance`'s `floor_active` already does — a permanently-red local suite trains contributors to ignore it.

  - **N3 (precision, low-pri):** `find_collection_source_with_path` matches `get`/`first`/`last`/`unwrap`/`expect` by NAME — a user type with a `get()` method receiver-arena-scoped+non-Copy also fires sec_19 (errs SAFE). Acceptable; note only.
  - **🐛 NEW (same scout) N1 sub-hole (a): CLOSURE-BODY carve-out escape.** `check_expr.rs` saves+ZEROES `arena_depth` walking `Closure`/`Do` bodies (~:844-875), so a closure defined AND called inside `with Arena` that assigns a materialization to a mut-captured outer var (`outer = f"esc-{1}"` in the body, then `f()`) compiles clean → ASan heap-UAF (probe `p18_closure_escape.gg` in the probes tarball). The zeroing is half-right (a closure may run after/outside the block); conservative-correct fix direction: keep the zeroing for the body walk but gate ESCAPING mut-captures of outer vars at closure-definition-inside-arena, or track "closure defined under arena_depth>0" and apply the producer at its call sites inside the block. NOT covered by the landed core (which gates statement/expr positions only). Probe `p18_closure_escape.gg` in `/tmp/recover_r33_arenaescape_probes.tar.gz`.
  - **🐛 NEW (same scout) N1 sub-hole (b): nested-arena depth-blindness (LOGICAL, ASan-INVISIBLE).** `arena_scoped_vars` is a flat set — no depth tag — so inner-arena→outer-arena-var escapes (`vo = !vi` with `vo` under the OUTER `with Arena`, `vi` under the INNER) skip every gate (`target ∈ arena_scoped_vars`). Runtime happens to read valid bytes because the inner arena's storage is drawn FROM the outer arena (nesting rule) and bump free is a no-op — logically dangling, physically intact until the OUTER destroy, invisible to ASan without arena poisoning (probes `p17`/`p17b`: check-clean, run-CLEAN). Fix (follow-up to the landed N1 core): tag `arena_scoped_vars` values with their `arena_depth` and reject on depth mismatch (inner→outer), or poison arena regions on destroy under `--sanitize`.

- **🛡 STRUCTURAL GUARD — producer-side leak validator (design grounded; NEEDS the ≥3-pass plan-review before execute).** Converts the leak-freedom campaign from per-site hand-fixes to a build-breaking invariant. **Invariant:** every value produced as `Owned`/`FreshOwned` of a droppable type must, before fn exit, be registered for drop (`Drop`/`DropIfAlive`) OR moved out (`MoveZero`). Existing guards police CONSUME (Tier 2a `validate_consume_sites`, 2c `validate_drop_pre_rebind`) + TYPE (`validate_drop_completeness`); NONE policies PRODUCTION (scout-confirmed gap). **SELF-HOST FIRST:** add `Vector[String] validate_owned_temps_registered_or_moved(GirModule &gmod)` to `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/validate.gg` (wired into `run_validators`/`validate_gir_after` `:288`/`:361`), keying on `is_droppable_type` (`lower.gg:1161`), scanning `GIDrop`/`GIDropIfAlive`/`GIMoveZero`, gated `GG_VALIDATE_OWNED_TEMP_DROP`. **Rust sibling** in `src/ir/validate.rs` reusing `collect_drop_registered_locals` (`:1999`) + a `MoveZero` scan; owned-ness on `Local.ownership ∈ {Owned,FreshOwned}` (`src/ir/mod.rs:447/667`), droppability on `needs_drop`/`is_resource_type`. Lifecycle (devbook/25): env-gate→triage→burn to 0→fatal. **Risks:** (1) lights up the `!`-param-no-drop gap (`drop_loop_reinit`) — blocks 'fatal' until closed/carved; (2) registered-but-conditionally-moved must PASS; (3) Rust-only str-view-escape (`drops.rs:226-228`) + CoW-string-alias (`assigns.rs:660-687/706/730`) carve-outs (self-host emits `GIMoveZero` on every move so unaffected); (4) static-literal `*_materialize` triage. RUN-verify vs ASan; gate on `fixed_point`.

## Concurrency

### High

- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — MEMORY-UNSAFE, DOUBLE-FREE at scope exit, SELF-HOST LANE ONLY; filed 2026-07-29 by Round XIV ride-along scout] A struct holding a `Mutex[T]` field alongside ANY OTHER droppable field (`String`, `Vector[int]`, …) aborts with `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` (exit 134, SIGABRT) at scope exit under the self-host lane. Rust gg (both backends) exits 0 with the correct stdout on the identical program.** RED-VERIFIED 2026-07-29 at HEAD (`21214388`): Rust C stdout `hello-heap-forced` exit 0, Rust LLVM likewise, SH driver→cc→run stderr `free(): double free detected in tcache 2` exit 134. Reproduces WITHOUT any `.lock()` call — pure drop-path defect. Field order does not matter (String-then-Mutex and Mutex-then-String both abort). Discriminator is the DROPPABLE-COMPANION AXIS: Mutex alone (SH exit 0), Mutex + `int` companion (SH exit 0), Mutex + String OR Vector companion (SH exit 134). Distinct from Round 14 Inc-A `Mutex[T]` scope-drop (that landed for owning LOCALS in both lanes; this is the STRUCT-FIELD position, a companion-field axis not covered by Inc-A). Hypothesised root (NOT chased to write-site — do not treat as established): the SH struct-drop lowering double-emits the Mutex-field's `gorget_mutex_free`, or mis-orders per-field drops when a droppable sibling is present; only single-owner Mutex `abort()`s on the second free (any refcounted companion would silently under/over-count). Companion families not yet probed: RWLock/Channel/Shared/Weak (a per-companion sibling census belongs to whichever round fixes this, Core #12). **DURABLE REPRO:** `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/sh_mutex_struct_field_double_free.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `sh_mutex_struct_field_double_free` in `tests/integration.rs` (wired via `self_host_emit_cc_run`, asserts SH parity to Rust). Un-ignore + graduate out of `known_gaps/` when the SH struct-drop path stops double-freeing Mutex handles in the presence of a droppable sibling field.

- **🆕 ROUND-33 shared-override follow-ups (filed at inc-1 landing per output-review):** (a) self-host ESpawn raw-passthrough is WHOLE-CALL not per-arg (mixed auto+override spawn would mis-pass the auto carrier raw); (b) `lower_shared_spawn` doesn't gate on kind (`&x` of an override-kind var → mutex-shaped token wrapper → loud cc-fail, not silent); (c) **Rust-side Core #8 candidate: Rust gg rwlock compound-assign takes the WRITE LOCK BEFORE lowering the RHS (`assigns.rs:1085-1105`) → `x += f(x)` SELF-DEADLOCKS in Rust; the self-host lowers RHS first and is correct — fix Rust.** (a)/(b) subsumed by the round-16 real-spawn keystone but distinct sites.

- **🆕 ROUND-39 T2 spawn/blocking follow-ups (filed at landing per brief; the +5 fixtures now MATCH, these are the residual semantic gaps — each output-invisible on the current corpus):**
  - **(1) Synchronous inline-closure-spawn model.** The self-host lowers `spawn ((): body)()` (inline closure literal) SYNCHRONOUSLY — the closure body runs on the CURRENT thread at the spawn site and the paired `.await()` is a no-op (the `case EClosure` arm in `lower_expr.gg` ESpawn→ECall now dispatches `__callable_<arity>` inline, same model as the already-passing `spawn_closure_copy`/`spawn_closure_void` closure-VAR path). Rust threads it via `build_spawn_wrapper` (a real spawned thread). CORRECT-OUTPUT ONLY for concurrency-agnostic fixtures — the 3 that landed (`spawn_closure_inline`/`spawn_unchecked`/`spawn_unchecked_bypasses_check`) all await immediately after spawn, so output is deterministic regardless of sync-vs-thread. A fixture whose output depends on real parallelism/interleaving would be right-by-luck. Fix = materialize the inline closure into a real spawn wrapper (mirror Rust `build_spawn_wrapper`); pairs with the Group-A pool gap below.
  - **(2) Anonymous awaited-String-temp leak (14B on `spawn_blocking_multi`) — PRE-EXISTING in the shared real-spawn/await path, NOT introduced by T2, NOT blocking-specific.** `print(t.await())` on a `Task[String]` leaks 14 bytes in 3 allocations (`str_alloc_copy` ← `gorget_file_read_all` ← `blocking_read`, via `__gorget_global_alloc_fn`): the awaited-String temp passed DIRECTLY to a call arg is never drop-registered. It is the anonymous-temp shape that leaks, NOT the await count — `spawn_blocking_basic` (`String result = t.await(); print(result)`) is ASan-CLEAN because the awaited result binds to a NAMED local that IS drop-registered (`gorget_string_free(&result)` emitted). **Re-verified 2026-07-04 by rebuilding the committed driver + ASan matrix: basic CLEAN, multi 14B → 4/5 clean.** The 14B reproduces IDENTICALLY in a regular (NON-blocking) `spawn areader(...)` awaited inline in `print` (`reg_inline` repro), and vanishes when that same regular spawn binds the result to a named local (`reg_named` clean) — so the leak lives in the shared await-result-temp lowering, exercised by both regular `spawn` and `spawn blocking`. Same CLASS as the already-filed trait-call-return-value temp leak (`print(s.speak())`, in this file's ROLLING queue item 6) — a call/await result temp consumed as an arg isn't drop-registered. Fix = drop-register the owned result temp at the consuming call-arg / auto-prop site (must be conditional to avoid a double-free when the result also binds to a VarDecl — deep drop-machinery, not a spawn-local patch; do NOT chase in a spawn track). ⚠ A pre-fix bare-passthrough driver mis-attributes this as a 21B `spawn_blocking_basic` "sync-inline" leak — that is the OLD behavior (direct `blocking_read` call, no spawn runtime); rebuild the driver from the committed `lower_expr.gg` before measuring.
  - **(3) Group-A blocking-pool routing divergence.** T2 routes `spawn blocking f()` through the SAME `lower_named_fn_spawn` wiring that regular `spawn` uses — main() emits REAL spawn/await (`__gorget_spawn_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_SUBMIT` on a pthread-backed `GorgetTask`, `__gorget_await_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_WAIT`; NOT sync-inline), but on the SAME fixed-size executor with no blocking-pool accounting. Rust routes it to an EXPANDABLE blocking pool (`blocking_fn_names`/`has_blocking_pool` → `BLOCKING_POOL_RUNTIME`). OUTPUT-INVISIBLE for the 5 fixtures (the emitted VALUE is identical — the awaited real return value — so the flip is reference-grade on OUTPUT), but a latent LIVENESS gap: fixed-executor saturation under many concurrent blocking tasks could deadlock where Rust's expandable pool would not. Fix = give the self-host a blocking-pool tag + expandable-pool runtime, or document the fixed-executor semantics as intentional.

PLUS the wrapper-routing bug `helpers.rs:250` (`Shared__Mutex__T__drop`→`gorget_shared_drop` frees only the 8B data buffer, not the inner mutex — route to `gorget_shared_mutex_drop` when `type_name.contains("__Mutex__")`; the runtime fn already exists). - **🆕 ROUND-16 PHASE-2 FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-30 by the Phase-2 await-splice output-review `aad66092`; the splice itself LANDED `0a8ee959` — see DONE; both are narrowings vs the Rust ref, NOT exercised by the 2 shipped fixtures, so not a shipped-behavior defect — but both are genuine pending gaps):**
  - **🐛 LATENT Core #8 soundness gap — the self-host body-await detector misses if-expr/block-expr/closure-expr suspensions.** `stmts_have_await`/`expr_has_await` (`lower_generics.gg:~1953`) counts `EAwait` + blocking-call names but omits the block-expr, if-expr, and closure-expr forms the Rust ref covers (`src/ir/lowering/context.rs:3722-3759`). An async callee whose ONLY suspension is buried in an if-expression VALUE (or block/closure expr) mis-routes to the SYNC `__shared_token_` path → **lock held across the await** (the exact deadlock the design prevents). Not reachable by `shared_with_spawned_refresh`/`shared_nested_spawn` (statement-level awaits), so latent — but a real Core #8 hole. Fix = extend the self-host detector to mirror `expr_has_await`'s expr-form coverage; add a fixture with an if-expr-value suspension in a spawned async callee.

A multi-shared-arg async callee would silently process only the first. Mirror the loop. So this is **wrong output `0,0` + a residual closure-env leak**, NOT a memory-safety crash.   - **🐛 Core #8 (NEW, filed by round-17 scout `a3b21138`, MEASURED in BOTH compilers) — `Shared[Vector[T]]` / `Shared[<resource>]` LEAKS its inner payload (64B for `shared_vector_elem`'s inner Vector buffer, IDENTICAL in self-host + Rust gg under LSan).** `gorget_shared_drop` frees the handle + the 8B data buffer but NEVER runs the inner type's drop fn on last-ref → the payload leaks. Both backends agree on the leaky answer → ≥2-bug, NOT a divergence. stdout still MATCHes (so Inc-1 above ships `shared_vector_elem` as a clean stdout-flip; this leak is ASan-only, orthogonal). Reference-grade fix = `gorget_shared_drop` runs the inner drop fn when `strong` hits 0, in the runtime (shared by both compilers) — needs the inner-type drop-fn threaded to the Shared carrier.     - **SEMANTICS ARE SETTLED — a lock IS a Resource (do NOT re-open as an "owner decision"; my earlier framing was over-cautious):** `language-design.md:319` (Resource = "owns a heap allocation, file handle, **or lock** — cannot be implicitly copied"); `devbook/18-runtime-abi.md:84-99` (Mutex is an opaque handle COVERING a runtime-owned allocation — "the runtime owns both halves"); `book/14-concurrency.md:135` + `book/16-smart-pointers.md:75` (the ARC owns the Mutex, "dropped when the last owner goes away"); `language-reference.md:3589` (guards drop to release the lock). The runtime ships a `gorget_mutex_new`/`gorget_mutex_free` PAIR and never calls free. The lone dissenter is the type metadata (`builtins.rs:587-588` `MUTEX = CopySemantics::Trivial, drop_fn: None`; self-host mirrors via `GtPtr(Mutex__int64_t)` + `is_droppable_type(GtPtr)=false` `lower_drops.gg:160-163`). The double-free worry is INVERTED: Resource = no-implicit-copy → single owner → single drop, so the Resource classification PREVENTS the double-free.

So this is not a bare-Mutex corner — the ARC-wrapped canonical form leaks too.

The fix must teach the `Shared` content-drop path to deep-drop its inner (which then frees Mutex AND any droppable inner uniformly), in BOTH compilers, alongside flipping `MUTEX`→`Resource`+`gorget_mutex_free` (and the self-host `GtPtr`→droppable + `drop_fn_for_type` Mutex/RWLock arms + `GIDropIfAlive` ptr-slot load). Confirm there's no path where a bare Mutex handle is a NON-owning view (`devbook/18:98` notes some opaque handles ARE non-owning — but `gorget_mutex_new` always-allocates says Mutex is owning; RUN-confirm).
    - **(c) PREREQUISITE — build the leak gate FIRST (the fix is unverifiable without it):** `self_host_runtime` snapshots check STDOUT only → leak-blind (same structural blind spot as the DIAG-blind `*_comparison` harness). - **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt form-changing round trip; found by the R42 Track-D brief-review pass 2, orchestrator-verified at HEAD 2026-08-14] `gg fmt` drops the trailing comma of a ONE-TUPLE TYPE: `(int,) t3 = (5,)` → `(int) t3 = (5,)`.** The VALUE spelling keeps its comma; only the TYPE loses it, so `(int)` re-parses as a parenthesized type, not a 1-tuple — form-changing, and the re-parsed program may not typecheck. Pre-existing (reproduces identically before any R42 track lands); distinct from the paren-deletion family (that is EXPRESSION grouping parens via `parse_paren_expr`; this is the `Type::Tuple` single-element EMITTER — likely the type-side twin of the `(x,)` value special-case). Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_one_tuple_type_comma_drop.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `fmt_one_tuple_type_keeps_comma` (RED-verified at HEAD: formatted decl reads `(int) t3`). ⚠ SEVERITY RAISED 2026-08-15 (R42 Track-D pass-5 measured the return-type sibling): `(int,) one(): return (5,)` formats to `int one(): return 5` and `gg check` flips **OK → E_TypeMismatch on fmt's own output** — an accept→reject flip, not just a comma drop; fmt output must never change accept/reject (Core #8). ⚠ FAMILY WIDENED 2026-08-15 (Track-D pass-9): the paren churn spans MULTI-tuple type/value positions too — `(int, int) pair(…)` → `int, int pair(…)` and `return (a, b)` → `return a, b` (form-changing; these reparse OK, unlike the 1-tuple cells). Other unsampled cells: param type / generic arg. Fix direction: the type emitter's tuple arms keep the author's parens/comma exactly like the value emitter's.

### High


- **🆕🚨 [HIGH — manifest error-swallowing + no format-evolution path; ALL FOUR CELLS MEASURED 2026-08-08 by direct `gg check` probes; durable repro committed] `gorget.toml` is parsed leniently and its parse ERRORS are discarded, so a broken manifest surfaces as a misleading diagnostic about an unrelated file.** ⭐ **(a) THE DEFECT — malformed manifest silently dropped.** `Manifest::from_path` returns a perfectly good `ManifestError::Parse` ("invalid gorget.toml at `<path>`", `src/manifest.rs:43`+`:55`), but `src/main.rs:174` discards it with `if let Ok(manifest)` — dependency resolution is skipped entirely and the user gets `cannot read '<app>/greeter.gg': No such file or directory`, pointing at a path that was never meant to exist. Durable repro `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/manifest_malformed/` (app + greeter packages; the ONLY defect in `app/gorget.toml` is an unclosed `[package` bracket), wired `#[ignore]`d as `manifest_malformed_is_reported` in `tests/integration.rs` asserting the INTENDED `invalid gorget.toml`. **RED-verified** — fails at HEAD with exactly the misleading missing-file message. **Positive control run:** swapping in a VALID manifest makes the identical layout `check` clean (exit 0), so the unclosed bracket is the discriminator, not the fixture layout. **(b) Same root cause —** a manifest MISSING the required `version` field is silently accepted (measured: builds fine). **(c) INCONSISTENT ACROSS COMMANDS —** `gg add` / `gg remove` DO report the parse error (`src/main.rs:2380`, `:2409` use `unwrap_or_else` + eprintln), so the same broken file is fatal to one command and invisible to another. **(d) NO `deny_unknown_fields`** on `Manifest` / `PackageInfo` (`src/manifest.rs:12-23` — only `#[serde(default)]` on `dependencies` and `#[serde(untagged)]` on `DepSpec`): unknown keys (`edition = "3"`, `future_field = {...}`) AND unknown top-level tables (`[workspace]`) are silently ignored — all measured accepted. **(e) NO schema/edition field AT ALL** — `PackageInfo` is `{name, version}`, so the manifest format has no evolution path: a file written for a future `gg` is silently misread rather than rejected, and there is no `edition`-style knob to switch semantics on. ⭐ **FIX SHAPE:** propagate `ManifestError` at the `resolve_deps_for_file` load path instead of swallowing it (that alone closes (a) and (b) and makes the commands consistent per (c)); then decide the strictness/evolution policy for (d)+(e). **Borrow the discipline `compiler/data/resources.gg` already has** — it carries `SCHEMA_VERSION` validated at load against `SCHEMA_VERSION_EXPECTED` (`src/resources.rs:28`, `:62-66`), which is exactly what the manifest lacks; Rust's `edition` is the same idea. ⚠ **Policy is a genuine choice, not a bug:** `deny_unknown_fields` (strict, breaks forward-compat) vs. warn-on-unknown vs. ignore-forward-compat-gated-on-an-`edition`-field. Own scout → owner call on (d)+(e); (a)-(c) are unambiguous defects and can land immediately. ⭐ **(f) 🆕 SAME FILE, TWO DIFFERENT DEPENDENCY UNIVERSES — the manifest lookup depends on the INVOCATION FORM, not the file (measured 2026-08-08).** `resolve_deps_for_file` (`src/main.rs:169`) does `input_path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new("."))`, but `Path::new("main.gg").parent()` returns `Some("")`, NOT `None` — so the `"."` fallback NEVER fires, `find_project_root` starts at `""`, and its `dir.pop()` returns false on the first iteration, aborting the walk before it begins. Measured on one identical file: `cd proj && gg check main.gg` FAILS to resolve the dep, while `gg check a/b/c/d/proj/main.gg` and the absolute-path form both SUCCEED. Fix: canonicalize the input path before deriving `start_dir` (or filter the empty parent). ⚠ **Under D43 C2 this escalates from a packaging annoyance to a SEMANTICS defect** — the manifest would define the visibility boundary, so the same source file would get different privacy scoping depending on how the developer typed the command. **(g) 🆕 THE UPWARD WALK IS UNBOUNDED** — `manifest::find_project_root` (`src/manifest.rs:104-118`) climbs to the filesystem ROOT with no `.git`/mount/depth boundary, so a stray manifest anywhere above a project silently claims it (measured: a manifest 6 levels up supplied the dependency graph for a deep source file). Bound the walk at a repo/filesystem boundary. Both (f) and (g) are prerequisites for C2 regardless of the manifest's FILENAME, and (g) is what makes a generic filename risky: the more generic the name, the likelier an unrelated ancestor file wins the walk. Durable repro owed for both — directory-tree shape like `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/manifest_malformed/`, asserting invocation-independence and a bounded walk. ⭐ **Related decision already made (2026-08-08 consult): the manifest STAYS TOML, not Gorget syntax.** Rationale on record — its readers are not all `gg` (registries, CI, editors, scanners); `lib/xtd/toml.gg` (1788 lines) already lets the self-host read TOML, which defuses the one argument that made `resources.gg` Gorget (single source of truth shared by two compilers, one written in Gorget); and `resources.gg` is itself consumed as INERT data — `src/resources.rs:7` walks "the literal-only subset", never evaluates — so the precedent supports a data-only format, not a manifest-as-program. Survey finding: every package manager that made its manifest executable was later forced to generate a declarative sidecar (Arch `.SRCINFO`, Gentoo `md5-cache`, FreeBSD ports `INDEX`, Homebrew's JSON API, Python's `setup.py`→`pyproject.toml` under PEP 517/518/621); the declarative ones (Cargo, Maven, `go.mod`, Deb822 `control`) never needed one.

Blocks natural `from engine.sheet import` from arbitrary entries. Document in reference/book and/or add `src_roots`. Writeup: `the gorget-sheets snag report (git history)`. Own scout (design) → owner decision.
- **🆕🐛 [MED — fmt interior-comment escape: the residual OUTSIDE the fill-emitter chokepoint] every FILL-emitted delimited list is now gated at one chokepoint (`Formatter::emit_delimited_list`); the shapes below are NOT fill-emitted, so the gate does not reach them and an interior comment is still re-parented.** All symptoms below were MEASURED on the post-chokepoint binary. The gate's structural markers in the source are the `Gate::UngatedCarveOut` sites, pinned with per-site attribution by `formatter_list_emit_fill_census`; regenerate the emitter census with `awk '/^    fn /{fn=$0;sub(/^ +fn /,"",fn);sub(/[(<].*/,"",fn)} /write\(", "\)/{print NR"\t"fn}' src/formatter/mod.rs`.
  - **(1) PRE-RENDER-ABOVE — a list nested inside a construct that pre-renders its own sub-parts.** Root: `element_to_string` builds a sub-`Formatter` with an EMPTY comment side-table, and a method chain (2+ segments), a binary chain, an `??` chain and the three comprehensions each pre-render their sub-parts through it FIRST, so any list emitter reached from inside one gates against no comments. Fix shape: the same decide-before-`Doc` move ONE LEVEL UP — the chain / comprehension builder consults the side-table over its own span and falls back to an imperative broken emission. A different mechanism, not a missing call. Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_delimited_list_pre_render_above.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `fmt_delimited_list_pre_render_above` (all six costumes in ONE fixture, one root). ⚠ The chain costume MUST use 2+ segments — a single-segment receiver call is FIXED by the chokepoint and would be green on arrival.
  - **(2) `Expr::Index` brackets** — `v[i]` has one "element" and hand-written delimiters (`write("[")` / expr / `write("]")`), so it never reaches the chokepoint. Measured: `int r = v[\n    # c\n    0\n]` → `int r = v[0]` with `# c` dedented onto the next statement. Repro: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_delimited_list_index_brackets.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `fmt_delimited_list_index_brackets`.
  - **(3) THE HAND-ROLLED COMMA-LOOP EMITTERS — a THIRD mechanism, 23 sites, none gated.** Census (`write(", ")` sites, comment lines excluded, matching `EXPECTED_WRITE_SEP = 23`), by enclosing fn with a disposition per row: `format_item` ×4 (meta-type-call args, meta-type-func params, `meta assert` message, `meta log` args) — DEFER, exotic · `format_attributes` ×1 (attribute args) — DEFER · `format_function` ×1 (tuple RETURN type) — DEFER · `format_enum` ×1 (tuple-variant field list) — **REAL, MEASURED** · `format_import` ×1 (⚠ NOT the grouped-name loop, which an earlier wording claimed and which no longer exists — the group routes through `emit_delimited_list`; the sole `write(", ")` in `format_import` is in the **`From`** arm) — **NEVER**: `from a import X,` is a measured PARSE ERROR, and the list has no delimiters to wrap in, so it is neither a magic-comma nor a gate candidate until the ratified parenthesized form lands · `format_trait_bound` ×2 (generic args + assoc-type bindings) — DEFER · `format_stmt` ×7 (tuple destructure, tuple `return`, `for` tuple pattern, `with` bindings, `assert` message, `assert return` message, `meta log`) — DEFER · `format_type` ×2 (tuple TYPE, function TYPE params) — DEFER · `format_pattern` ×3 (enum-pattern fields, tuple pattern, dot-shorthand pattern fields) — **REAL, MEASURED** · `format_closure_param` ×1 (destructure bindings) — DEFER. Measured snippet, both real rows in one input, post-chokepoint output:
    ```
    enum Shape:                        enum Shape:
        Point(                             Point(int, int)
            int,                           # y coordinate      ← now documents Blob
            # y coordinate         →       Blob(int)
            int
        )                              ...
        Blob(int)                          case Shape.Point(x, y): print(x + y)
    ...                                    # why two           ← now documents the
        case Shape.Point(                  case Shape.Blob(n): print(n)   NEXT ARM
            x,
            # why two
            y
        ): print(x + y)
    ```
    Fix shape: elements here are `Spanned`, so these are genuine chokepoint candidates — route them through `emit_delimited_list` (or a single-slot variant) rather than gating each loop. Zero corpus instances today (see the census below), so this is correctness debt, not a live corruption.

    **⊕ A SECOND, INDEPENDENT AXIS ON THE SAME SITES: THE MAGIC TRAILING COMMA (ratified 2026-08-16).** A trailing comma is now the AUTHOR'S signal to keep a list exploded, and it is honoured at the `emit_delimited_list` chokepoint — so at every position that does NOT route through it the formatter goes on DELETING a token that now MEANS something. That is the Core #10 silent-drop harm class, newly visible to users because the canon just gave the token a meaning, and it is a reason to route these loops that is independent of the comment escape above. Routing a kind through the chokepoint buys BOTH at once: the honour set and the gate set are the same set by construction. Dispositions over the TOTAL list-kind set:
      - **LAND — honoured today, 10 kinds** (the `emit_delimited_list` sites, pinned by `EXPECTED_DELIMITED_LIST_SITES`): fn/method/trait/extern parameter list · call + method-call arguments · generic parameters `[T]` · generic arguments `[int]` · closure parameters · array/set literal · tuple literal (2+ elements) · dict literal · `Expr::StructLiteral` (fmt-unreachable, kept converted as class hygiene) · grouped import `import a.{X, Y}`.
      - **DEFER — 15 positions, the rows enumerated above**: enum tuple-variant fields · constructor-pattern fields · tuple pattern · dot-shorthand pattern fields · tuple TYPE · function TYPE params · tuple RETURN type · `auto a, b = t` destructure · `for x, y in …` pattern · tuple `return a, b` · `with` bindings · attribute args · trait-bound generic args + assoc-type bindings · the `format_item` meta family (ONE row covering FIVE sites: meta type-call args, meta type-fn params, `meta assert` message, `meta log` args ×2) · closure destructure bindings. Each is a hand-rolled `write(", ")` loop with no `Doc` layer.
      - **NEVER, with the reason**: the 1-element tuple `(x,)` — the comma IS the syntax that makes it a tuple, not a layout signal (Black exempts the same shape) · `from a import x, y` — `from a import X,` is a measured PARSE ERROR and the list has no delimiters to wrap in; it becomes a candidate only if the ratified parenthesized form lands · `assert` / `assert return` message — two fixed operands, not a list · `Expr::Index` `v[i]` — one "element", not a list · trait `extends A & B` — not comma-separated · struct fields / enum variants / trait items / equip items / extern items — newline-separated.
      - **⚠ METHOD-CHAIN SEGMENTS ARE **NOT** IN THE DEFER SET FOR THIS AXIS.** They are `Gate::UngatedCarveOut` for the COMMENT gate (pre-rendered into a comment-blind sub-formatter), but the comma probe is deliberately GATE-INDEPENDENT — it reads the source, which does travel into the sub-formatter — so a magic comma inside a chain segment's argument list IS honoured. Pinned by `tests/fixtures/fmt_magic_comma/chain_carve_out.gg`.
  - **(4) CROSS-REGION TRAILING-COMMENT CLAIM — measured against the comment-attachment rework and NOT closed by it; now has its own durable repro at `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/fmt_cross_region_trailing_claim.gg` + `#[ignore]`d `known_gap_fmt_cross_region_trailing_claim` asserting the INTENDED placement. The claiming rework (typed comment table · one claim/emit chokepoint · runs claimed with their heads) changes nothing here, because the mechanism is `emit_trailing_comment_after`'s same-source-line test being blind to DELIMITER BOUNDARIES: when the generic-args region and the call's own `)` end on one line, the generic-arg hook reaches the shared forward-only cursor first. Fix = a REGION-AWARE claim.** A trailing comment on a call whose last GENERIC argument shares its source line with the call's `)` is claimed by `emit_trailing_comment_after` for the generic-args region. Discovered-by, not introduced-by, the chokepoint: the same input is mishandled WORSE without it (both comments escape to statement level). Idempotent, so it re-parses — which is exactly why no fixture must lock the current output in (Core #8). Measured, post-chokepoint:
    ```
    auto b = wrap[               auto b = wrap[
        int,                         int,
        # SEN gb-interior     →      # SEN gb-interior
        String](6)  # SEN B          String,    # SEN B   ← was the CALL's trailing
                                 ](6)                        comment; now documents
                                                             the last TYPE ARGUMENT
    ```
    Belongs with the comment-attachment machinery (`emit_trailing_comment_after`'s same-source-line test does not know it is crossing a delimiter boundary), not with the gate.
  - **CORPUS EXPOSURE — measured, mechanism-agnostic.** An own-line comment at delimiter depth > 0, string-aware, over `tests/fixtures lib examples compiler` (2809 `.gg` files): ONE real instance outside this round's own fixtures — `compiler/data/resources.gg`, fill-class, now fixed. **ZERO comma-loop-class instances.** (The instrument also flags two lines in `tests/fixtures/string_algorithms.gg`; those are FALSE POSITIVES — nested quotes inside f-strings (`f"(()): {is_balanced("(())")}"`) defeat its depth tracker. Ground truth: that file is byte-identical before and after the chokepoint.) Re-run the census + its positive control before the bulk `gg fmt` sweep; the instrument is mechanism-agnostic on purpose, so it sees members no enumeration anticipated.

### Medium

- **🆕 CI/TOOLING FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-30, from the `test_result_advanced` CI-red fix — see DONE):**
  - **🔧 macOS dev machine CANNOT run the self-host runtime net / bootstrap locally without a `cc` shim — this is WHY the `Result.map` drop-corruption shipped (4 days red on CI, invisible locally).** The self-host's emitted C contains constructs that Linux/gcc accepts as WARNINGS but Apple clang (the dev toolchain) rejects as ERRORS, so `self_host_runtime` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` CC-FAIL on ~925/~36/8 fixtures locally for reasons UNRELATED to any given change → the dev never sees real runtime regressions; CI (Linux/gcc) is the only place these fixtures execute. **Workaround proven this round:** a PATH-shimmed `cc` wrapping `/usr/bin/cc -Wno-int-conversion -Wno-implicit-function-declaration -Wno-incompatible-pointer-types -Wno-return-mismatch -Wno-return-type` makes the net regress down to 8 genuinely-platform-specific fixtures (the `send`/`clone_inplace`/`RxNode` macOS-header/fwd-decl clashes + `meta_platform_guard` OS-detection). **Fix options (own decision):** (a) make the test harness append these `-Wno-*` flags to its `cc` invocations on macOS (`tests/integration.rs` `self_host_emit_cc_run` :18426 + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` :18633) so local validation is faithful; OR (b) document the shim in CLAUDE.md Build&Test as the macOS prerequisite for self-host gates. Without one of these, every self-host codegen change is un-validatable on the dev box and ships blind to CI.
  - **🐛 CLEAN UP the gcc-tolerated-but-clang-rejected self-host EMIT classes (latent UB, benign on the gcc/x86_64 production target but real codegen sloppiness).** Surfaced as the ~36 CC-FAIL classes when the net runs under strict clang: (i) **int↔pointer untyped-local stores** — the self-host emits `void* local = (int32_t)0` for some unresolved-type locals (dead stores in `test_result_advanced`, but the class is real: a local whose type the lowerer couldn't resolve defaults to `void*` + an int-0 init); (ii) **non-void functions that fall off the end without a `return`** (`-Wreturn-mismatch`: `Column__col_len`, `IoError__display`, `MapIter__…__next`, `Color__eq`, etc. — the self-host emits a non-void signature but no terminal return on some path); (iii) **conflicting forward-decl vs definition types** (`X__clone_inplace`, `RxNode`) and the **`send` libc-symbol collision** (the self-host emits a function named `send`, clashing with macOS `<sys/socket.h>`; rename-mangle runtime-colliding symbols). All round-trip harmlessly on 64-bit gcc but are principled bugs; (i) is also the reason the `map` crash was a SEGFAULT rather than a clean error on x86_64. Own scout per class; lower priority than parity work but they erode the "reference-grade emit" bar.

- **🏁 GG_IMPL ENDGAME TRACK (owner-chosen 2026-06-19, ACTIVE) — build-time compiler selector: `GG_IMPL=selfhost|rust` produces a SINGLE installed `gg` that IS that implementation.** Full design + sequencing + grounded current-state in `the gg_impl-endgame plan (git history)`. Sub-requirements (the self-host must own the WHOLE pipeline — today `driver.gg` is source→C, the harness does cc): (1) cc/link ownership in the self-host driver; (2) full CLI surface (`build`/`run`/`check`, `-o`, flags); (3) runtime + `lib/std` bundling (mirror the Rust gg resource-schema `include_str!`); (4) relocate self-host source out of `tests/fixtures/`; (5) the `GG_IMPL` build orchestrator (Makefile/`build.sh`/`gg bootstrap`). Bootstrap stays: Rust gg (or a stage0 blob) is the build-time SEED; `GG_IMPL` picks the INSTALLED binary. **Near-term value = parity ACCELERATOR** (a standalone `gg-selfhost` lets us dogfood REAL programs, surfacing gaps the fixture corpus misses). Runs PARALLEL to parity tracks (disjoint files). Foundational milestone = `GG_IMPL=selfhost` build → a `gg-selfhost build hello.gg -o hello && ./hello` that works standalone.
  - **STATUS (sub-reqs 1/2-partial/3-through-Inc-3 landed — see DONE.md). REMAINING:** **sub-req 2** (CLI surface) PARTIAL — `check`/`--help`/`--backend`/`--emit-*`/`--version`/`-V` + unknown-command diagnostic + the pipeline test landed (G1+G2 landed `1d6d33a8`, 2026-06-24, scout-validated 4-review); build/run/-o were already there. REMAINING (pure plumbing, low value): `lex`/`parse` as real commands need format-matched output (NOT plumbing — defer); broader error coverage gated on the typechecker-permissiveness gap (separate, filed). G3 (stricter unknown-flag/`-o`-no-value validation) is REFUTED — Rust gg is itself permissive there, so tightening would be a divergence. **sub-req 3** (runtime + `lib/std` bundling = the relocatable-single-binary ENDGAME BLOCKER) DONE through Inc-3 (a `gg-selfhost` for import-free + `lib/std` programs is ALREADY fully relocatable, no env); REMAINING: Inc-3b (`lib/xtd`, 876KB — most pull SQLite/SDL/GL externs) + Inc-4 (SQLite, conditional) — both later/optional. **sub-req 4 (relocate `driver.gg` out of `tests/fixtures/`) — DEFERRED, needs its OWN scout (scoping-scout 2026-06-24 measured HIGH/broad blast radius, NOT a safe single round, NOT parallel-safe):** 27+7 harness refs to `self_host_lowerer` paths, ~15 `tests/lints.rs` paths that would SILENTLY NEUTER (read-empty → 0 violations → false green, Core-#6 rot trap), 90 depth-sensitive `embed_file("../../../…")` paths in `driver.gg`, and 15 symlinks to `self_host_typechecker/` (its OWN comparison fixture — can't move with it). Lower-risk path: a shipped dir at the SAME repo-depth + path-indirection + re-pointed symlinks + a lints.rs silent-guard audit — pick the depth-preserving target dir up front. Do as its own scout→brief→reviews→executor when funded. **sub-req 5** (orchestrator) MOSTLY done (`scripts/gg_impl.sh` both modes); after Inc-2+3 it can drop the `GG_RUNTIME_DIR`/`GG_LIB_DIR` exports for a truly relocatable install — wire that up.   - **🐛 accelerator find #2 (harness exit-code coverage) — REFRAMED (the "EXIT-CODE-BLIND" claim was OVER-STATED; output-review `a5b48428` corrected it).** `self_host_runtime_diff` does NOT silently MATCH a wrong exit code: `self_host_emit_cc_run` (`tests/integration.rs:16449`) returns `Crashed{exit_code}` on any non-zero self-host exit, and the oracle path excludes oracle-non-zero fixtures — so a wrong non-zero exit surfaces as CRASH, not a false MATCH. The void-main bug was invisible only because NO corpus fixture used the tail-`print` `void main` form (all 1016 mains are block-body). A `GG_RUNTIME_DIFF_EXIT=1` exact-exit-vs-oracle guard would be near-vacuous (expected-zero); low-pri. The `print_tail_exit_*` regression fixtures (landed) are the real guard.
- **🔧 `--release` (-O2) silently IGNORED on 3 build sub-paths** (the main `build`/`run` paths WORK — see DONE.md). `release` isn't consumed at: (a) the `--shared` library build (`src/main.rs:838-887`, own cc invocation early-returning before the `:1103` `-O2` site → `gg build --shared --release` silently emits a `-O0` lib); (b) the freestanding/UEFI cc path (same early-return); (c) the `gg script.gg` shorthand (`src/main.rs:~2476` never threads `release` into `LoweringOptions`). Same gap-class — wire `release` into those 3 sites' opt-level (or scope the `appendix-cli.md` wording). Nothing breaks (default intact); completeness gap. Low-pri.

- **🐛 GG_IMPL self-host `exec` is shell-based (`system()` → `sh -c`) — word-splits on spaces/shell-metacharacters in paths** (filed by the run-mode output-review `afd371e2`, 2026-06-22; PRE-EXISTING, NOT introduced by the run-mode fix `d2e64d22`). The self-host driver's `exec` (`std.process`, runtime `gorget_exec` → `process_runtime.c:5-6` `system(cmd)`) is used for BOTH the run path (`driver.gg:704`, now `path_absolute`-resolved) AND the `cc_cmd` (`driver.gg:695-696`) — so a build/run path or `-o` target containing a space or shell metacharacter word-splits or injects. Rust gg avoids this entirely by exec'ing the built binary shell-free via `Command::new(&exe_path).status()` (`src/main.rs:2489-2497`) and building the cc argv as a vector. Reference-grade fix = give the self-host a shell-free exec (argv vector, no `sh -c`) for both sites. Lower-pri (no corpus path has spaces today); doesn't trip Core-#8 (pre-existing, unchanged). Own small scout→fix. (Cosmetic sibling: `tests/integration.rs:~16292` `self_host_cli_pipeline` step-6 comment says "NOT where the .gg lives" but the cwd IS where it lives — the bite is the bare `-o` name; fix the comment on contact.)

Biggest payoff, biggest investment — don't start until the smaller papercuts are done; multi-week. The semantic analyzer already builds a complete typed scope tree (`ScopeTable` + `function_info` + `TypeRegistry`); the work is the LSP-protocol layer + file-watching + the hover design pass.

- **[LOW — deferred tidy, discovered during D21 `gg sim` deletion 2026-07-24] `regex = "1"` in `Cargo.toml:26`'s `[dependencies]` is now used only by `tests/lints.rs` (a test target)** — move it to `[dev-dependencies]`. Self-contained, optional, out of scope for the D21 round itself.

- **[LOW — honest coverage note, from D21 `gg sim` deletion 2026-07-24] No exact live successor for sim's type-agnostic `UninitializedRead` slot-liveness check** (was `src/sim/dispatch.rs:815+`, now deleted). `--sanitize` wires ASan+UBSan (`-fsanitize=address,undefined`) but NOT MemorySanitizer, and UBSan only validates `bool`/`enum` load-validity, not a generic uninitialized read of an arbitrary-typed local. Plausibly unreachable from valid Gorget source (type-first decls mandate initializers, so an uninit read would require a lowering bug), and sim only ever checked its OWN GIR execution, never the real backend — so no user-facing guarantee was lost by the deletion. If a future lowering bug ever produces an uninit read, the successor is wiring MSan into `--sanitize` or a static check, NOT resurrecting the interpreter.

#### Toolchain exit-code scheme follow-ups (filed 2026-07-15 — production polish, surfaced by the exit-code research backing the ggdef elab∘eval verdict-triple; scheme RATIFIED Option A in decisions.md: 0 success / 1 static-rejection / 2 usage / 101 trap+ICE / 103 ggdef-only fuel)
- **[LOW — production `gg`, consensus polish] Usage/CLI errors collapse into exit 1 instead of 2.** Per the ratified scheme, genuine usage errors (bad flags, missing file, misinvocation) should exit **2** (GNU/argparse consensus), distinct from a *source* static-rejection (exit 1). Production `gg` currently exits 1 at ~50 `process::exit(1)` sites in `src/main.rs`, conflating "you invoked me wrong" with "your source is wrong." Audit those sites, split genuine usage errors to 2, leave parse/semantic/build failures at 1. Not conformance-compared (ggdef's own usage=2 already correct); reference-grade legibility polish. Pairs with the ggdef `EXIT_USAGE=2` that already holds.
- **[LOW — production `gg`, Core-#8 flavor] Internal runtime panics exit 1, colliding with the compile-error code.** `gorget_panic_at` (`src/backend/c/runtime/panic_normal.c:5`) handles *internal* runtime failures (OOM, closed-channel send, file-open error) and exits **1** — numerically colliding with the static-rejection/compile-error code, so a shell script can't distinguish "your program failed to compile" from "your program ran and hit an internal panic." Per the ratified taxonomy these are *runtime* failures and should either be traps (exit 101, via `gorget_trap_at`) or get their own distinct code. Decide which (are these D11 catchable traps or a separate ICE-adjacent class?), then reroute + a both-backend test. Distinct from the user-facing `gorget_trap_at`=101 path which is already correct.

- **[LOW] 🐞 DWARF line-table debug info (gdb/lldb stepping).** `Backend.debug_info: bool` exists but is hardwired `false` (`src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:~3438`). The C backend could get source-line stepping nearly free via `#line` directives mapping emitted C back to `.gg` source; LLVM needs `!dbg` metadata. Distinct from (and cheaper than) the existing Low entry on `caller_location()`/multi-frame backtraces. Unblocks "can I actually debug a Gorget program" — table stakes before 1.0, after LSP.




- **🆕🐛 [MED — R40, `gg fmt` CLI arg handling] Two pre-existing `gg fmt` CLI arg-parsing bugs (surfaced by Track G doc verification).** (1) **Short flag BEFORE the file fails:** `gg fmt -i <file>` / `-c <file>` errors ("Error reading -i") — the filename parser (`src/main.rs:2773-2796`) only skips `--`-prefixed args, so a leading `-i`/`-c` is taken as the filename; the file-first form (`gg fmt <file> -i`) works. (2) **Extra file args SILENTLY IGNORED (Core #10 silent-drop):** `gg fmt --check f1.gg f2.gg` returns exit 0 ignoring `f2`, so `gg fmt --check src/*.gg` checks only the FIRST globbed file — materially misleading for CI. Reference-grade fix: (1) skip any `-`-prefixed arg in the filename parse; (2) either process ALL file args or REJECT extras with a clear error (never silently drop — Core #10). Pre-existing, not R40-introduced. Docs (appendix E) use the verified file-first form + a per-file CI loop as the workaround.

## Docs / devbook + misc language features

### Medium
- **🆕✨ [MED — RATIFIED 2026-08-11 (owner, live session; ledger `docs/define-gorget/decisions.md`), not built] The PARENTHESIZED IMPORT FORM: `from x import (a, b, c)`.** Parens suspend newline-significance inside the name list exactly as in expression context (Python-identical; PEP 8's preferred wrap). WHY: the `from x import a, b, c` name list is the language's ONE undelimited list, so in indentation-based syntax it cannot wrap — a bare name on a fresh line re-parses as a new statement (the rationale comment at `src/formatter/mod.rs`'s `format_import` says exactly this) — while every peer language ships a bracketed form. Consequences, in order: **(1) ACCEPT-SURFACE CHANGE ⇒ Core #9 cross-lane** — the Rust parser plus the three self-host parser copies, with POS/NEG conformance fixtures encoding the intended final state and a ggdef subset note (in-subset or filed as a subset gap). Backslash continuation stays REJECTED; the split-into-repeated-`from` statements workaround stops being needed. **(2) THEN `gg fmt` fill-packs import name lists at 120** through the existing `surround_fill` machinery — the grouped `import a.{X, Y}` path already does this, so the from-import path is the same call with the new delimiters, and the `format_import` no-wrap rationale comment RETIRES with it. **(3) THEN delete the width exemption** documented in `docs/book/appendix-formatting.md` §"Line width and wrapping" and in `docs/devbook/05-formatter.md`'s third-budget-escape clause, both of which currently state the exemption and point at the ratified form as its retirement. Its own small track, NOT R41's. ⚠ NOT ratified alongside it: fmt-merging adjacent same-module imports (the isort precedent) — a separate knob if ever wanted.
- **🆕✨ [MED — designed, never built, never filed; found 2026-08-05] Meta "diamond inference" (`Vector[int] items = Vector()`)** — an empty type-argument list on the RHS infers from the declared type. Designed in the meta RFC as item M9; zero implementation and, until now, zero ledger presence (`diamond` has no hits in `src/`, `docs/devbook/`, `docs/language-reference.md` or `docs/book/`). Recorded so the design survives the retirement of `docs/internals/meta.md`.
- **🆕✨ [MED — the ITERATOR-SURFACE RESIDUE, consolidated 2026-08-05 from two design notes slated for deletion (`stdlib-design.md` §10 Phase 2c/2d + `method-level-inference.md` §"Sequencing After This Lands"). Filed self-contained so those files can go; two live code comments previously delegated to them.** Method-level generic inference SHIPPED (`try_infer_method_targs`, `src/semantic/typecheck.rs:7545`); what it was meant to unlock did not. Remaining: **(a)** delete the builtin Vector `HofExpand` variants now that user-space `iter().method().collect()` wrappers subsume them — they stay as fallbacks today, and `vector_userspace_hofs` in `tests/integration.rs` is the fixture pinning both paths coexisting; **(b)** retire the `_iter` free functions (`count_iter`/`fold_iter`/`any_iter`/`all_iter`/`find_iter`, live at `lib/std/iter.gg:768-769`) in favour of the `.method()` form; **(c)** `join` is still a free function in `std.iter` because `Displayable.display()` dispatch through an abstract `T` is not threaded through default-method emission (pinned by `tests/fixtures/stdlib_iter_more_terminals.gg`); **(d)** broaden the `MethodGenericInferenceFailed` trigger (`src/semantic/errors.rs:487`) — it fires only when EVERY fallback dispatch also fails, so a call whose generic never bound can type silently; **(e)** `chain[Other]`/`zip[Other]` on `Iterator[T]` need an iterator-generic `Other` threaded through the adapter struct's field (stuck on `VectorIter`, alongside `lazy_windows`/`lazy_chunks`); **(f)** per-method trait-bound declarations (`min`/`max`/`sum` needing `T: Comparable`) — bound syntax plus emission-skip logic; **(g)** two ADAPTER LAWS are stated but not testable because their operations do not exist — an involution law for `rev()` (needs a double-ended iterator concept; `rev`/`DoubleEndedIterator` have zero hits in `lib/std/iter.gg`) and a sortedness law pairing `sort()` with an `is_sorted()` predicate (also absent). The four laws that DO hold over the shipped surface are folded into `docs/devbook/23-stdlib.md` §"The adapter contract"; these two land when the operations do. **(h)** ⚠ **Core #9 lane gap:** the self-host typechecker has NO method-level generic tracking at all (`EMethodCall` `targs` destructured-and-unused, hardcoded arms per HOF name). The prior note claimed this was "tracked in TODO Phase 2c (h)"; no such entry existed — this is its first filing.

#### 🆕 BOOK-FOLLOW AUDIT #1 FINDINGS (filed 2026-07-18; the first newcomer-simulation audit, ch1-3 — full report was /tmp/bookaudit_report.md, re-runnable; ch1 10/10, ch2 6/10, ch3 7/10)
- **[HIGH — language semantics, ledger-grade] Narrow-type overflow is unenforced: `uint8 255 + 1` → 256 (no panic), `255 +% 1` → 256 (no wrap).** The book says "integer overflow always panics"; only 64-bit `int` does. Narrow types appear to be int64 in disguise — neither trapping nor wrapping at their declared width. Needs a D-decision (what IS uint8 arithmetic?) + conformance fixtures once ruled; check D26 (`+!`/ArithError) for the intended model first.
- **[MED — emit] f-string mixing `int` + `float` leaks C `%lld`-vs-`int64_t` format warnings to the user's terminal** (aarch64: int64_t is `long`, emitted spec is `%lld`). Fix the emitted format (PRId64-style), not a `-w` blanket — hits the book's own first multi-type example.
- **[LOW — UX] `gg build` leaves the emitted `.c` in the cwd** — route to a temp/build dir unless `--emit-c` is asked.
- **[LOW — book, one-liners batched for the next docs touch] ch2 `float64 precise = 3.14` example doesn't compile (type table says `float`); ch3 enum example omits the value construction (`Direction direction = North`); ch3 match section uses enum/Option/Result with no forward reference to ch6/ch9; ch3 while-else/`loop:` snippets call undefined helpers so they can't run as printed.**
